# IG-harvested principles vs Solen law, full verdicts (2026-07-16)

Owner ask (dictated): analyze every principle from the two IG research files, exists-check vs law AND code, ADD / DONT verdicts with reasoning, before/after mockups for the ADD list. Sources: IG_DESIGNPARSER_PRINCIPLES.md + IG_DESIGNMOTIONHQ_PRINCIPLES.md (ig-handoff worktree). Pipeline: 47-agent workflow (2 extractors, 44 evaluator groups, 1 synthesizer), every verdict carries law + code evidence. Rerunnable for the next batch (workflow script ig-principles-eval).

**217/217 principles evaluated.** Verdicts: ADD 12, OWNER_CALL 0, CONFLICTS_WITH_LOCK 35, DONT_ADD 27, ALREADY_EXISTS 143.

## ADD (12)

### dp-crop-bone-shaft-not-joint , Crop through the bone shaft, never through a joint
- Reasoning: Solen's photo pipeline has no crop UI anywhere: salons drag-and-drop portfolio photos straight into fixed-aspect grids (aspect-square / aspect-[4/3]) and a plain centered CSS object-cover does the cropping automatically, with zero joint-awareness and zero framing guidance at upload time. Portfolio/gallery photos are exactly the case this principle covers: hairstyling close-ups, hand and nail work, massage/spa treatment shots routinely include wrists, elbows, knees and necks near the frame edge, and an arbitrary center-crop on an unpredictable UGC composition can genuinely cut a limb at the joint and read as amputation, which actively hurts trust on a beauty/wellness booking marketplace where the photo IS the offer (PSYCHOLOGY.md law 7). This is not covered anywhere: PHOTO_STRATEGY.md only specs the 6 static category-hero interiors and explicitly keeps people out of them ('no readable faces'), so the one place Solen DOES control photo composition never triggers this problem, while the one place it DOES matter (salon-uploaded work photos) has zero guidance. Fix is cheap and non-structural: one line of upload-time framing copy plus biasing object-position toward center-top on the public gallery grid, both treatment-only on real existing surfaces.
- Law evidence: none found: _design-system/PHOTO_STRATEGY.md is the only photo-composition spec and covers only the 6 static category-hero interiors (no people, no crop/joint guidance); no crop/limb/joint guidance anywhere in RATIONALE.md, LOCKFILE.md, or TASTE_LOG.md
- Code evidence: components-legacy/dashboard/GalleryManager.tsx:181-193 (salon photo uploader: aspect-square + object-cover, drag-to-reorder only, zero crop control or framing copy); app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonImageGallery.tsx:159,173 (public gallery grid: aspect-[4/3] and aspect-square + object-cover on salon-uploaded portfolio photos, plain center crop with no object-position tuning)
- Mockup brief: Real surface: components-legacy/dashboard/GalleryManager.tsx (salon photo uploader) + app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonImageGallery.tsx (public gallery grid). Before: drop zone has no framing guidance, uploaded photos get a blind centered object-cover crop into aspect-square/aspect-[4/3] tiles. After: add one short line under the drop zone ('Keep hands, elbows and knees inside the frame, not cut at the edge') and change object-position from default center to center-top on the aspect-square team-photo grid, so a portrait-orientation UGC photo is less likely to force-crop through a wrist or elbow at the bottom edge. Treatment-only, no layout/structure change.

### dp-irradiation-illusion , Irradiation illusion: bright shapes read as bigger and thicker
- Reasoning: This is a real, well-evidenced (T1, Kremkow et al. 2014, PNAS) perceptual mechanism that Solen's own docs are already primed to accept: domain 5 of RATIONALE.md already applies comparable optical corrections (glyph nudges, overshoot percentages) as a matter of course, and the icon-stroke-width rule is explicitly flagged as an open, unreconciled inconsistency (RATIONALE.md retrofit item 8, line 271). Nothing in LOCKFILE, TASTE_LOG, or REJECTED_TREATMENTS addresses white-on-dark weight parity, so this is a genuine, narrow gap, not a duplicate. It matters here because Solen deliberately runs the SAME salon-name text in two polarities on the same route: ink-on-white in the results grid and white-on-black-photo in the featured carousel directly above it, and currently the white version also jumps from weight 600 to 700, compounding with irradiation to read heavier than the design intends.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 6 (line 144, 'Optical vs mathematical alignment... circles sized next to squares get a ~2-4 percent overshoot') establishes the house already corrects for perceived-vs-measured size; RATIONALE.md section 13 retrofit item 8 (line 271, 'Icon stroke widths 1.9 vs 2... internally inconsistent, reconcile') is an open item this principle can resolve; RATIONALE.md:714 notes the positive-polarity/halation mechanic already binds the mobile dark canon, establishing precedent for polarity-aware type/stroke treatment, just not yet on this web surface.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/search/CategoryHeroCarousel.tsx:144 renders the salon name at 'font-bold' (700) white-on-photo, while the equivalent name treatment elsewhere on the same page (SalonCard, results grid) runs at LOCKFILE's locked 600 ink-on-white; _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1218,1279 (stroke 1.9 default, 2.8-3 only for a filled-disc check) shows stroke width is already varied by context, just not yet by polarity.
- Mockup brief: Real surface: CategoryHeroCarousel card (app/[locale]/_components/search/CategoryHeroCarousel.tsx) on any /coiffeur, /barbershop, /nails etc. category route. Before: salon name h3 at font-bold(700) white-on-photo directly above the same name at 600 ink-on-white in the results grid below. After: drop the white h3 to 600 (matching the ink role weight) and show side-by-side that it now reads as equal perceived weight to its ink counterpart instead of visibly heavier, closing retrofit item 8's open stroke/weight inconsistency.

### dp-line-length-measure , Line Length (Measure): 50-75 Characters Per Line
- Reasoning: Not a new pitch, the owner already approved this exact number a day before this review (68ch, almost identical to the harvested claim's 66-CPL sweet spot). The gap is pure execution: no shared ch-based measure utility exists anywhere in the codebase. The one place it matters most, SalonAbout's venue description, renders at max-w-3xl (768px) at 14-15px body text, well past 100 characters per line on desktop, exactly the return-sweep failure the law already flagged as absent. This bites hardest for Solen specifically because DE/FR/IT copy runs 20-35 percent longer than EN and German compounds already break unpredictably, so an uncapped line makes both problems worse in the one section where a salon's own voice is supposed to build trust.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:128 (45-75 CPL, ~66 ideal, CONV/Bringhurst) + _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:189 (owner-approved 2026-07-15, verbatim probe pick: "4B: cap long body/description text at 68ch, line-height stays 1.5")
- Code evidence: Repo-wide grep for ch-based max-width found zero systemic usage; only unrelated hit is app/[locale]/_components/homepage/SolenStory.tsx:63 max-w-[42ch] on a 2-line marketing subhead (not the long-form target). The actual long-form target, app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonAbout.tsx:33, renders the "Uber uns" description at max-w-3xl (768px) with no ch cap.
- Mockup brief: SalonAbout.tsx "Uber uns" section (real PDP surface), treatment-only: swap max-w-3xl for max-w-[68ch] on the description div (line 33), same font-size/leading/color. Before = paragraph spanning 100+ characters per line at 1280px viewport; after = capped ~68ch with ragged right margin. If approved, promote to a shared .prose-measure utility so reviews and any future rich-text block reuse one definition instead of hand-rolling ch values.

### dp-grid-matches-content , A grid is structure, not decoration
- Reasoning: The house has already adopted the Swiss mathematical-grid lineage as a stated VALUE and has already been burned once by exactly this mismatch (a modular equal-weight card grid applied to dashboard content that was actually hierarchical, one hero plus minor facts), but its own 2026-07-16 audit explicitly names the general decision procedure as missing: the system 'can grade a mockup twelve ways... but holds few priors that produce a RIGHT first mockup.' Formalizing manuscript/column/modular/hierarchical as a one-line classification step before laying out a new section closes a self-identified gap rather than inventing a new axis, and prevents the D1 failure from recurring on the next section nobody has redesigned yet.
- Law evidence: RATIONALE.md:818 (Gap register G2, open, judge-flagged 2026-07-16: 'Layout-pattern selector: list vs grid vs carousel vs table vs bento, given content shape... Probe: same content 3 ways'); RATIONALE.md:788 (Swiss/International Typographic Style adopted as a VALUE including 'mathematical grid,' Muller-Brockmann Grid Systems 1981, never operationalized into a decision step); TASTE_LOG.md:294,298-302 (Dashboard D1 already hit this exact failure once, fixed ad hoc, never generalized)
- Code evidence: none checked in: no grid-type decision step found in LOCKFILE section 10 dual-axis pipeline or the fable-frontend skill; homepage mixes carousel rows, category tile grids, and list-style search results (app/[locale]/_components/homepage/, app/[locale]/_components/search/) with no single named framework connecting content shape to grid type
- Mockup brief: Demo on the operator dashboard home summary panel, the exact site of the D1 clutter complaint: before = an implicit equal-weight modular card grid guessed ad hoc; after = an explicit hierarchical-grid call (one large hero zone plus minor bare-text facts) named using the manuscript/column/modular/hierarchical vocabulary as a one-line design note before layout, closing RATIONALE.md Gap G2.

### dp-asymmetric-balance , Asymmetric Balance: Area + Value + Negative Space
- Reasoning: Solen's own rationale layer already names the vocabulary (balance symmetric/asymmetric/radial, visual weight, negative space as shape) but explicitly flags the computable version as an open, mechanizable gap it has not built: 'Visual-weight/balance centroid metric (answers unbalanced with a number: per-element bbox area x darkness x saturation, centroid offset)'. Checking the actual diagnostic skill confirms the gap is real in practice, not just on paper: solen-taste-diagnosis's measured walk has a squint-test/hierarchy-count step, a grouping decision tree, and contrast math, but no dedicated balance step. First-principles fit for THIS product specifically: Solen's B&W pivot deliberately zeroed out hue as a hierarchy carrier (color-as-sole-carrier is itself a named violation in RATIONALE.md), which means in Solen's actual palette, visual weight really is close to pure area x darkness x negative-space, the exact formula this principle states, making it unusually well-matched rather than a generic import. The owner has already hit this exact complaint by name and without a repeatable tool: TASTE_LOG.md records 'what about the balance... the gaps and space between the bento boxes' as a live owner objection, resolved ad hoc via the 16/32 gap rule rather than a reusable check. The genuinely new, cheap contribution from this principle is the collapse test (mentally remove the darkest element, then the biggest shape, then the largest open-space block, one at a time, and check whether the composition still reads as intentional) as an operational stand-in for the not-yet-built centroid math, addable as one more step in the existing skill rather than a new system.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:784 (balance vocabulary named as CONV, computable form deferred to gap register), :830 (gap register D1: 'Visual-weight/balance centroid metric... Mechanizable: YES', unbuilt); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:296 (owner's own 'what about the balance... gaps and space between the bento boxes' complaint, resolved only via the 16/32 rhythm rule, not a repeatable balance check)
- Code evidence: /Users/sulo/.claude/skills/solen-taste-diagnosis/SKILL.md:18-37 (measured walk has squint/hierarchy-count, grouping decision tree, and contrast-math steps; no balance/negative-space step exists between them)
- Mockup brief: Not a visual treatment, a diagnostic-procedure add: insert a 'balance check (collapse test)' step into solen-taste-diagnosis's measured walk, demoed by running it against a real surface with an on-record balance complaint, app/[locale]/_components/homepage/BentoBusiness.tsx's asymmetric 4-card grid on /business (Booking col-span-2 + Stars col-span-1 in row 1, Calendar col-span-1 + Chart col-span-2 in row 2) ,  show the collapse test's before/after verdict on that live grid rather than building any new UI.

### dp-stem-matched-icon-stroke , Stem-matched icon stroke weight
- Reasoning: LOCKFILE currently locks TWO different fixed default stroke widths for the same monochrome icon system (1.9 for inline glyphs vs 2/1.75 in the size-pairing table), and RATIONALE.md itself flags this exact pair as CONV ('asserted, not derived') and as an unretrofitted internal inconsistency still on the open queue. That is a genuine, law-acknowledged gap, not a settled frozen row the owner would need to re-open from scratch. The harvested principle supplies the missing derivation Solen never had: measure the real typeface's stem (Inter/Inter Tight H or N) at the actual locked type-role sizes and set stroke-width from that, instead of picking arbitrarily between two library defaults. This should ship as a small precomputed size-to-stroke table (same pattern already used for the tracking scale, RATIONALE.md item 5), not a runtime canvas measurement, so it stays inside the existing closed-token discipline and directly resolves the flagged reconciliation instead of adding new unresolved surface area.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1219 ('Stroke width: Lucide default 1.9 for inline glyphs, 2.8-3 only on a check inside a filled disc') vs LOCKFILE.md:1282 ('Lucide only, strokeWidth 2' in the icon-size pairing table); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:557-563 ('Icon stroke width inconsistency ... asserted, not derived, house convention (CONV) ... RATIONALE.md's own retrofit queue already names this exact pair as internally inconsistent and unretrofitted').
- Code evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md:580 hardcodes strokeWidth={2.2} for the hamburger/X as a third ad-hoc value; grepped app/components/lib for stemWidth/measureStem/canvas-based stroke derivation, zero hits, confirming no size-aware mechanism exists today.
- Mockup brief: Real surfaces: the FilterSheet chip icon next to its 12-13px label (today's flat 1.9), the ProgressStepper's 18px step-icon (today's 2), and the header hamburger/X at 24px (today's ad-hoc 2.2). Before = today's three inconsistent flat values side by side; after = a small calibrated table (e.g. ~2.2 at 12px, ~2.0 at 14-16px, ~1.7 at 20-24px, derived from Inter/Inter Tight's own measured H-stem at each locked type-role size) applied to the same three live components so the owner can eyeball whether the stems now read as matched weight.

### dp-progress-motion-is-status-signal , Progress bar motion is the status signal, not the fill level
- Reasoning: No law anywhere addresses what a determinate percentage bar should do when its underlying signal stalls; MOTION.md only covers unknown-duration loading (skeleton shimmer) and button spinners, not a width-fill bar with a live position value. The one real, live determinate bar in the codebase (ImportProgressBar, used for the TikTok discovery bulk-import in the admin dashboard) has no independent activity signal: it is pure width-transition, so if `current` stalls between API pages (a realistic scenario for a paginated external fetch), the bar visually freezes exactly the way the principle predicts, reading as done or stuck. Note the booking flow specifically is off-limits: REMOVED.md graveyards 'ANY progress UI in the booking flow' outright, so this fix must stay scoped to the one live non-booking bar. Given it is CSS-only and admin-facing (low blast radius), it is a cheap, genuine gap-fill, not a new design decision that risks colliding with anything locked.
- Law evidence: _design-system/MOTION.md:96 (skeleton shimmer for unknown-duration, spinners only inside buttons, no rule for a determinate bar's motion-while-stalled); _design-system/components/ProgressStepper.md:18 and _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1246 govern step trackers, not fill-percentage bars, and explicitly scope to 'ANY progress UI in the booking flow' being graveyarded per _design-system/REMOVED.md:15 ('booking stepper progress-bar step-indicator ... ANY progress UI in the booking flow ... mockup-20 lock').
- Code evidence: components-legacy/discovery/ImportProgressBar.tsx:20-25 (`className="h-full bg-s-ink rounded-pill transition-[width] duration-200"`, `style={{ width: `${percent}%` }}`, no independent motion layer), used live at app/[locale]/dashboard/discovery-admin/page.tsx:191.

### dp-logo-format-earned-recognition , Logo format follows earned recognition
- Reasoning: The header/footer half of this principle (wordmark-only while recognition is low) is already correctly implemented and locked: Solen deliberately dropped its dot/symbol accent to ship wordmark-only, which is the right call for a brand this early. But the principle's other half, that space-constrained placements need a compact symbol regardless of recognition stage, exposes a real, currently-unaddressed gap. Favicon, PWA/home-screen icon, and app-icon surfaces cannot render the word 'Solen' legibly at 16-48px, so they still carry an icon-only mark, and that mark has been left to drift: I found three mutually inconsistent brand eras live simultaneously (a maroon-on-cream PWA icon set, a separate teal favicon circle, neither matching the current locked ink/white wordmark). This isn't a taste debate, it's a shipped inconsistency the principle explains and would concretely resolve by defining one small locked symbol for icon-only contexts while keeping the wordmark everywhere else.
- Law evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Logo.tsx:8-21,90-100 (V2-D27/V3-D146 history: dot accent fully removed, noDot forced true, 'Solen' wordmark only per owner 'drop the dot entirely, just Solen', B&W pivot); no LOCKFILE/TASTE_LOG entry addresses a symbol mark for icon-constrained placements (grepped _design-system/*.md and _plans/*.md for symbol mark|app icon|brand mark|monogram, no hits besides an unrelated salon-avatar 'monogram fallback' in MapSalonDetail)
- Code evidence: public/favicon.svg (teal #043338 circle, V2-D27 era, superseded by the current ink/white wordmark); public/icons/icon-192.png and icon-512.png (PIL pixel sample: dominant color #9B1D30 maroon on #F8F4ED cream, an even older pre-teal era); public/manifest.json:9-10 (theme_color #E8624A, background_color #FAF6EF, both retired coral/cream values per SOURCE.md:240's B&W pivot)
- Mockup brief: Real surface = browser favicon tab + PWA/home-screen icon set + social share preview (og:image). Before: three inconsistent legacy marks live today (maroon monogram PWA icons, teal circle favicon, cream/coral manifest colors), none related to the current ink/white wordmark. After: one small locked ink-on-white symbol (simple geometric 'S' mark, LOCKFILE tokens only, no color revival) used ONLY where the full wordmark can't physically fit; the 'Solen' wordmark stays canonical everywhere else (header, footer, marketing) since recognition doesn't yet support symbol-alone use anywhere but the icon slot.

### dm-two-months-range-picker , Show two adjacent months by default in a range picker
- Reasoning: Booking and search are deliberately single-date (no range use case exists there, per DateTimePicker.md's own scope note), so this principle has no target on those surfaces. But the dashboard's live Vacation Mode feature is a genuine from/to range that regularly crosses a month boundary (a week off in late July routinely runs into August), and today it renders as two disconnected native <input type="date"> boxes with zero visual range preview, no calendar at all. That is also a live violation of the 'ONE DateTimePicker primitive, no bespoke date UI' law, since the primitive's own doc already flags a range variant as 'not yet built; add here when needed, don't fork' ,  this is that needed moment, and two-adjacent-months is the correct default once it is built, because a salon owner picking a week-plus closure needs to see both ends without paging away.
- Law evidence: _design-system/components/DateTimePicker.md:22 ('Range picking (vacation blocks), not yet built; add a range variant here when needed, don't fork.') read against _design-system/LOCKFILE.md 'date / time' contract row (V3-D445: ONE DateTimePicker primitive, no bespoke date UI); no REJECTED_TREATMENTS.json or TASTE_LOG entry rejects a calendar-based range UI
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/dashboard/settings/page.tsx:668-731 (VacationTab: two bare <input type="date"> fields for vacation_start/vacation_end, no calendar, currently the only live cross-month range-picking surface in the product)
- Mockup brief: Extend app/[locale]/_components/primitives/DateTimePicker.tsx with a `range` variant and use it in dashboard settings' VacationTab (app/[locale]/dashboard/settings/page.tsx:718-731), replacing the two disconnected native date inputs. Default to two adjacent months (e.g. 'Juli 2026' / 'August 2026') with the selected span shaded between start and end and a result pill '28. Jul -> 5. Aug' below, so an owner picking a vacation that crosses a month boundary sees both ends at once instead of paging the native picker back and forth.

### dm-preserve-caret-on-autoformat , Preserve caret position during auto-formatting
- Reasoning: This is a genuine, verified gap, not a guess. Both live phone-number fields that auto-format as you type do a naive controlled reassignment (setState(formatSwissPhoneInput(e.target.value))) with zero caret-position math: no selectionStart capture, no post-render setSelectionRange restore. Because formatSwissPhoneInput fully rebuilds the string from scratch (strips all non-digits, re-inserts spaces), editing anywhere but the very end (the common repair case: 'I mistyped the third digit') will snap the caret to the end after each keystroke, forcing the user to delete-and-retype from the end instead of fixing in place. Both call sites sit in money-path UI (guest checkout phone, PayConfirmStep contact phone) where a frustrating input costs a completed booking. Fixing it is a small, surgical change (compute digits-before-caret pre-format, remap to the equivalent position post-format, call inputRef.setSelectionRange after the state update) that doesn't touch layout, copy, or visual treatment, so it doesn't need a visual mockup approval, just the behavior fix at the two call sites.
- Law evidence: none found in REMOVED.md, REJECTED_TREATMENTS.json, or TASTE_LOG.md for phone masking or caret handling, so no conflict with a prior owner decision
- Code evidence: components-legacy/booking/GuestBookingForm.tsx:179-182 (onChange={(ev) => setPhone(formatSwissPhoneInput(ev.target.value))}, no selection handling); components-legacy/booking/PayConfirmStep.tsx:481 (onChange={(e) => setContactPhone(formatSwissPhoneInput(e.target.value))}, no selection handling); lib/format-phone.ts:6-24 (the reformatting function itself, which rebuilds the whole string from digits every call)
- Mockup brief: Not a visual mockup, a behavior fix: on the real checkout guest-phone field (components-legacy/booking/PayConfirmStep.tsx:477-485) and the guest-booking phone field (components-legacy/booking/GuestBookingForm.tsx:173-187), demo before/after by placing the cursor mid-number (e.g. after '079 12|3 45 67') and typing a digit: before = caret jumps to the end after the reformat; after = caret stays right after the digit just typed.

### dm-cursor-pagination-over-offset , Cursor pagination beats offset pagination for mutating lists
- Reasoning: Every paginated list in Solen (salons, reviews, bookings, cut/nail history, discovery feed) uses OFFSET/LIMIT via Supabase .range() or a p_offset RPC param; there is zero cursor pagination anywhere in the codebase. Most of those surfaces are low risk: a user clicks 'Load more' once or twice inside a short session on data that barely mutates meanwhile, so offset there is fine and swapping it would be needless engineering. The Inspo feed is different. It is explicitly the product's habit/retention surface, rendered as Pinterest-style infinite scroll via IntersectionObserver, and it reads from discovery_feed, a table actively appended to by scheduled ingestion (a 30-minute cron backfill plus ongoing TikTok harvest), ordered by di.created_at desc before LIMIT/OFFSET. That is the exact insert-shifts-the-offset failure: a newly published item pushes every later page down by one row, so the next auto-loaded page repeats the last card the user already scrolled past. For a feed meant to be scrolled passively and often, a visibly duplicating card is a small but real trust-eroding glitch, not a cosmetic one.
- Law evidence: none - no LOCKFILE/RATIONALE/TASTE_LOG coverage of pagination mechanics; this is a backend correctness gap, not a locked design token
- Code evidence: app/api/discovery/feed/route.ts:42 (offset=(page-1)*limit, passed as p_offset); supabase/migrations/20260625_discovery_feed_tags_any.sql:49-57 (order by ... di.created_at desc limit ... offset ...); app/[locale]/inspo/page.tsx:248-260 (IntersectionObserver auto-advances page); same offset-everywhere pattern also at app/api/salons/route.ts:59-61,445, app/api/reviews/salon/[salon_id]/route.ts:16-46, app/api/bookings/user/route.ts:31-88, app/api/clients/[id]/cut-history/route.ts and nail-history/route.ts
- Mockup brief: Demo on the live /inspo feed: publish a new discovery_item mid-scroll on today's build and show the next auto-loaded page repeating the previous page's last card (before); switch discovery_feed to a keyset cursor over (gender-rank, sort_order, created_at, id) and show the identical insert produce no repeat or skip (after).

### dm-length-beats-symbols , Length beats symbols: password strength is entropy
- Reasoning: Solen's live password rule is exactly the anti-pattern this principle targets: client-side it demands an uppercase letter AND a digit on top of an 8-char floor, with zero entropy measurement anywhere in the codebase (no zxcvbn or similar dependency in package.json). That rule actively REJECTS 'wrong-mango-battery-sky' (23 chars, no symbol, far higher real entropy) while happily accepting 'Passwort1', one of the first patterns any cracking dictionary checks. Server-side the rule is even looser (min 8, no composition check at all) so client and server already disagree, meaning the composition gate is pure UI friction with no backing security rationale, not a deliberate hardened policy. For a consumer booking marketplace, signup/reset friction is pure conversion cost bought for no real security, and the fix is narrow: swap the 3-checkbox composition list for a length-weighted strength bar computed by a real algorithm. This is a genuine, unexamined gap, not a locked call to relitigate, since no LOCKFILE/TASTE_LOG/RATIONALE row defends the current composition rule.
- Law evidence: none , no LOCKFILE/TASTE_LOG/RATIONALE/PSYCHOLOGY row locks password composition rules; only PSYCHOLOGY.md:39-40 and RATIONALE's no-fabricated-data law bind the IMPLEMENTATION (any strength/crack-time number shown must be live-computed, never a decorative fake figure, same as the existing no-fabricated-data rule)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/auth/register/page.tsx:89-100 (client toast rejects on password.length<8, missing digit, missing uppercase); app/[locale]/auth/reset-password/page.tsx:53,171-173 (inline 3-item Requirement checklist: 8 chars / uppercase / digit gates passwordValid); lib/validations.ts:662-671 (server signupSchema only enforces z.string().min(8).max(200), no composition check at all , confirms the composition rule is UI-only friction); no zxcvbn/entropy package in package.json
- Mockup brief: On app/[locale]/auth/register/page.tsx and app/[locale]/auth/reset-password/page.tsx: replace the 3-checkbox composition list (8 chars / uppercase / digit) under the password field with one slim strength bar, reusing the exact h-1.5 bg-s-ink progress-bar treatment already shipping for upload progress (components-legacy/ui/ImageUpload.tsx:265-273), filled by a real computed score (e.g. zxcvbn) plus one line of exact-cause copy underneath (per LOCKFILE's error-copy rule) instead of checkmarks. Before: 3 checkmarks gate submit and reject long symbol-free passphrases. After: one bar + one line, gated on real entropy, still floors at a sane minimum length (8, matching the server schema) so it never contradicts the server rule again.

## CONFLICTS_WITH_LOCK (35)

### dp-scan-path-f-z , Scan-Path Placement: F for Reading, Z for Action
- Reasoning: The system already researched this exact question (dated 2026-07-15) and reached the opposite conclusion the principle assumes. F-shaped scanning is documented as a FAILURE MODE of unformatted content, not a target to design toward: the fix is the layer-cake pattern (distinct, descriptive headings that get fixated while body is skipped), which the whole hierarchy system (squint test, ~3 type-size ceiling, max-2-top-tier-elements) already builds toward. Z-pattern is formally entered in the design folklore table as a MYTH with 'never cite' status, no primary eyetracking source exists, and NN/g's own zigzag research is critical of unpredictable diagonal scanning. Designing 'for' an F shape would mean deliberately under-formatting content to produce it; designing 'for' a Z shape would mean forcing four-corner placement regardless of the actual task. Neither serves a booking marketplace where a user lands on a salon page or the booking wizard with one concrete task (find a slot, confirm) that the system already answers with task-optimized hierarchy, not a scan-path overlay.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:73 ('the F-pattern is a FAILURE MODE of unformatted content... Z-pattern: unverified folk convention, added to the folklore table'); RATIONALE.md:242 (folklore table row: 'Z-pattern scanning as an eyetracking fact | Unverified folk convention... | Cite F-pattern (failure mode) and layer-cake (target)'); research/TASTE_HIERARCHY.md findings 2-3 and the 'What I could NOT verify and dropped' section (Z-pattern explicitly dropped as unsourced).
- Code evidence: none checked in: the conflict is at the law/research level, not a specific component; the booking wizard's step-by-step headers (services -> Staff -> Zeit -> Bezahlen, per project_booking_flow_canonical memory) are the live example of the layer-cake alternative the law prescribes instead of F/Z placement.

### dp-blur-opacity-elevation-ladder , Elevation as Blur x Opacity: The Five-Level Shadow Ladder
- Reasoning: Solen's chrome is ~80% flat white/stone with photos confined to a few framed slots, so the house rule (V3-D420, researched against 6 real Uber Eats screens) is 'elevation is earned by the background, not the button' ,  mapping a shadow level to a component TYPE (L1 = every Button/Chip) is exactly the photo-heavy-app logic that was measured and rejected for Solen's flatter chrome, because it reintroduces the 'grey haze' a dedicated sweep spent a whole pass removing from TabPill, filter pills, carousel arrows, and the mobile hamburger. Panel/drawer/modal separation already exists in this codebase, but via a Z-INDEX stacking scale (sheet 400/410, modal 500/510, toast 600, tooltip 700), not a shadow-blur ladder, and the shadow ladder itself is deliberately capped at 3 named levels with 'no formula to extend from' stated explicitly. Shipping a 5-level blur x opacity ladder that puts shadow back on every button/chip at rest would directly violate the hard 'zero box-shadow on a resting control' rule and undo a specific, named, already-completed cleanup.
- Law evidence: _design-system/CONTROL_ELEVATION.md HARD RULES #3 ('Shadow lives in exactly two places: the one ink primary, and glass-over-photo. Zero box-shadow on any control resting on white or s-bg-sunken') and its Q1-Q3 decision tree; _design-system/RATIONALE.md:391-396 (elevation capped at 3 levels, extension has 'no formula'); LOCKFILE.md:494-497 (z-index scale, not a shadow ladder, differentiates sheet/modal/toast/tooltip)
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:261-294 defines only elevation-1/2/3 + whisper + pressed + float (3 named levels, not 5); CONTROL_ELEVATION.md's AUDIT+SWEEP LOG table lists TabPill, Header hamburger, CardFilterRow filter pill, FeaturedSalonCarousel arrows, CityTopBar city picker, SalonWalkInPanel (i) button as components where resting shadow-on-a-control was explicitly removed as '❌ degraded' drift

### dp-two-layer-shadows , Two-layer shadows: directional primary + contact ambient
- Reasoning: This exact treatment was tried on Solen's own elevation-2/3 tokens and explicitly reverted by owner+council on 2026-06-29 because the two-layer stack 'read as a double line' ,  a dated rejection discoverable only by reading code, since RATIONALE.md's prose table is stale and still describes the old two-layer values (surfacing this contradiction per the doc-vs-code precedence rule). On Solen's white/stone chrome a doubled shadow edge is legible in a way it would not be on a photo-heavy reference surface, which is presumably why it read badly here. Where two-layer stacks do remain (elevation-1, whisper, legacy aliases) they don't even implement the principle's specific physical claim that the ambient layer shrinks and fades with height while the primary grows: in Solen's tokens both layers grow together as elevation increases, which RATIONALE.md itself calls a single-light-source CONVENTION, not the two-distinct-phenomena model this principle describes. Reintroducing full primary+ambient two-layer shadows on the visible elevation tiers would resurrect a look the owner already looked at and asked to simplify.
- Law evidence: RATIONALE.md:391-396 and :712 document the OLD two-layer elevation table and frame it as a 'key+ambient' model, but this is stale relative to the locked token values (rule-18 contradiction: code postdates and overrides this prose)
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:279-283 ,  elevation-2 and elevation-3 are single-value box-shadows with a dated comment: 'V3 (2026-06-29, owner + council): single-layer (the old two-layer read as a "double line"). "A" (0 1px 4px) came back too light; council pick = a touch heavier, still single-layer, visible.' Two-layer stacks survive only in elevation-1, whisper, and legacy aliases (card, card-hover, warm-md/lg/xl), not in the current primary elevation-2/3 tokens most surfaces reference.

### dp-subitizing , Subitizing: the ~4-item pre-attentive glance limit
- Reasoning: Subitizing itself is a real, replicated finding, but its scope is enumeration/counting of undifferentiated items, not recognition of distinct, habitually-positioned, labeled navigation destinations. Solen's law already rejects this exact category error for the sibling number: Cowan's ~4-chunk limit is explicitly scoped to recall, and RATIONALE.md states 'No Solen rule should ever justify "7 items max" from Miller... recognition is not span-limited.' The same logic applies to a 4-item subitizing cap on a tab bar. Concretely, the shipped iOS app's bottom tab bar is already locked at 5 items (Home/Suche/Inspo/Karte/Profil), with the code comment itself citing 'D1: 5 tabs' as a deliberate, Apple-native decision, and the dashboard icon rail runs to 9. Adopting a hard 4-item glance cap would force reopening a locked, functioning, already-shipped navigation decision to fix a miscounting problem that does not occur once icons are stable, familiar, and each maps to one destination the user already knows the position of.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:71 ("Memory span: cite Cowan (~4 chunks, 2001), never Miller's 7+/-2... Recognition (scanning a visible list) is not span-limited at all; the limit binds recall. No Solen rule should ever justify '7 items max' from Miller."); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:236 (folklore table, same reasoning for the sibling number); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1176 (dashboard icon rail lists 9 items: Übersicht/Kalender/Katalog/Kund:innen/Marketing/Verkäufe/Team/Berichte/Einstellungen)
- Code evidence: /Users/sulo/Documents/solen-mobile/src/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:6-33 (5 NativeTabs.Trigger elements: index/suche/entdecken/karte/profil; code comment "D1: 5 tabs, D5: Apple feel" cites the count as a locked decision, not an oversight)

### dp-aspect-ratio-isolation-context , Aspect Ratio as a Perceptual Tool: Isolation vs Context
- Reasoning: The isolation half of this claim already lives implicitly in Solen's locked aspect-ratio set: card photos were moved from 3:2 to a tighter 5:4 specifically to make the salon read as the isolated subject (the 2026-07-13 card redesign). But the contextualizing half, using a wider frame so 'attention spreads' into the environment, maps directly onto the full-bleed hero direction the owner explicitly killed on sight one day before this evaluation ('i told you full bleed is not okay i want it gone the mockup is denied bro'), and formalizing 'aspect ratio is a deliberate isolation-vs-context dial' would also mean writing new imagery/art-direction rationale, a domain the owner separately declined to open. Both halves of the practical implication are either already covered by frozen literals or freshly and explicitly banned, so this needs a new owner yes by name, not an agent-side add.
- Law evidence: _design-system/REJECTED_TREATMENTS.json entry "fullbleed-direction" (added 2026-07-16: owner denied the whole full-bleed/takeover/flood/montage direction, supersedes the same-day approval, no re-add without a new owner yes by name); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:822 (G6, imagery/art-direction domain OWNER-DECLINED 2026-07-15); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1164 (canonical aspect-ratio set frozen under drift rule A11: 1:1, 3:2, 16:9, 21:9-heroes-only, 4:3-PDP-only)
- Code evidence: _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:200 (card redesign C1: "photo 5/4, supersedes the 3/2 landscape of 2026-07-02", the isolating-ratio move already made); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1164 (21:9 gated specifically to "full-bleed wide heroes", the exact treatment the fullbleed-direction rejection bans)

### dp-visual-angle-arcminutes , Visual-Angle Sizing (Arcminutes), Not Fixed Physical Size
- Reasoning: Solen's entire type system is a table of frozen literal px values per role, council-stamped LOCKED (V3-D443) with an explicit 'do not re-open any row without the owner saying so by name' clause. Re-deriving those sizes from a viewing-distance formula would mean unlocking and rewriting that table, which is exactly the re-opening the contract forbids. It is also the wrong tool for a responsive web app: the existing clamp(min,vw,max) pattern already lets size respond to viewport width, which is the practical, CSS-native proxy for device-class viewing distance (phone close, desktop far) without needing literal arcminute math or a stated assumed distance per spec, which nobody could enforce or verify at runtime anyway.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md 'Design contract - LOCKED (V3-D443)' table, text size row, and _design-system/LOCKFILE.md type scale table (Hero H1 through Star rating small, all fixed px/weight/LH/tracking per role) which RATIONALE.md section 5 (line 126) explicitly frames as 'hand-tuned, not ratio-derived... do not retrofit a fake ratio onto it, record it as a hand-tuned constraint system' , the same section that would have to reject an arcminute-derived scale for the identical reason.
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:221-261 type scale table uses fixed px pairs per role (mobile/desktop), e.g. 'Body 14px/15px', 'Section H2 18px/20px'; app/[locale]/_components/salon/*.tsx (grep across 14+ files) all consume these as literal text-[clamp(...)] values keyed to viewport width, not viewing distance.

### dp-modular-type-scale , Modular (multiplicative) type scale over linear scale
- Reasoning: This is not a gap, it is a question Solen's own design-rationale layer already asked and answered by name: the type scale is deliberately hand-tuned, not ratio-derived, and the doc explicitly instructs future agents not to retrofit a modular ratio onto it. For a Fresha-structure/Uber-restraint hybrid product, a strict multiplicative ladder would also fight the locked per-role table, which mixes independently-tuned weight, line-height, and tracking per size (not just a size number), something a pure ratio scale does not model. Re-deriving the frozen LOCKFILE literals to fit x1.25 steps would also break the council-stamped V3-D443 lock.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 5, line 126: 'Modular scale (CONV, Bringhurst / Tim Brown)... Solen's ladder... was tuned by hand, not ratio-derived, and that is FINE... Do not retrofit a fake ratio onto it; record it as a hand-tuned constraint system.' This is a dated, explicit, direct rejection of exactly this principle, reinforced by CLAUDE.md's LOCKED design contract clause forbidding re-opening any type-scale row without the owner naming it.
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:221-261 scale table: sizes step 11/12/13/14/15/16/18/20/22/24/26/30/34/40/64px, not a fixed-ratio ladder (e.g. 12 to 24 is not the same multiplier as 14 to 15); confirmed live in app/[locale]/_components/salon/*.tsx clamp() values matching these exact non-ratio numbers.

### dp-typeface-class-emotional-signal , Typeface Class Is a Pre-Conscious Emotional Signal
- Reasoning: The descriptive claim (type class carries a pre-conscious emotional register) is plausible, but its actionable suggestion, one limited expressive/decorative face for warm or boutique content, directly reopens a decision the owner has closed five separate times: Geist ('no Geist anywhere'), Hanken Grotesk, Anton (SignatureLockup), Peace Sans, Plus Jakarta Sans, and Bricolage Grotesque were all tried and explicitly killed, landing on a locked, dated rule of exactly one family in two optical cuts with 'no third face.' Solen's answer is stricter than the principle allows (zero decorative faces, not one), so applying the principle as written would violate a frozen literal, not fill a gap.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:213 ('Two optical cuts of one family, no third face') + _design-system/REMOVED.md:19-20 (Geist and the Anton SignatureLockup killed 2026-06-11) + LOCKFILE.md:211 (NEVER list: Hanken Grotesk, Peace Sans, Plus Jakarta Sans, Bricolage Grotesque, JetBrains Mono all retired)
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:226-229 declares exactly two font-family keys (display/heading, body), both Inter variants; grep for any script/decorative font-family across app/**/*.css and components returns none

### dp-imperfection-authenticity-signal , Imperfection as an authenticity signal (handwriting variance)
- Reasoning: Solen's whole typographic law is the opposite bet: one machine-precise family in two optical cuts, zero decorative or expressive faces, because the B&W pivot's trust signal for a payment/booking flow is Swiss calm and consistency, not human warmth or hand-touch. A handwriting-variance treatment is a third typeface by definition, which the font lock explicitly forbids, and the one prior attempt at an expressive/personality font (Anton, in SignatureLockup) was killed outright during the pivot. Even setting the font lock aside, this product's authenticity signal is already handled by a different, evidence-based mechanism (real photos, real review counts, no fabricated data per taste rule 1 and PSYCHOLOGY.md's real-numbers-only law), so introducing simulated imperfection to signal 'human-made' would be solving an already-solved problem with a tool this system has already rejected.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:212-213 (NEVER list: 'Peace Sans, Plus Jakarta Sans, Bricolage Grotesque... system-default-only'; ACTIVE: 'Two optical cuts of one family, no third face'); _design-system/REMOVED.md:20 (Anton signature-lockup font killed 2026-06-11 pre-pivot); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:607-620 (Personality zone table: personality/illustration is confined to 404/empty/success/onboarding and explicitly banned in booking/checkout/search/PDP, the surfaces where a trust signal would matter most)
- Code evidence: app/layout.tsx:13-14 loads only Inter_Tight and Inter via next/font/google, no third or script/handwriting family anywhere in the font stack; grep for handwriting/cursive/script fonts across _design-system docs and app/components returned nothing

### dp-dark-mode-irradiation-compensation , Dark-mode irradiation compensation
- Reasoning: Web dark mode itself is graveyarded: owner declined it verbatim ('no dark mode') in Taste Lab round 1 on 2026-07-15, one day before this review, so there is no dark surface on the booking marketplace for this compensation to apply to. Even setting that aside, the B&W pivot's whole point is a single calm 80/17 light canon; building a parallel dark type ramp (size step up, weight tier up, +tracking) for a surface that was just explicitly rejected would be reopening a locked, dated, owner-verbatim decision without the owner asking by name, which the precedence chain forbids. Mobile (solen-mobile) does ship a real Revolut-charcoal dark theme, but its own canon addresses the underlying halation risk a different way (elevation by lightness, charcoal never pure black, RATIONALE.md:714) rather than a text-metrics bump, and that file is out of scope for this web-repo review.
- Law evidence: _design-system/REMOVED.md:72 'dark mode web dunkelmodus dark theme darkmode | Web dark mode... | owner declined 2026-07-15 in Taste Lab round 1, verbatim no dark mode'; _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:190 'Web dark mode | DECLINED ("no dark mode"). Graveyarded; do not re-propose without an owner yes'; _design-system/RATIONALE.md:658 and :714 confirm the decline and note web dark mode 'stays so', with the mobile dark canon treated as pointer-only, not binding here.
- Code evidence: app/globals.css has zero @media(prefers-color-scheme:dark), no data-theme selectors, and no dark CSS variables anywhere (grep for prefers-color-scheme / data-theme / dark: in app/globals.css and tailwind.config.js returned nothing) , confirming the web app has no dark-mode infrastructure at all to apply this technique to.

### dp-separate-fab-from-tabs , Separate the Action Trigger (FAB) From Location Tabs
- Reasoning: Solen is a booking marketplace, not a content-creation app: there is no create/add verb (new post, new listing) that would ever need a floating trigger pulled out of a tab bar. The one place a real tab bar exists (the iOS app) ships 5 pure destination tabs with zero inline action icon among them, so the principle's own failure case (an orange plus buried in 7 nav icons) is not a problem Solen has. On web/dashboard/map, the owner has killed any bottom-nav-shaped bar twice by name and stated the actual operating philosophy directly: actions live contextually, not behind a floating global trigger. Building a FAB-over-tabs treatment would resurrect exactly the 'second nav' shape the owner rejected on sight both times.
- Law evidence: REMOVED.md:48 (map bottom nav + swipe pager + Book button rejected, owner 2026-07-02: 'Solen has NO bottom nav (fabricated)'); REMOVED.md:78 (dashboard bottom-dock/action-dock rejected, owner 2026-07-15: 'what does this bottom navigation do? now we have two navigation... just clutter'; actions live contextually, sidebar is the only nav); TASTE_LOG.md:285 ('Second nav | NEVER a bottom action dock or any second nav-shaped bar')
- Code evidence: solen-mobile/src/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx (5 destination-only NativeTabs: Home/Suche/Inspo/Karte/Profil, no create/add trigger); grep for FloatingActionButton/FAB across app/ and components-legacy/ returned zero hits

### dp-root-2-rectangle , The Root-2 Rectangle (Self-Similar Aspect Ratio)
- Reasoning: LOCKFILE section 11 closes the canonical aspect-ratio set to 1:1, 3:2, 16:9, 21:9, and 4:3 (PDP cover photos only), with its own drift rule flagging anything outside that list as drift. √2:1 (1.4142) is not in it, so adding it to any photo/card treatment directly conflicts with a frozen literal. Beyond the lock, first-principles: the root-2 rectangle's entire value proposition is that it halves cleanly for PHYSICAL paper stock (ISO 216 / A-series), a print-production constraint. Solen has no print or PDF-export surface (no jspdf/pdfkit/invoice-PDF code exists anywhere in the repo) where that halving property would ever be exercised on a digital booking marketplace. Carving a new, unlocked aspect ratio into photo or card imagery for a mathematical curiosity with zero functional payoff here is exactly the kind of unrequested structural change the design contract exists to block.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1164-1166 (Sec 11 canonical aspect-ratio closed set: aspect-square, aspect-[3/2], aspect-video, aspect-[21/9], aspect-[4/3]; 'Anything else = drift A11')
- Code evidence: none checked in: grepped app/ and lib/ for jspdf/pdfkit/generatePDF/invoice-pdf/receipt-pdf, zero hits, confirming no print/paper-format surface exists in the product; existing aspect-ratio usage (app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonHero.tsx:208-229, SalonCard photo treatments) draws only from the closed LOCKFILE set

### dp-base-unit-radius-scale , Base-unit radius scale
- Reasoning: Solen's radius family has to do two structurally different jobs at once: containment shapes (card 16, card-lg 20, panel 16, sheet 28) and full-round affordance shapes (search 99, btn 99, pill 9999) that signal 'tappable' regardless of the control's height. A single base-derived scale (say 4/8/16/24/32) has no clean path to a 44px button reading as fully round, so pills would end up as a hardcoded exception anyway, at which point the 'one derived scale' promise is already broken. The team looked at this exact tradeoff and picked per-role literals instead of a formula, on purpose, and logged why.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:352-356 (DECISION/MECHANIC block): 'Radius tokens locked per role... the specific step values (12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 9999) are a hand-picked set, CONV, not derived from a ratio or formula.' LOCKFILE.md:414-426 locks the same values as a per-role table, not a base-unit progression.
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:246-260 shows role-keyed literal tokens (card 16, card-lg 20, panel 16, search 99, pill 9999, btn 99, input 16, sheet 28) with no shared base or common ratio between them (e.g. 20 is not a clean multiple of 16, 28 is not a clean multiple of either).

### dp-proportional-corner-radius , Proportional Corner Radius
- Reasoning: Proportional radius would mean every new component invents a radius by measuring its own rendered height, which is the opposite of Solen's 'ground in a locked component, never invent' rule (CLAUDE.md taste rule 9). It also breaks the pill family Solen actually relies on: a 44px CTA and a 32px filter chip both need to read as the SAME rounded-affordance language (fully round, height-independent), not two different curvatures computed from two different heights. The live tokens already sit outside the claimed 15-25%-of-height band on purpose (a 44px button at rounded-btn is 99px, effectively 100% round) because full-round IS the tappability signal here, not an artifact of size.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:352-356, same hand-picked-not-formula finding. LOCKFILE.md:414-426 fixes radius per role, independent of instance size (one 'card' token covers both a compact and a large salon card; 'card-lg' is a separate role, not a scaled-up card).
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:246-260 (role-keyed literal tokens: card 16, card-lg 20, panel 16, search 99, pill 9999, btn 99, input 16, sheet 28); app/[locale]/profile/settings/SettingsForm.tsx:268 (`h-11 rounded-btn` = 44px-tall control at 99px radius, i.e. fully round, far outside a 15-25%-of-height ratio by design).

### dp-60-30-10 , The 60/30/10 Color Ratio
- Reasoning: Solen already evaluated and explicitly rejected this exact framework in favor of its own tighter, dated ratio. 60/30/10 gives 30% to a 'secondary structural color' ,  but Solen's law is 80% neutral surfaces, ~17% ink, and blue held SPARSE (small clickable bits only: text links, small buttons/chips, tappable metadata like review counts, never big CTAs, never structure). Applying 60/30/10 literally would triple the room given to secondary color and directly break CLAUDE.md's pinned taste rule 3 ('blue is SPARSE... NOT on big CTAs, NOT on see-all arrows, NOT on secondary buttons'). For a calm, restrained booking marketplace where the whole point of the B&W pivot is that blue stays rare enough to keep meaning clickable, importing a folk ratio that gives color 40% of the screen is a regression, not an improvement.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:89 'The 80/17 budget (CONV, internal). Same claim-shape as the 60-30-10 folk rule, but it is OUR dated budget decision (V3-D138/V3-D197), stated as a house constraint, not as science.' RATIONALE.md:238 table row explicitly: '60-30-10 color proportion | Interior-design folk rule, no empirical basis | Our 80/17 is an internal CONV budget, labeled as such.' Also CLAUDE.md pinned rule 3: '80/17 surfaces+ink; blue is SPARSE.'
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:224 s-accent DEFAULT #276EF1 restricted by LOCKFILE:16 to hyperlink-reading text only (review counts, Mehr lesen, Ändern links) ,  not a 30%-of-screen structural color anywhere in app/ or components/

### dp-hsl-tonal-scale-from-one-hue , Derive one HSL tonal scale from a single hue
- Reasoning: Solen's law names this exact mechanism and rejects it by name: HSL lightness is called out as 'a math artifact of RGB' that produces uneven perceived brightness across a ramp, and OKLCH is mandated as the generator space instead, with HSL explicitly demoted to legacy documentation only. The system already has the systematic-derivation pattern the principle is chasing, just executed correctly (OKLCH fixed L+C, hue-stepped) rather than in HSL. Swapping in an HSL-based generator would be a step backward on a booking marketplace where chart series and any future multi-hue extension needs perceptually even steps for scannability, not an HSL ramp that drifts in perceived brightness.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:79 'OKLab/OKLCH (Ottosson 2020) gives near-uniform L, orthogonal L/C/H, predictable contrast across hues' vs 'HSL lightness is a math artifact of RGB: hues at equal HSL-L differ wildly in perceived brightness.' RATIONALE.md:81 'HSL stays as legacy documentation, not as the generator.' _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:98-100 (DS-A3): 'derive hues in OKLCH from s-accent, fixed lightness+chroma, hue stepped +25-30 per series' is the named pattern to generalize.
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js s-ink history (V2-D70 -> V3-D73 -> V3-D87 -> V3-D138, lines ~108-130) shows the neutral ramp was hand-tuned against measured WCAG contrast per substrate at each step, not interpolated from one locked hue via HSL L/S sweep; the only systematic hue-derivation in the codebase is the OKLCH chart-series stepping at LOCKFILE.md:98-100 and the amber 'HSL S+L held, hue rotated' comment at tailwind.config.js:175, neither of which is the single-hue-L/S-sweep method the principle proposes

### dp-shadow-color-inherits-surface-hue , Shadow Color Inherits the Surface's Hue and Saturation
- Reasoning: The principle asks shadows to be a lightness-only drop of the surface's own hue. Solen already tried the opposite question and locked the opposite answer: when the surface hairlines and backgrounds moved from a warm palette to cool neutrals (white / #F4F4F5 / #E4E4E7, which are near-hueless), the shadow tokens were deliberately kept warm-tinted (rgba(50,47,44,...), a warm brown-black) as a 'beauty-domain softening' independent of whatever hue the surface underneath happens to be. The lock explicitly anticipates and forbids the exact move this principle proposes: 'Don't fix shadows to cool grey.' Adopting the principle here would mean re-deriving every shadow color per-surface, undoing a named, dated, owner-reasoned decision for a product where the surfaces are intentionally near-neutral anyway (an OKLCH hue-inherit rule has almost nothing to bite on against #FFFFFF/#F4F4F5).
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md line 488 ('Warm tint is intentional ... shadow tokens stay warm-tinted rgba(50,47,44,...) even though surfaces/hairlines went COOL ... Don't "fix" shadows to cool grey.')
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js boxShadow block (elevation-1/2/3, whisper, card, surface tokens) all fixed at rgba(50,47,44,*) regardless of which surface token (white #FFFFFF vs sunken #F4F4F5) the shadow is cast under; FROST_GLASS (lib/frost-glass.ts, cited LOCKFILE.md:488) uses a separate pure-black shadow for over-photo controls, also hue-independent of the photo beneath it

### dp-color-emotion-association , Color-emotion association drives UI color choice
- Reasoning: This exact claim shape is named and rejected in the product's own design-rationale law, not merely unaddressed. RATIONALE.md classifies 'color X causes emotion Y' universals as a culture-bound convention (CONV tier, not T1/T2 evidence) and lists it verbatim in the 'never cite' folklore table alongside golden-ratio and 60-30-10 as claims that must not be used to justify a color choice. Solen's actual, already-implemented color law is deliberately narrower and more defensible for a booking marketplace: hues are assigned by universal SEMANTIC convention that users have learned from a lifetime of UI exposure (star=yellow, error=red, success=green), not by an appeal to pre-verbal emotional priming that varies across the DE/FR/IT/EN Swiss market this product serves. Building a new 'pick blue for trust, yellow for optimism' framework on top of that would reintroduce the exact justification style the rationale layer was written to keep out.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 3 ('Color psychology and harmony schemes: CONV at best ... "white = purity" class claims are conventions of our market, not laws') and section 12 folklore table row ('"Color X causes emotion Y" universals | Culture-bound conventions | Semantic conventions of our market, labeled CONV')
- Code evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md 'Universal colors, we don't invent semantic hues' table (tailwind.config.js s-success/s-error/s-warning/s-star tokens) grounds every hue in recognized UI convention, not an emotion-priming rationale

### dp-color-temperature-depth-cue , Color temperature as a depth cue: 60/30/10 resolves warm/cool conflict
- Reasoning: The principle's specific packaging, presenting a 60/30/10 threshold as the mechanism that 'resolves depth, not style,' is the exact folk claim this codebase has already researched and explicitly banned from citation. It is not merely unproven here, it is on a named never-cite list with the debunking reason given in the same row. Separately, the underlying warm-advances/cool-recedes premise doesn't even have a foothold on this product: Solen's chrome is COOL-only by lock (hairlines, sunken surfaces, borders all reversed from warm to cool), and any warmth in the UI is deliberately confined to photography, never distributed across chrome in a warm/cool ratio. There is no warm/cool split in the surface system for a 60/30/10 rule to arbitrate.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:231-238 (Design folklore table, 'never cite'): '60-30-10 color proportion | Interior-design folk rule, no empirical basis | Our 80/17 is an internal CONV budget, labeled as such'; also RATIONALE.md:317-322 and LOCKFILE.md:40,44 (cool-neutral lock, warm/cream explicitly rejected twice, chrome is cool-only, warmth reserved for photography per LOCKFILE.md:126-132)
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js s-border #E4E4E7 and s-bg.sunken #F4F4F5 are both cool-toned (not warm), confirming no warm/cool chrome split exists in code to apply a 60/30/10 ratio to

### dp-dark-mode-hct-hue-lock , Dark-mode color needs hue-lock, chroma-drop, tone-lift
- Reasoning: For the web product this evaluation covers, the premise is moot: web dark mode was explicitly declined by the owner ('no dark mode', Taste Lab round 1) and graveyarded, so there is no dark-mode variant to derive colors for. RATIONALE.md is unusually explicit that this exact color-science domain (positive-polarity/dark-surface color adjustment) is recorded as a pointer to the separate mobile canon only, and does not bind web. Proposing an HCT hue-lock/chroma-drop/tone-lift system for Solen web would mean rebuilding the dark-mode variant the owner already rejected. Note for completeness: mobile (a separate governed system, solen-mobile/_design-system/THEMING.md) already ships full light/dark token pairs, but a quick check shows it does not follow the specific 'chroma cut 20-30%' half of this claim, saturation for error/accent tokens actually rises in dark rather than dropping, only hue-lock and tone-lift are empirically true there, and the doc has a dangling 'see section 3d' pointer with no such section, so even on mobile this would be a documentation-gap fix, not a web-product addition, and out of scope for this evaluation's law set.
- Law evidence: _design-system/REMOVED.md:72 ('dark mode web... owner declined 2026-07-15... verbatim no dark mode'); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:190 ('Web dark mode | DECLINED... Graveyarded; do not re-propose without an owner yes'); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:658 ('web dark mode DECLINED and graveyarded') and :714 ('BOUNDARY: on web this only confirms the default (web dark mode is graveyarded)... It BINDS the mobile dark canon... Pointer only; the mobile file is the canon')
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:3 area has no dark theme (single light palette; per RATIONALE.md:236 'removed 2026-05-02 per Q62'); cross-checked solen-mobile/src/lib/theme.ts values via THEMING.md:49-75 table: success #16A34A→#2BD17E, error #DC2626→#FF5A5A, accent #276EF1→#4187FF measured in HSL show hue held within a few degrees and lightness raised in all cases (tone-lift correct) but saturation rising, not dropping 20-30%, for error/accent/save, so even mobile's shipped values don't fully match this principle's chroma-drop claim

### dp-chevron-navigation-only , Chevron as a Navigation-Only Affordance
- Reasoning: Solen ships a locked component whose entire job is a chevron used exactly the way this principle forbids: FAQItem is a native <details>/<summary> accordion where a ChevronDown rotates 180 degrees to signal expand/collapse in place, not "tapping this pushes to a new screen," and it is registered as locked (V3-D218/V3-D220). The same disclosure use of ChevronDown recurs across dozens of live surfaces: Select.tsx's dropdown trigger, SearchTemplate.tsx's filter pills (open/close a panel, not navigate), Header.tsx's menu triggers, and multiple dashboard collapsible sections (services, cases, segments, admin-sandbox), all rotating a chevron to reflect toggle state. This is not an oversight; it is the standard, well-established UX split Solen already uses on purpose: ChevronRight = drill-in/navigate (SeeAllButton, LOCKFILE:1209 "navigation / disclosure affordance"), ChevronDown = expand/disclosure toggle-in-place, disambiguated by direction the same way Material Design's own "expand more" icon works. The genuine risk the harvested principle is reaching for (a chevron that misleads about what it does) is already covered, more precisely, by the dated dead-chevron rule: a chevron/arrow may never render without a real wired destination or action. Enforcing a literal chevron-means-navigate-only rule would require ripping out FAQItem, Select, filter pills, and dashboard accordions in favor of toggle switches that make no sense for disclosure interactions (an accordion is not a binary preference).
- Law evidence: _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:146 ("FAQItem... Chevron rotates 180 degrees on open... locked V3-D220"); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1209 (chevron-right reserved for "Navigation / disclosure affordance", i.e. both classes share the glyph by direction, not a single meaning); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:238-268 (2026-07-16 dead-chevron rule: "A chevron/arrow affordance may NEVER render without a real wired destination", the actual owner-set guardrail against misleading chevrons)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/business/FAQItem.tsx:36-41 (ChevronDown rotates group-open, pure disclosure toggle, not navigation); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Select.tsx:104 (ChevronDown on a dropdown trigger); app/[locale]/_components/search/SearchTemplate.tsx:1405,1473,1902 (ChevronDown on filter pills, active-state driven by p.active, opens a panel in place); app/[locale]/dashboard/services/page.tsx:295 and dashboard/cases/page.tsx:315 and dashboard/segments/page.tsx:124 (ChevronDown/Up rotating on isExpanded/collapsed state for accordion sections)

### dp-links-need-non-color-signal , Links need a non-color signal and destination-naming text
- Reasoning: The principle bundles two claims that split apart under Solen's actual law. The destination-naming half already exists and is stronger than the example given (SOURCE.md explicitly bans "click here"/generic CTAs). The underline-as-shape-signal half directly collides with a dated, owner-council-ruled lock: v3.1 (2026-06-11) states "NO UNDERLINES on ink links... Blue inline links also sit bare at rest (no resting underline). Hover may darken/underline." This was reached after two earlier reversals (the v2 generous-blue model was rejected specifically because blue-with-decoration everywhere read as noisy), so re-proposing mandatory resting underlines is reopening a settled, twice-litigated fight, not filling a gap. Solen's law already satisfies the underlying WCAG 1.4.1 concern through a different, deliberately chosen non-color channel (weight, position, chevron affordance per RATIONALE.md:684) rather than underline, and the color risk itself is lower than generic because Solen's palette pairs blue against ink/grey, not the red-green axis where ~99% of CVD sits. There is one real, narrow gap worth flagging for an owner call rather than a blanket ADD: a few inline body links (e.g. onboarding/salon/page.tsx ToS/Privacy links) render as `text-s-accent hover:underline` with no weight differentiator at rest, meaning on a touch device (no hover) they are distinguished from surrounding prose by color alone, which is exactly the anti-pattern SOURCE.md:1046 itself bans ("Color as the ONLY signal of state"). That's a possible targeted fix on 2-3 specific links, not grounds to add underlines system-wide against a locked v3.1 decision.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:153-156 (v3.1 owner lock: "NO UNDERLINES on ink links... Blue inline links also sit bare at rest"), LOCKFILE.md:166,187 (blue inline links "bare at rest, hover darkens to deep"), SOURCE.md:1143 ("Click here or generic CTAs" already banned), SOURCE.md:1046 ("Color as the ONLY signal of state" already an anti-pattern), RATIONALE.md:684 (affordance = chevron/underline-on-hover/weight/icon, not color alone)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/onboarding/salon/page.tsx:167,169 (`text-s-accent hover:underline`, no font-weight, no resting underline, inline in a sentence) vs. app/[locale]/_components/search/SearchTemplate.tsx:2196 and FlowBar.tsx:27,36 (same blue-link pattern but with font-medium/font-semibold as an additional at-rest signal)

### dp-radius-brand-personality-dial , Radius as a Brand-Personality Dial, Scaled Proportionally
- Reasoning: The brand-tone half of this claim is already settled and locked for Solen (rounded reads warm/approachable, cited to the same Bar and Neta research, expressed as a fixed 12-28px + full-pill token band rather than the 0% sharp-authority end of the dial). The novel, actionable half, expressing every radius as a percentage of the element's own shortest side instead of a fixed pixel number, directly reopens a frozen design-contract row: Solen's radius system is deliberately fixed-pixel and role-keyed (card 16, card-lg 20, sheet 28, input 12) with an absolute nested-radius formula (inner = outer minus gap, not a ratio). Switching to proportional radius would change how every card, sheet, and input renders at every viewport and is exactly the kind of single-value the LOCKFILE says not to reopen without the owner naming it.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md Design contract table row 'radius | card/block 16 (rounded-card), button/chip pill, input 16, sheet 28, image flush(0)' under 'Frozen single-values. Do NOT re-open any row without the owner saying so by name'; _design-system/LOCKFILE.md section 3 radius token table (12-28px fixed values) plus the nested-radius formula (DS-4, 'inner radius = outer radius minus gap', absolute not proportional); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:149,266,352 already cites Bar and Neta 2006/2007 for the rounded-reads-friendly warrant behind the existing fixed band
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:246 borderRadius block defines the fixed px scale consumed by rounded-card/rounded-2xl/etc classes across components; no proportional (%-of-shortest-side) radius utility exists in the codebase

### dp-every-interactive-state-needs-treatment , Every Interactive State Needs Its Own Visual Treatment
- Reasoning: The hover/active/disabled thirds of this claim are already comprehensively built and locked: LOCKFILE's 'State matrix (ENFORCED ,  every interactive primitive)' table defines rest/hover/pressed/selected/focus/disabled for Card, Primary button, Photo chip, List row and Input, and real code backs it (hover: in 160 files, active: in 105, disabled: in 53 across app/components). But the focus third directly collides with a dated, repeated, explicit owner decision: on 2026-07-01 the owner killed the global 2px ink :focus-visible outline on ALL interactive chrome (buttons/links/pills) after it 'lit up on every button/link/pill', then on a second furious repeat killed plain :focus too for iOS tap-focus leakage. The comment in code states plainly: 'this drops the WCAG 2.4.7 keyboard focus indicator on chrome, owner call.' That is the exact tradeoff this principle calls non-negotiable ('a WCAG requirement'), and the owner has now overridden it twice, on purpose, in writing. Only inputs keep a focus treatment (box-shadow halo). The 'visited link' portion of the claim also has no fit: Solen's links are a handful of in-page nav/CTA links (Mehr lesen, Buchung verwalten), not a large reference list where visited-tracking helps the user, and a purple visited state would violate LOCKFILE's 'no new semantic hues invented' rule and the 80/17 neutral+sparse-blue palette. Net: most of the claim is already shipped; the part that isn't is a known, litigated rejection, not a gap.
- Law evidence: app/globals.css:350-366 (commit e6af39ad1, 2026-07-01, 'owner: no focus rings, furious repeat'; commit 0c91d5d04 second furious repeat) removes :focus-visible/:focus outline on a/button/[role=button]/[tabindex]/summary; LOCKFILE.md:526-535 State matrix table (hover/active/disabled already locked); LOCKFILE.md §1.5 'no new semantic hues invented'
- Code evidence: app/globals.css:360-366 (outline: none on chrome); hover:/active:/disabled: usage counts of 160/105/53 files under app+components (verified grep); grep for 'visited:' pseudo-class across app+components = 0 hits

### dm-golden-ratio-spacing , Generate a spacing scale from one fixed multiplier
- Reasoning: RATIONALE.md's design-folklore table names 'Golden ratio governs beauty' a MYTH outright (Markowsky 1992), and section 11 describes a folklore-citation gate whose literal self-test should-block example is a doc edit citing 'golden ratio dictates our card proportions' ,  this principle is the named target of an enforcement mechanism, not an open question. Past the golden-ratio branding, the harvested principle's actual mechanism (an unconstrained multiplicative scale) also fights the real locked law: LOCKFILE's spacing rhythm is deliberately three fixed numbers sitewide (32 section / 12 card / 16 group), and RATIONALE.md states the whole point of that choice is maximizing constraint ('the 32/12/16 rhythm tiers restore the constraint at the layout level'). A generative scale, whatever the ratio, produces more permissible values than three, which runs directly against a system whose stated goal is fewer numbers, not more.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:235 (myth table: 'Golden ratio governs beauty | MYTH (Markowsky 1992...)'); RATIONALE.md:225 (folklore-citation gate, should-block self-test is literally a golden-ratio spacing claim); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:442-450 ('Three numbers, sitewide... Everything vertical maps to one of them': Section 32px / Card 12px / Group 16px); RATIONALE.md:140 ('the 32/12/16 rhythm tiers restore the constraint at the layout level')
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js has no spacing-scale override (uses Tailwind's default linear 4px-step scale, not a multiplicative ratio); no golden-ratio or geometric-multiplier spacing utility exists anywhere in the codebase

### dm-complementary-contrast-alerts , Complementary contrast for attention-demanding moments
- Reasoning: Solen's alert/error treatment is deliberately mono-hue: one locked red family (#DC2626 icon/text on #FEE2E2 pale bg, 'ONE red system' V3-D421) carries the entire error signal, and the recovery action stays ink, never a second contrasting hue. Adding a complementary cyan/teal 'Fix now' button would introduce a hue that exists nowhere in the palette, directly against the icon-color law's 'never invent a hue.' It would also cut against the reason the system leans on red specifically: red-green is the highest-CVD-risk pair (~8% of men), so the system's answer to 'must interrupt' is icon+text+color redundancy inside one hue, not a second saturated hue competing for attention. For a calm Swiss marketplace a red/cyan double-jolt reads as adtech urgency, the opposite of the restraint the owner locked repeatedly (V3-D421 one red system, blue sparse).
- Law evidence: LOCKFILE.md:71 (Error = s-error #DC2626/#FEE2E2, 'V3-D421 consolidated error onto the locked red, ONE red system'); LOCKFILE.md:1214 'Never invent a hue (§0.4)'; RATIONALE.md folklore table: 'Harmony schemes as laws -> Scaffolds (Itten tradition), weak direct evidence -> Use as starting constraints'; RATIONALE.md CVD section: red-green highest-risk pair, 'color never the sole carrier.'
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/ErrorState.tsx:57-58 (bg-s-error-bg pale chip + text-s-error icon, single red family) and :64-70 (retry button is bg-s-ink white text, not a second hue).

### dm-monochromatic-component-hierarchy , Monochromatic palette for component hierarchy
- Reasoning: Solen's actual Primary/Secondary/Ghost hierarchy is not a single-hue lightness/saturation ramp: Primary is an ink FILL (#0A0A0A), Secondary/Ghost are an INK OUTLINE, and blue is explicitly locked OUT of buttons entirely at every tier ('blue NEVER FILLS a primary action button'; 'Secondary/ghost buttons: INK outline, never blue-filled, never blue-ghost'). The hierarchy channel here is fill-weight (solid vs outline vs bare) plus size, not hue-desaturation, because blue is reserved system-wide as the sparse hyperlink-reading signal; spending it on a four-step button ramp would blow that budget and blur the one meaning blue currently carries. A blue Primary-Secondary-Ghost ramp would resurrect the exact 'blue-filled primary' pattern V3-D192-fix already killed.
- Law evidence: LOCKFILE.md:16 (V3-D192-fix: 'Accent blue NEVER FILLS a primary action button... never a blue-filled primary'); LOCKFILE.md:173 ('Secondary / ghost buttons: INK outline, never blue-filled, never blue-ghost'); LOCKFILE.md:531 (Primary button recipe = bg-s-ink); CLAUDE.md taste rule 3 ('blue is SPARSE... NOT on secondary buttons').
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/dashboard/calendar/page.tsx:820, app/[locale]/dashboard/cases/page.tsx:266, app/[locale]/dashboard/upcharge/page.tsx:192 (bg-s-ink primary/retry buttons live in code); no blue-filled or blue-outline button variant found in a repo-wide grep for border-s-ink/bg-s-ink button patterns.

### dm-60-30-10-color-ratio , 60:30:10 color ratio (base / secondary / CTA accent)
- Reasoning: This exact rule is already named and rejected in Solen's own folklore table as an interior-design folk rule with no empirical basis, and replaced with the system's own dated internal budget: ~80% neutral surfaces + ~17% ink + a deliberately sparse (well under 10%) blue accent, which is a different ratio for a different reason. The harvested principle's own worked example breaks two more locks at once: its dark #0f172a base fights the owner's on-sight rejection of web dark mode, and its amber #f59e0b 'CTA' color fights the locked CTA hue, Solen's one primary commit button is always ink (bg-s-ink), never the amber token, which is reserved for warning chips only. Nothing here is a genuine gap, it is a named-and-superseded folk rule wearing a slightly different ratio.
- Law evidence: RATIONALE.md folklore table: '60-30-10 color proportion -> Interior-design folk rule, no empirical basis -> Our 80/17 is an internal CONV budget, labeled as such'; CLAUDE.md taste rule 3 ('~80% neutral surfaces... ~17% ink... blue is SPARSE'); REJECTED_TREATMENTS.json entry 'web-dark-mode' (owner declined web dark mode on sight, 2026-07-15).
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:175 (s-warning amber #F1AE27/#FDF6E7/#B45309 is a distinct warning-only token, not a CTA color); LOCKFILE.md:531 and multiple live call sites (dashboard/calendar/page.tsx:820 etc.) confirm CTAs are bg-s-ink, never amber.

### dm-analogous-harmony-related-set , Analogous harmony for a related group of elements
- Reasoning: The exact surface the principle's own example targets, a row of stat cards, already exists on the dashboard as the locked DashStatCard component, and it is explicitly neutral: white background, ink hairline border, ink value text, with color appearing ONLY on the delta arrow (green up, red down, grey flat) as a semantic signal, never as a per-card decorative hue. Giving Revenue/Growth/Profit each a different wheel-adjacent accent color would invent three new semantic hues outside the closed color table, which LOCKFILE bans outright, and would break the 80/17 B&W restraint that governs every customer and dashboard surface alike. This is a direct conflict, not just a style mismatch.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:18 section 0.4 "No new semantic hues invented. Use the section 3 universal-color table... Don't pick a 'nice teal' for a status"; CLAUDE.md taste rule 3 "80/17 surfaces+ink; blue is SPARSE"
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/dashboard/DashboardUI.tsx:122-160 DashStatCard (white bg, border-s-border hairline, ink value, color confined to the semantic delta arrow only); _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:158 "DashStatCard... Ink value + semantic delta arrow... locked V3-D346"

### dm-fake-typing-duration , Fake duration: artificial typing-indicator delay (dark pattern)
- Reasoning: The source material frames this as manipulation to avoid, not a pattern to copy, and it directly collides with Solen's strongest, most-repeated law: never render a status that doesn't correspond to live reality.
