A fake 'they are typing' delay before an already-composed message is precisely a fabricated status, the same category of lie as a fake 'Frei in 15 Min' badge. It also has no live surface to attach to: chat/messaging was fully turned off 2026-06-13 and the actual TypingIndicator.tsx component was deleted and graveyarded. The one surviving 'typing' visual is a decorative, infinitely-looping Typewriter animation on the /business marketing page illustrating how replies look, not a real message-send timer, so there is nothing to retrofit a fake delay onto without first resurrecting a killed feature and then deliberately lying to real customers about when a real human typed something. Correct call for a calm, trust-first Swiss booking product is to leave this out entirely.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md taste rule 1 ("No fabricated data... a fake value is worse than a dot, it's a lie the user trusts"); _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:64 (hard line 1: "No fabricated numbers, counts, urgency, or social proof, ever"); _design-system/REMOVED.md:67 (TypingIndicator.tsx explicitly graveyarded 2026-07-11 as a dead fragment of the chat feature turned off 2026-06-13, meaning a graveyard hit requiring an explicit owner yes to ever re-propose)
- Code evidence: grep for chat/messaging shows the feature is dead (REMOVED.md:63-67); the only remaining 'typing' artifact is app/[locale]/_components/homepage/BentoBusiness.tsx:144-169, a looping Typewriter + pulsing-dots illustration on the /business marketing page with no real message-send event behind it, not a functional messaging surface

### dm-press-compress-then-overshoot , Press feedback: compress then spring past rest
- Reasoning: Solen's press-feedback recipe is a single locked value per control tier (CTA/card 0.97, row 0.98, icon 0.94) applied on ease-thud, a decisive accelerate curve with no overshoot, and the gesture-physics section of the lockfile is explicit that spring overshoot past rest is earned ONLY by real finger momentum on a dragged element (sheet drag, SearchMorph), never by a discrete tap. Adding a 1.05x overshoot bounce to an ordinary button release is exactly the case the lockfile calls out as banned. A tap on 'Buchen' or 'Bezahlen' overshooting past its resting size would also read as toy-like against the Uber/Apple-restraint DNA this product is calibrated to, the opposite of the intended premium feel.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1446-1450 ('Return home / any default UI spring | 1.0 (bounce 0, critically damped, no overshoot)... Overshoot is EARNED by gesture momentum only, a bounce on something that merely faded in is banned'); LOCKFILE.md:1427 scopes the gesture/overshoot rules ONLY to pointer-driven 1:1 drags, explicitly excluding button presses/toggles which stay on the fixed 4-easing set.
- Code evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md:481-485 (`ease-thud` cubic-bezier(0.7,0,0.84,0), 'Decisive press-down feel (button press scale)', no overshoot curve defined) and SOURCE.md:515-518 (every button/card row locks a single `active:scale-[0.9x] active:duration-[80ms]` value, not a two-stage compress-then-overshoot); grep for `whileTap` across app/components shows single-value taps (e.g. HeroDuo.tsx:137 `whileTap={{ scale: 0.97 }}`), never a two-keyframe overshoot sequence.

### dm-entrance-200-300ms , Entrance animations land in the 200-300ms window
- Reasoning: Solen's dominant entrance pattern is not 200-300ms, it is a dated, owner-approved, gate-enforced LOCK at 420ms (opacity 0->1 + scale 0.96->1 + blur 8px->0, glide easing). The lock exists precisely BECAUSE a faster/subtler entrance was tested and rejected: MOTION.md records that a sub-300ms opacity-only fade was imperceptible to the owner ('i dont understand it') and got reverted, and the owner's own words locking the recipe were 'make rule abt it for any new stuff.' A blanket 200-300ms ceiling would force reverting that explicit, dated decision. Sheet entrances go even further (600ms, justified as 'long-distance smooth' for a large surface). This is not a case where a faster window is simply unconsidered, it was tried and explicitly rejected as underwhelming for a 'material arriving' feel, which is the opposite failure mode the harvested principle warns about (sluggishness). Some minority overlays (Modal 250ms, Toast 200-350ms) do land inside or near the claimed band, but that's incidental, not because the product follows a universal 200-300ms rule.
- Law evidence: _design-system/MOTION.md:16-44 (ENTER RECIPE, 420ms locked 2026-07-09, gate-enforced by ~/.claude/hooks/motion-recipe-gate.py) and lines 78-79 (owner: earlier subtle/faster fade was imperceptible, reverted, then explicitly told to go BIGGER not smaller); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:594 'Sheet open/close: 300ms ease-glide' plus §4 canonical duration list up to 500ms
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Sheet.tsx:43 (duration-[600ms] ease-glide entrance for the sheet surface); the shared enter-motion module referenced in MOTION.md:33-34 ships 420ms as the default for card/page entrances app-wide

### dm-variable-rewards-compulsive-recheck , Variable rewards manufacture compulsive re-checking
- Reasoning: This is a real mechanism (slot-machine variable-ratio reinforcement) but it is the wrong lever for this product on two counts. First, Solen's own booking cadence (3-8 week cycles per law 13) sits below any realistic habit-formation floor, so a variable-reward loop on booking would be manufacturing compulsion with no natural underlying resource to reward, i.e. pure manipulation rather than a byproduct of genuine utility. Second, the one surface the product designates as its legitimate habit surface (Inspo) is explicitly locked to the opposite design: deterministic ranking, not surprise payoff. Building this would directly contradict a named, dated law, not just be unnecessary.
- Law evidence: PSYCHOLOGY.md law 13 (lines 51-52): 'Streaks/variable-reward mechanics on booking are miscalibrated by design... Habit-design budget goes to Inspo (open-at-will, no purchase intent), with deterministic, not surprise, rewards.' Hard line 1 (real numbers only, backfire risk once noticed) and hard line 6 (no dark-pattern countdown unless live and user-relevant) reinforce the same anti-manufactured-urgency stance.
- Code evidence: No random/variable-reward code exists in the Inspo feed: grep for Math.random, streak, or surprise-reward patterns in app/[locale]/inspo returns nothing, confirming the deterministic DNA-point ranking (project_style_affinity_for_you) is what actually ships, not just what's documented.

### dm-progress-numeric-plus-checkmark-stepper , Progress indicators should pair a numeric fraction with a labelled, checkmarked stepper
- Reasoning: Solen's flows (booking, walk-in queue) are 3-4 steps, short enough that a single well-designed indicator already tells the user where they are; stacking a numeric percentage on top of a stepper is exactly the redundancy the owner rejected once already ('why two') and fights the calm-not-loud B&W restraint (three simultaneous progress signals reads as anxious, not reassuring, for a beauty-appointment flow that takes under two minutes). The checkmarked-stepper-plus-label half of the principle is not a gap, it already ships (queue tracker); the numeric-fraction half is explicitly graveyarded for the other progress surface in the product (booking). Net: the ADD would double the code that already exists and violate a named lock at the same time.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1246 'One progress indicator per screen. Owner flagged a screen showing both a step tracker AND a separate time/progress bar ,  "why two." Pick the step tracker OR a single bar, never both.' + _design-system/REMOVED.md:15 'booking stepper progress-bar step-indicator | ANY progress UI in the booking flow (icon stepper, segment bar, Schritt-N meta) | mockup-20 lock: back arrow is the navigation, exactly Fresha'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/queue/[token]/page.tsx:314-319,423-449 already ships the exact checkmark-stepper-plus-label recipe from LOCKFILE §13.2 (done=blue disc+Check icon, current=ring, future=grey, 10.5px label under each node). components-legacy/booking/BookingWizard.tsx:42 'NO progress UI anywhere' (comment, confirms the booking flow deliberately carries zero progress chrome per the mockup-20 lock). Note: the SAME queue page also currently renders a separate numeric time-progress bar at lines 405-421 alongside the stepper, which is itself a live instance of the two-indicators-on-one-screen pattern LOCKFILE:1246 was written to ban (worth a separate cleanup flag, out of scope for this principle's verdict).

### dm-range-selection-hover-click-drag , Range selection should progress through hover-preview, click-lock, drag-refine
- Reasoning: Solen books a single appointment on a single day, it is not a stay/rental product with a start-end date range, so there is no product surface where a customer picks a date RANGE at all; search and booking are both locked to single-select. A three-stage hover-then-lock-then-drag choreography is also fundamentally a desktop mouse pattern, and Solen is mobile-first where touch has no hover stage, so half the interaction model has no analog on the primary device. The one place a date range legitimately exists (salon owner blocking vacation days in the dashboard) is a low-frequency admin action better served by two plain date inputs (the pattern already shipped for analytics reporting) than by stagecraft built for a booking flow this product doesn't have.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md:70 'date / time | ONE DateTimePicker primitive ... NO bespoke date UI (V3-D445)'; _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:55 DateTimePicker entry: 'Don't: ... range picking (not yet built, extend here)' ,  i.e. any range picker must extend the single locked primitive, not become a new bespoke component; memory project_search_facts.md 'search SINGLE-select (owner lock)'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/DateTimePicker.tsx:38 'v1 ships single-date + date-and-time. Range picker (vacation blocks) defers to v2' (range explicitly not built); lines 460-469 show the existing day-cell interaction is a single CSS hover affordance (hover:bg-s-bg-sunken) followed directly by a click-commit, with no intermediate lock/refine stage. components-legacy/ui/DateRangePicker.tsx (dashboard analytics, the only shipped range picker in the product) uses two plain <input type="date"> fields plus presets, zero hover/lock/drag pointer choreography, and is owner-facing reporting, not a customer surface.

## DONT_ADD (27)

### dp-rule-of-odds , Rule of Odds (Parity and Perceptual Closure)
- Reasoning: Rule of odds is the same evidence class as rule of thirds, which Solen's own folklore table already demotes to a weak-evidence composition convention, never a justification, and its primary sourced use case (grouping subjects in a photographic composition) falls inside the imagery/art-direction domain the owner explicitly declined to open. Worse, applying it as written would be actively wrong on a real Solen surface: the 4 trust badges on /fuer-salons (Basel, GDPR, Stripe, no-contract) are equal-weight facts that must not read as having one dominant member, so manufacturing an unpaired 'apex' via count parity would misrepresent them. Where Solen does want a deliberate focal element (price vs. salon name on a card), it already has T1 evidence-backed tools for that (preattentive pop-out isolation, the one-ink-anchor-per-card rule), so parity tricks would duplicate a job already done better.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:250 and :786 ("Rule of thirds as perceptual law | Composition convention, weak perceptual evidence (same class as the golden ratio) | Use as a CONV heuristic, never as a justification"); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:822 (G6, "Imagery/art direction: OWNER-DECLINED 2026-07-15; listed as a known gap, needs a NEW yes")
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/fuer-salons/page.tsx:281-282 (3-step "how it works" process, already naturally odd with no rule invoked); app/[locale]/fuer-salons/page.tsx:410-416 (4 trust badges, deliberately even, equal-weight facts)

### dp-bouba-kiki , Bouba-Kiki Effect (Sound-Shape Symbolism)
- Reasoning: Bouba-kiki only bites when a name is phonetically novel and carries zero prior semantic content, forcing the listener to fall back on pure sound-shape mapping. Every Solen category name is a real word in the user's own language (Coiffeur, Barbershop, Nails, Spa) that resolves instantly on meaning; the phonaesthetic channel never gets a chance to matter once real-word semantics kick in. Solen also already solves 'what is this category' with literal, representational iconography (scissors, clippers, a nail-polish bottle, spa stones), which is a stronger, unambiguous comprehension cue than an abstract round-vs-angular shape a user would have to intuit from sound alone. Introducing category-by-category shape variance to chase phonetic congruence would fragment the ONE uniform shape language the B&W restraint pivot deliberately locked (same 16px card radius, pill buttons, rounded-full avatars, flush images across every category, no per-category branching). There is no Solen surface, current or planned, that hands a user an unlabeled abstract shape and asks them to guess its meaning from sound alone, which is the actual use case this effect serves.
- Law evidence: none found: no mention of sound-shape symbolism, phonaesthetics, or naming-to-shape mapping in RATIONALE.md, LOCKFILE.md, TASTE_LOG.md, PSYCHOLOGY.md, SOURCE.md, or any research/*.md file
- Code evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md:597 (category icons are literal representational glyphs, e.g. scissors/clippers/nail-bottle/spa-stones, not abstract shapes); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md radius contract (rounded-full avatars/icon-buttons, 16px cards, pill buttons, flush images) applied identically across all categories per the locked universal-components rule V3-D205 (no category branching), also restated project CLAUDE.md 'No category branches in components'

### dp-serif-terminals-scan-anchors , Serif terminals as optical scan anchors (unverified hypothesis)
- Reasoning: Solen carries zero serif usage anywhere and the whole type system is a deliberate, dated Swiss/International Typographic Style commitment (sans-serif as a house VALUE, not a default). The principle itself concedes it is a hedged, uncited hypothesis that runs against most controlled reading-speed research. Introducing serif terminals into price/time scan surfaces on the strength of a speculative claim, in a product whose one recorded typographic failure mode was too many competing typefaces (not too few landing marks), is a regression risk with no evidenced upside. Skip.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:213 ('Two optical cuts of one family, no third face') + LOCKFILE.md:211 (NEVER list of 5+ retired alternate faces) + _design-system/RATIONALE.md:788 (sans-serif adopted as house VALUE, Swiss/International Typographic Style)
- Code evidence: none checked in: no serif family declared in tailwind.config.js or app/**; the only 'serif' string in the design docs is the sans-serif fallback keyword (LOCKFILE.md:202-204)

### dp-variable-font-size-conditional , Variable Font Size Is Conditional, Not Automatic
- Reasoning: The caution is sound engineering advice in the abstract, but it answers a question Solen isn't asking. The codebase never adopted a variable font in the first place, it loads Inter Tight and Inter through next/font/google with an explicit static weight array, which self-hosts, subsets to Latin, and only ships the weight files a given page actually renders. There is no unmeasured 'variable is smaller' assumption anywhere in the law or the code to correct. Adding this as a design-system principle would bolt a build-tooling caveat onto a token/typography doc where it has no locked value to attach to and no surface to demo it on; it is a checklist item for a future font-loading decision, not a gap in the current one.
- Law evidence: none in LOCKFILE/RATIONALE/TASTE_LOG; _design-system/RATIONALE.md:778 mentions opsz/GRAD variable axes as 'available, not yet exploited' but that is about optical-size/grade rendering, not file-size tradeoffs, and is not a decision to add a variable font
- Code evidence: app/layout.tsx:13-14 (Inter_Tight weight:["400","500","600","700","800","900"], Inter weight:["400","500","600","700"], explicit static instances, not weight:"variable"); .next/static/media/*.woff2 confirms 10 discrete static files ranging 4KB-88KB, no single variable-axis font file present

### dp-fake-vs-true-small-caps , Fake (scaled) small caps vs. true OpenType small caps
- Reasoning: Solen has no small-caps usage of either kind to fix. Its 'Eyebrow' and 'Tag/Status' roles, the only uppercase text in the system, are plain ALL-CAPS via text-transform at one fixed size (e.g. 12px/600/tracking 0.08em), never a scaled-down synthetic small-caps effect, so there is no thinned-stroke bug to catch. Introducing genuine OpenType small caps would mean adding a stylistic feature check (smcp support in Inter) and a new casing convention to a system that already solved 'a distinct, legible small label' with uppercase plus weight plus tracking, one of the 11 enumerated, drift-gated typography roles. That is added typographic machinery for a booking-flow product whose type law optimizes for restraint and a small closed vocabulary, not for editorial refinement techniques with no live target surface.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:287-302 (Type Role Registry: Eyebrow and Tag/Status are the only two UPPERCASE-permitted roles, both plain uppercase text-transform, not small caps) and :339-343 (uppercase application policy, rule A7, caps only two roles)
- Code evidence: app/globals.css:853 shows the only uppercase implementation is text-transform:uppercase; grep for font-variant-caps/small-caps across app, components, lib returned zero matches, confirming neither fake nor true small caps exist anywhere to compare or fix

### dp-grid-columns-lcm , Grid columns = the least common multiple of the splits you need
- Reasoning: This principle solves a problem from an earlier generation of layout tooling: fixed float/table grids needed a shared literal column count (12 = LCM of 2,3,4) because a cell could only span whole columns. Solen's layout code is CSS Grid via Tailwind's grid-cols-[...] and fr syntax, which expresses any split ratio directly without a shared intermediate unit. The Footer already renders a 1.5fr/1fr/1fr/1fr/1fr split, the dashboard already does a 2-of-3 and 1-of-2 split via col-span, and SalonHero already does a 2-of-3 photo layout, none of them derived from, or blocked by the absence of, a 12-column matrix. Bolting a formal 12-column grid system on top would add an abstraction layer that fixes no observed pain point and works against the product's restraint-first DNA (new infrastructure nobody asked for). This is an internal engineering-convention question, not a customer-facing taste gap, so it does not clear the bar for ADD on this product.
- Law evidence: none (no LCM/12-column system is referenced anywhere); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:816-818 documents container max-widths (1280px / 1180px), not a fixed column-count grid
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/layout/Footer.tsx:101 (grid-cols-2 md:grid-cols-[1.5fr_1fr_1fr_1fr_1fr], an arbitrary fr split with no LCM basis) + app/[locale]/dashboard/page.tsx:177-240 (grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-2/3 with ad hoc col-span-2/col-span-1 splits) + app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonHero.tsx:223-229 (grid-cols-3 grid-rows-2, col-span-2 row-span-2 Fresha-pattern photo grid) - all three achieve arbitrary splits directly through CSS Grid fr/span, with no shared column-count system involved

### dp-icon-padding-compensation , Icon-side padding compensation
- Reasoning: This formula solves a layout problem Solen's buttons don't have. The technique (P_icon = P_text minus gap) only matters when a button is a fixed-width box with independent left/right padding and an icon anchored to one edge, text anchored to the other, so the optical imbalance from the icon's internal whitespace shows up as a visible drift toward one side. Every icon+label button actually shipped in this codebase is built the opposite way: flex + justify-center + a fixed gap token, so the icon and label move together as one centered unit inside the button, with the SAME padding on both outer edges (LOCKFILE's DS-2 rule locks standalone-button horizontal padding symmetric, ~2x vertical, e.g. 12/24, no asymmetric icon-side exception carved out). Centering the icon+text group sidesteps the exact optical-imbalance problem the principle is trying to fix, which is a cleaner solution for a system that already bans per-component invented values (taste rule 9, ground in the locked component, don't eyeball). Introducing a new asymmetric-padding formula would mean two competing button-padding systems (the locked symmetric DS-2 ratio vs. a new per-icon-button exception), which is exactly the kind of 19-variant CTA drift the LOCKFILE's own audit history says caused the 'cheap CTA fonts' / 'busy hierarchy' complaints in the first place.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:438-439 (Button padding ratio DS-2: standalone buttons get symmetric horizontal padding approx 2x vertical, no icon-side carve-out); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:285 (root-cause note: 19 distinct primary-CTA variants existed because no upstream policy enforced role-to-recipe pairing, which a new bespoke padding formula would reopen)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/queue/[token]/page.tsx:534 (directions CTA: flex items-center justify-center gap-[7px], icon and label centered as one unit, no independent pl/pr); grep across app/ and components/ for an icon button using asymmetric pl-/pr- values around an icon returned no matches, confirming the pattern is absent from the codebase

### dp-logo-clearspace-self-scaling , Logo clearspace as a self-scaling exclusion zone
- Reasoning: This principle answers a brand-collateral question Solen doesn't currently have: how do you protect a mark that gets reproduced at arbitrary, unpredictable sizes by third parties (packaging, co-branded print, partner decks). Solen's actual Logo primitive is a locked CVA component with exactly four hardcoded size variants (sm 18px/md 28px/lg 40px/xl 64px), each shipped with its own owner-approved surrounding treatment tied to a specific context (header collapsed, header default, marketing hero, landing splash), and its placement in each context is already governed by the surrounding layout's own locked spacing (flex gaps in Header.tsx, tuned through several dated owner decisions like V3-D410/V3-D411 visible in the component's own history). A self-scaling cap-height-multiple margin formula would be solving for infinite reproduction sizes that never occur in this codebase, and would sit on top of, and potentially conflict with, spacing that was already hand-tuned per context. The Terms page does reference partner-facing 'brand guidelines published by solen.ch' that don't appear to exist as an actual document anywhere in the repo, which is a real gap, but it's a content/publishing gap, not evidence that the mechanism this principle proposes (a scaling exclusion-zone formula) is the right fix; building one speculatively, with no partner co-branding surface active and no owner ask, is exactly the kind of new-system-before-checking-the-need move rule 12 (research before you build) warns against.
- Law evidence: No LOCKFILE/RATIONALE/TASTE_LOG entry defines a logo clearspace, exclusion zone, or minimum-size rule; the only related law is the Logo.tsx component's own locked four-variant size table (comment-documented lock, V2-D27/V3-D193/V3-D146)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Logo.tsx:17-21,38-56 (four fixed size variants, no clearspace/margin prop or logic); app/[locale]/terms/components/TermsContent.tsx:250 references partner 'brand guidelines published by solen.ch' but no such guideline document exists anywhere under _design-system/ or public/ (searched, no hits)

### dp-even-stroke-weights , Even Stroke Weights for Pixel-Perfect Center Alignment
- Reasoning: This technique targets fixed-scale, pixel-hinted raster icon design (single export size, path snapped to an integer pixel grid so a center stroke splits into whole pixels each side), which is not how Solen ships icons at all. Solen renders scalable Lucide SVGs via CSS at many different sizes across the product (14px meta icons up to 56px focal discs per LOCKFILE table), across arbitrary device pixel ratios, so the on-screen physical stroke width is never the raw strokeWidth number and is anti-aliased by the browser/GPU regardless of whether that source number is even. Enforcing "must be even" would directly conflict with Solen's own currently locked stroke values, which are intentionally fractional for optical-weight-matching reasons (1.75 at >=24px, 1.9 inline default, 2.5 on small chevrons, 2.8-3 on filled-disc checks) - none of these are even integers, and RATIONALE.md already tracks the real open problem here (1.9-vs-2 internal inconsistency, an unresolved consistency gap, not an evenness gap). Adding this principle would add a second, contradicting stroke rule on top of an already-flagged one instead of fixing the actual documented issue.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:557-563 (retrofit item 8: "Icon stroke width inconsistency... two different default stroke widths asserted for the same monochrome icon system", framed as CONV optical-weight-matching, not pixel-grid alignment); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1218 ("Stroke width: Lucide default 1.9 for inline glyphs, 2.8-3 only on a check inside a filled disc") and :1282-1283 ("Lucide only, strokeWidth 2 (1.75 at >=24px)")
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/business/FAQItem.tsx:38 (strokeWidth={2.5}), app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Select.tsx:104 (strokeWidth={2.5}), app/[locale]/_components/search/SearchTemplate.tsx:1473 (strokeWidth={2.25}) ,  live shipped stroke widths are fractional/odd by deliberate optical choice, none conform to an even-integer rule, confirming the mismatch between this principle and Solen's actual scalable-SVG pipeline.

### dp-anticipation-counter-movement , Anticipation: the pre-reveal counter-movement
- Reasoning: Solen's motion philosophy (MOTION.md thesis, owner-calibrated) is explicitly "restrained statics + rich motion" where over-decorating is the named failure mode ("visual overworking"), and the one place overshoot is allowed is gated to celebratory or gesture-driven moments only: "Overshoot is EARNED by gesture momentum only, a bounce on something that merely faded in is banned" (LOCKFILE:1448). An anticipation dip is the same category of extra flourish on a plain entrance or press, just on the opposite side of the timeline, and it adds a beat of latency before the real motion starts, which cuts against a dense booking flow's actual test: the owner already rejected a subtle imperceptible fade-up for being too faint, not for lacking a wind-up, and the fix chosen was a bigger, simpler recipe (larger offset, same three properties), not a new pre-move phase. The ENTER RECIPE is also PreToolUse-gate-enforced to exactly three properties (opacity/scale/blur); a counter-movement keyframe is a structural addition that would need a fresh owner call to even be legal to ship, not a default add.
- Law evidence: _design-system/MOTION.md (thesis: "Premium = RESTRAINED statics + RICH motion... Over-decorating... is visual overworking"), LOCKFILE.md:1448 ("Overshoot is EARNED by gesture momentum only"), MOTION.md ENTER RECIPE (locked to opacity/scale/blur only, motion-recipe-gate.py blocks additions)
- Code evidence: none checked in: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/motion.ts, app/globals.css, app/[locale]/_components/primitives/SuccessMark.tsx (choreography is ring->disc pop->check draw->text, no pre-reveal counter-move at any step)

### dp-indexical-marks-physical-impression , Indexical Marks: A Physically-Produced Impression Beats a Simulated One
- Reasoning: Solen is a digital booking marketplace with no packaging, print collateral, or physical brand touchpoint comparable to a snack-food box, so there is no surface where 'press the real material into it and scan it' is executable; the entire mechanism (a causal physical impression versus a simulated texture) has no analog in a web/mobile UI. This principle answers a disbelief problem for physical-goods branding, not a booking flow, and forcing it onto Solen would mean inventing a physical production step the product doesn't have.
- Law evidence: none
- Code evidence: none checked in: _design-system/PHOTO_STRATEGY.md and RATIONALE.md contain no packaging/print/physical-production content to extend or conflict with

### dp-unverifiable-timestamps-note , Non-principle: unverifiable segment timestamps disclosure
- Reasoning: This entry is explicitly self-described as the source material's own disclosure about missing transcript timing data, not a design claim about any interface, so there is nothing to check it against Solen's design law or code for. It should be dropped from the harvested set rather than evaluated as a principle.
- Law evidence: none
- Code evidence: none checked in: not applicable, the entry describes a documentation limitation of the source video, not a UI pattern

### dp-semantic-form-checkable-fact , Semantic Form: shape or pattern only argues when it maps to a checkable fact
- Reasoning: This is a data-journalism/editorial-infographic technique (an irregular custom illustration, like a broken chocolate bar or coastline silhouette, standing in for a specific stat). Solen is a booking marketplace with a transactional UI, not a publication that produces advocacy infographics, so there is no content genre here that would ever call for a custom illustrative shape tied to an external stat. Its actual data surfaces (dashboard revenue/analytics) use conventional recharts bar/line charts, which already read as evidence through axes and labels, not through shape symbolism. The principle's underlying spirit, that unexplained visual irregularity reads as noise rather than intent, is already enforced here by stronger, more applicable law: taste rule 1 (no fabricated data, every element must carry real information or be deleted) and RATIONALE.md's 'dead affordances are fabrications' rule. Adding a dedicated semantic-shape system would import a pattern language for a genre this product doesn't operate in.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md taste rule 1 (no fabricated data, no decorative artifacts); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:694 (dead affordances are fabrications, the interaction twin of the no-fabrication rule)
- Code evidence: none checked in: no coastline/infographic/custom-shape dataviz found in app/ or components/ (grep for coastline|infographic|silhouette returned nothing in shipped code); package.json:64 recharts is the only charting library, used for conventional bar/line charts in app/[locale]/dashboard/revenue/page.tsx and app/[locale]/dashboard/analytics/page.tsx

### dp-rgb-cmyk-gamut-mismatch , RGB-to-CMYK Gamut Mismatch
- Reasoning: Solen has no print production pipeline of any kind: it's a Next.js web app plus an Expo mobile app, and every color in the system is defined and consumed as sRGB/hex (Tailwind tokens, CSS variables). The only 'print' surface I found is a salon payout invoice that opens an HTML page and calls the browser's native window.print(), which renders through the screen's own RGB path with no CMYK ink conversion or professional press step involved at all. A CMYK-gamut-mismatch rule protects against colors that look fine on screen but die when converted for ink on paper; nothing in this product ever undergoes that conversion, so the rule has no surface to apply to.
- Law evidence: none
- Code evidence: app/api/salon/invoices/[payoutId]/route.ts:35-60 (the only print-adjacent code in the repo: a browser-print HTML invoice using @media print { .no-print } to hide a button, no CMYK/press pipeline); no InDesign/print-production tooling or CMYK references found anywhere in _design-system/ (grepped cmyk|pantone across _design-system/, zero hits)

### dp-bleed-extend-past-trim , Bleed: extend artwork past the trim line
- Reasoning: Bleed exists to compensate for a physical cutter's mechanical tolerance when trimming printed sheets; Solen produces no physically-trimmed collateral (no business cards, posters, packaging, or printed marketing found anywhere in the repo or design docs). The one print-adjacent surface (the browser-print salon invoice) is printed by the salon owner on whatever printer/paper they have, with no die-cut trim step downstream. There is no artwork boundary in this product that a blade ever crosses, so the concept has no application.
- Law evidence: none
- Code evidence: none checked in: grepped bleed|trim line|print file across _design-system/*.md, only hits are the UI-layout term 'full-bleed' (photo layout reaching the edge of a screen container) and CSS negative-margin 'bleed' (ScrollRow carousel technique, SOURCE.md:412, COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:109/SectionTitle.md), both screen-layout metaphors unrelated to physical print trim

### dp-icc-profile-screen-to-print , A color value has no fixed appearance across color spaces: assign an ICC profile
- Reasoning: Same root cause as the other two print-color principles: there is no print production step anywhere in Solen's pipeline for an ICC profile to attach to. Every color the codebase renders (Tailwind s-ink/s-accent/etc. tokens, CSS variables, hex literals) is consumed directly by a screen (browser or iOS/Android renderer); there is no press, ink, or paper stage where the same hex would shift. Soft-proofing against an ICC profile is only meaningful once a print output exists to proof against, which this product does not have.
- Law evidence: none
- Code evidence: none checked in: no .icc/.icm profile files, no CMYK color definitions, and no print-shop/press references anywhere in _design-system/ or the app's color token files (app/globals.css, tailwind.config.js define colors as hex/rgb only)

### dp-spot-pantone-vs-cmyk , Spot (Pantone) Ink vs. CMYK Process Color
- Reasoning: Solen has no physical print-production surface: it is a web/mobile booking marketplace, not a company that ships brand collateral through offset or digital press runs where spot-ink consistency matters. The one 'print' surface that exists (a server-rendered salon-payout invoice PDF, app/api/salon/invoices/[payoutId]/route.ts) is a digital document viewed on screen or laser-printed by an owner ad hoc, not a controlled spot-color production process. Every color in the system is already a single defined digital value (hex/OKLCH token), which is precisely the property Pantone buys in the physical world, so the problem the principle solves (CMYK's halftone-dot approximation drifting from a target) has no analog here: there is no drift to fix because there is no ink laydown. Importing this principle would mean inventing a print-color-management workflow the product does not need.
- Law evidence: none, no print-production section exists in LOCKFILE.md / RATIONALE.md / SOURCE.md
- Code evidence: app/api/salon/invoices/[payoutId]/route.ts (only print-adjacent surface, a digital PDF, not spot-ink production); none checked in: _design-system/*, app/**, components/**

### dp-manuscript-canon-1-1-2-2-margins , Manuscript canon: diagonal-constructed margins land near 1:1:2:2
- Reasoning: This canon is defined for facing book pages with a physical spine and gutter (top:inner:outer:bottom asymmetry exists because the inner margin sits against a binding). Solen has no paginated, two-page-spread surface: it is a continuously scrolling single-column mobile-first product with no spine or gutter concept, so the specific 1:1:2:2 ratio has no honest host surface to apply to. The underlying perceptual mechanic it rests on (a mathematically-centered block reads as sitting too low) is real and already tracked at the correct digital granularity: RATIONALE.md domain 5's 'Optical vs mathematical alignment' section already prescribes eye-checked nudges for icon-in-disc centering and chevron/play-glyph optical centering, which is the right-scale generalization of the same optical-center problem for a UI made of small controls and cards rather than book spreads. Importing the print ratio itself would be forcing a category (bound-page layout) onto a domain (responsive scroll UI) it was never built for.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:144 (Optical vs mathematical alignment, probe 2, unsettled) is the closest real analog and already covers the underlying mechanic at the correct digital scale
- Code evidence: none checked in: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md spacing section, app/[locale]/**/page.tsx layout wrappers; no facing-page/spine layout exists in the codebase to apply the ratio to

### dp-convincing-glass-five-parameters , Convincing glass is five independent light-physics parameters, not blur plus opacity
- Reasoning: Solen already has a LOCKED, owner-approved glass treatment (FROST_GLASS: 80% white + 4px blur + white inner border + soft shadow, lib/frost-glass.ts, used at 21 call-sites for small chrome controls over photos: hero back/share/heart buttons, card overlays). CONTROL_ELEVATION.md's MATERIALS section (owner-approved 2026-07-10 via Apple materials guidance) already reasons explicitly about vibrancy, material-weight scaling for larger surfaces, and accessibility fallbacks (prefers-reduced-transparency, prefers-contrast), which is a more scoped and already-shipped answer to 'what makes glass read correctly' for this product's actual use cases. The five-parameter model (refraction, dispersion, depth) targets large glossy panels (Apple Vision Pro / Liquid Glass control centers) where light bends visibly across a big surface; Solen's glass is a 40-44px icon-button chip, at which scale refraction/dispersion are imperceptible and CSS backdrop-filter cannot produce them without custom SVG-filter or WebGL shader work per instance, a real engineering and performance cost for a marketplace app. Chasing physically-simulated glass on tiny controls would also read as showy against the deliberate Uber-restraint, calm-not-loud DNA this product is pivoted to, and reopening a component the registry marks 'locked' without an owner-named reason conflicts with the never-redesign-approved-surfaces rule.
- Law evidence: _design-system/CONTROL_ELEVATION.md:71-82 (MATERIALS section, owner-approved 2026-07-10); _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md FROST_GLASS row marked 'locked'
- Code evidence: lib/frost-glass.ts:1-21 (canonical recipe); 21 call-sites found via grep for frost-glass/FROST_GLASS across app/components/lib/primitives

### dp-decoy-effect , The Decoy Effect (Asymmetric Dominance Effect)
- Reasoning: Solen is a marketplace, not a SaaS selling its own tiered product: salons own their own service/bundle pricing (service_bundles table, salon-side CRUD in app/[locale]/dashboard/bundles/page.tsx), so there is no place where Solen itself picks three price points to editorialize. The only structural fit (SalonBundles.tsx on the PDP) computes bundle price server-side from real services and shows real salon-created bundles; engineering a strictly-worse dominated bundle purely to steer choice would mean either fabricating a non-existent option (violates the no-fabricated-data law) or Solen unilaterally distorting a salon's real catalog (integrity problem for a marketplace that doesn't own the inventory). It also runs against the adopted law 11 'Comparability beats trimming' (help users compare fairly across real options, group and label, never manufacture an artificial anchor) and the ethics stance at RATIONALE.md:808 that already refuses the dark-pattern taxonomy regardless of EU/CH jurisdiction question. Tellingly, the psychology research fleet catalogued decoy pricing at only medium/low confidence (lab study, small artificial samples) and the owner's curated 15-law canon in PSYCHOLOGY.md, built from that same research pass, did not promote it, unlike peak-end, defaults, or price-transparency which did make the cut.
- Law evidence: _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md laws 1-15 (decoy absent from the curated canon) esp. law 11 lines 45-46 'Comparability beats trimming'; _design-system/RATIONALE.md:808 (hard lines already refuse manipulate/distort taxonomy, DSA Art. 25 cited); research files catalog it only as directional: _design-system/research/PSYCH_CONVERSION.md:115, PSYCH_BUSINESS_IMPACT.md:73,140 (medium/low confidence, 'test on Solen's own data, not assume replication')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonBundles.tsx:1-45 (bundle price computed server-side from real services.price, no artificial tiers); app/[locale]/dashboard/bundles/page.tsx (salon-owner-authored bundles, not Solen-curated); no 'decoy'/dominated-tier construction found anywhere in app/ or components-legacy/

### dm-adjacent-hues-gradient-stops , Gradient stops should sit close together on the color wheel
- Reasoning: Solen is a near-monochrome 80/17 B&W system where color is chosen by semantic MEANING (a token table: accent blue, success green, star yellow, save pink), never by hue-wheel math. RATIONALE.md explicitly downgrades harmony-wheel theory to 'a scaffold, CONV at best' rather than a rule worth encoding. The handful of real gradients in the app are either single-hue shade ramps (black-to-transparent photo scrims, white-to-transparent scroll fades, grey skeleton shimmer) or, in the one case that combines two distinct hues (accent blue #276EF1 + star yellow #FFC32B in the BentoBusiness glow, ~178 degrees apart, i.e. wheel-opposite), the pairing is deliberately justified in-code by TOKEN MEANING ('same two universal-color tokens used elsewhere on this page') and defused by heavy blur + low opacity, not by moving the stops closer on the wheel. Formalizing a wheel-adjacency law would fight the token-semantic model this product actually runs on, for a product that has maybe a dozen gradients total, none of them brand-defining hero art.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:87 ("Color psychology and harmony schemes: CONV at best... Harmony wheels (complementary/triadic) are scaffolds. Solen's hue choices are justified by universal SEMANTIC convention... plus brand restraint"); LOCKFILE.md:18 section 0.4 "No new semantic hues invented"
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/BentoBusiness.tsx:66-73 (blue+yellow radial glow, wheel-opposite hues, justified by token meaning + blur/opacity, not wheel-distance); app/globals.css:961,968 (functional white/grey single-hue fades)

### dm-gradient-angle-deliberate , Gradient angle is a deliberate decision, not a default
- Reasoning: The claim is also incomplete as harvested (it names four test angles and asserts one is correct but never says which), so there is nothing concrete to operationalize even if adopted. More importantly, Solen already derives its one systemized, load-bearing gradient (the DS-10 photo scrim used on PDP heroes and photo cards) by a FUNCTIONAL rule, not an aesthetic light-direction one: it is locked at 180 degrees, bottom-anchored, so the darkening sits exactly where overlay text needs contrast. The remaining gradients in the app (WhySolen icon-blob backgrounds at 135deg, Entdecken dark-card backgrounds at 150deg) are minor decorative accents on a handful of homepage tiles, not hero art carrying brand weight, so there's no real surface that needs a generalized angle-decision law added on top of the existing legibility-first derivation.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1136-1144 (DS-10 gradient scrim: "bottom-anchored gradient: photo stays clean, text zone earns contrast", background: linear-gradient(180deg, ...)); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:87 (aesthetic color/harmony theory treated as CONV, not law)
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1138-1139 (180deg locked scrim angle, functional derivation); app/[locale]/_components/homepage/WhySolen.tsx:154,203 (135deg decorative icon-blob gradients, minor); app/[locale]/_components/homepage/Entdecken.tsx:58-64 (150deg dark-card gradients, minor)

### dm-three-signal-drag-pickup , Three-signal drag pickup confirmation
- Reasoning: Solen has zero Kanban-style drag surfaces (no cards moved between columns anywhere in the product; 'Salon owner: booking calendar drag-to-reschedule' is still an open TODO, not built). Every real drag surface today is a single-list or single-grid REORDER (discovery-admin content grid, dashboard services list, dashboard calendar slot grid), all internal-admin/ops tools, never customer-facing. For that interaction shape, dimming the rest of the screen is actively counterproductive: the user's drop target IS the surrounding rows/cells the dim would obscure, unlike a Kanban card where the destination column is a large fixed zone that tolerates the rest of the board fading back. Two of the three signals already exist ad hoc and inconsistently (cursor-grab on all three surfaces; a lift/shadow on two of three: dashboard/calendar's slot drag gets scale-105 + shadow-2xl, dashboard/services gets a bg-sunken + shadow highlight, discovery-admin gets only an opacity fade with no lift at all), which is a real but narrow consolidation opportunity, not a case for importing the third (background-dim) signal. If a true Kanban-style booking board ever ships, this principle should be revisited as an OWNER_CALL at that point, not before.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md 'No gesture-only actions, ever (DS-11): every swipe/drag affordance has a visible button twin' (adjacent law, not directly on point); no COMPONENT_REGISTRY or MOTION.md entry codifies a canonical drag-pickup treatment, so today's three implementations are unlocked and drifted from each other
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/dashboard/calendar/page.tsx:946 (dragSnapshot.isDragging ? 'shadow-2xl z-50 scale-105' : ''); app/[locale]/dashboard/services/page.tsx:527,529 (snapshot.isDragging ? 'bg-s-bg-sunken shadow-warm-md' : '', plus cursor-grab active:cursor-grabbing); app/[locale]/dashboard/discovery-admin/page.tsx:537,541,556 (useSortable, opacity: isDragging ? 0.5 : 1, cursor-grab active:cursor-grabbing, no lift, no DragOverlay); _tasks/INCOMPLETE_FEATURES.md:247 notes two different drag libraries (@dnd-kit vs @hello-pangea/dnd) already coexist unconsolidated

### dm-segmented-code-auto-advance , Auto-advance and auto-retreat focus in segmented code inputs
- Reasoning: There is no live surface that needs this component right now. A phone-OTP backend exists (send + check routes, properly built with per-IP and per-phone rate limiting and Redis-backed codes) but it has zero frontend callers anywhere in the app. The one flow that would plausibly have used it, salon onboarding, explicitly dropped the step: the code says outright 'Step 2 is now Quick Win (phone verification removed).' Building a polished multi-box auto-advance/auto-retreat input now would be inventing UI ahead of a wired need, which is exactly what the no-invented-UI rule and the exists-check protocol exist to prevent, and it can't get a concrete mockupBrief because there's no real surface to demo it on (the ADD bar this evaluation requires). If phone verification comes back to onboarding or account security, the auto-advance/auto-retreat behavior described here is the right spec to build into that component at that time, not before.
- Law evidence: none: no REMOVED.md line formally logs the phone-verification-step removal (a gap in the graveyard protocol worth a follow-up, but not this evaluation's job to fix), and no LOCKFILE/TASTE_LOG row addresses segmented code inputs one way or the other
- Code evidence: app/api/auth/verify-phone/send/route.ts and app/api/auth/verify-phone/check/route.ts exist and are fully built, but `grep -rn "verify-phone" --include=*.tsx app` returns zero frontend call sites; app/[locale]/onboarding/salon/page.tsx:179 comment: '// Step 2 is now Quick Win (phone verification removed)'; no maxLength={1}/digit-box pattern exists anywhere in app or components (checked via grep, zero hits)

### dm-paste-problem-strip-and-distribute , The paste problem: strip and distribute pasted codes at the container level
- Reasoning: There is no segmented N-box code-entry UI anywhere in the product to apply this fix to. Solen's customer auth is email/password plus Apple OAuth, with no phone/SMS or email one-time-code screen in the frontend. Two backend OTP endpoints exist but are never called from any component, so they are dormant/orphaned, not a live surface with a paste bug. Recommending this principle now would mean inventing a verification flow that doesn't exist to justify a paste-handler pattern, which is exactly the invented-UI failure mode the project guards against; the moment the owner actually asks for phone/email verification, the container-level-paste-strip technique is good, uncontroversial engineering and should be applied then, not speculatively now.
- Law evidence: memory feedback_no_invented_ui.md 'never invent a UI element; ground in the owner's ref or an existing component' (no-invented-ui-gate.py); project exists-check protocol (CLAUDE.md 'Exists-check protocol') requires a real surface before proposing new UI
- Code evidence: grep across app/ and components/ for segmented code-box patterns (Array(6), inputRefs, codeBoxes, maxLength={1}) returned zero hits; app/[locale]/auth/register/page.tsx and app/[locale]/auth/signup/page.tsx contain no otp/verify/code references. app/api/auth/verify-otp/route.ts and app/api/auth/verify-phone/{send,check}/route.ts exist but have zero frontend call-sites (grep for verify-otp / verify-phone / verifyOtp / verifyPhone across app/ and components/ found no importer), confirming the backend is unwired and dormant.

### dm-one-string-not-n-states , Model a multi-box code input as one string, not N states
- Reasoning: Sound React state-management advice with no live surface to attach it to. Solen has no multi-box code-entry UI anywhere in the app. The two API routes that imply one, verify-otp and verify-phone/send+check, have zero frontend callers (no .tsx anywhere references either path) - they are dead endpoints left over from a phone-verification step that was explicitly pulled from the registration wizard. The one code-entry field Solen does ship, the booking-lookup order code, is already a single plain input bound to one string state, not split boxes, so the N-state trap this principle warns about was never even a temptation here. Building a 6-box OTP UI now purely to host this tip would be inventing UI ungrounded in a current owner ask; if phone verification is ever rebuilt, this is the right pattern to reach for then, matching the single-string convention the booking-lookup field already sets.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md exists-check / graveyard protocol (a removed feature is not rebuilt without an explicit owner yes) and feedback_no_invented_ui.md (never invent a UI element not grounded in an owner reference or existing component)
- Code evidence: no .tsx references verify-otp or verify-phone anywhere (grep across app/, checked); app/[locale]/onboarding/salon/page.tsx:179 ('Step 2 is now Quick Win (phone verification removed)'); app/[locale]/booking/lookup/page.tsx:340-348 (existing code field is already a single input bound to one codeBody string, not N boxes)

### dm-strength-bar-false-progress , A strength bar filling on early characters implies false progress
- Reasoning: There is no password-strength bar anywhere in Solen's codebase and no law doc references one, so there is no live instance of the false-progress bug this principle warns about to fix. More to the point, the pattern Solen already ships for the identical job, the reset-password Requirement checklist, sidesteps this exact failure mode by construction: it is a discrete per-rule checklist rather than a continuous fill, so there is no pre-attentive 'fill equals closer to done' channel for a user to misread against a text label. Adding a strength bar now, even a carefully labeled one, would introduce a second, weaker pattern alongside a proven one and reopen the precise risk this principle is arguing against.
- Law evidence: none - no LOCKFILE/RATIONALE/REMOVED entry on strength meters; the DONT_ADD rests on the existing superior checklist precedent (see dm-live-checklist-beats-post-submit-errors) rather than a written prohibition
- Code evidence: no matches for 'strength'/StrengthMeter/passwordStrength anywhere in app, components, or lib (grep, checked); superseding pattern already live at app/[locale]/auth/reset-password/page.tsx:169-175,214-221

## ALREADY_EXISTS (143)

### dp-proximity , Proximity (Gestalt grouping by spatial distance)
- Reasoning: This is the system's primary grouping mechanic and it is used precisely because it is invisible: no border, label, or extra pixel is spent to signal 'these rows belong together', which matches the calm-not-loud, 80/17-neutral DNA better than a drawn boundary would. Booking-marketplace pages are dense with repeated peer units (service rows, staff cards, review cards) that users skim fast on mobile; the three-tier spacing rhythm (section > card > group) is exactly 'space within a group is smaller than space between groups', applied sitewide as a hard rule rather than eyeballed per screen.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:52-56 ('Gestalt grouping (T1, Wertheimer/Koffka). Proximity is the strongest grouping signal... Grounds: spacing rhythm 32/12/16 (LOCKFILE:446-450): section gaps larger than card gaps IS proximity math'); LOCKFILE.md:442-461 (Spacing rhythm DS-5: Section 32px / Card 12px / Group 16px, 'Drift signal: any mt-5/mt-6/mt-7 between sections or cards').
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/ueber-uns/page.tsx:23, app/[locale]/kontakt/page.tsx:21 (space-y-8 section tier); app/not-found.tsx:12, app/[locale]/staff-invite/page.tsx:110 (gap-3 card/group tier) -- the 32/12/16 scale is applied across 40+ files at the section tier and 120+ files at the card tier, not a one-off.

### dp-similarity , Similarity
- Reasoning: The system already codifies similarity prescriptively, not just descriptively: the Twin-control rule says two controls with the same purpose MUST be the same component instance (identical size/radius/weight/color), because styling them differently is 'drift' -- i.e. it breaks the similarity signal users rely on to know two things do the same thing. For a marketplace where the same actions repeat across many salons and screens (filter chips, book buttons, category tags), enforcing one shared component (TabPill, PriceFrom, etc.) instead of per-screen restyled twins is what lets similarity work FOR fast scanning rather than against it.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:435-437 ('Twin-control rule (DS-4): two controls with the same purpose... are the SAME component instance... Styling twins differently is drift'); RATIONALE.md:53 (Gestalt grouping list names similarity explicitly); research/AUDIT_WAVE1_2026-07.md:72 ('grounded in Gestalt similarity (T1) -- identical form is what tells the eye two elements do the same thing').
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonServices.tsx:8,117 (imports and reuses the shared TabPill primitive for filter chips rather than a bespoke per-screen chip, the concrete enforcement of the twin-control/similarity rule).

### dp-common-region , Common Region
- Reasoning: This is not just present but over-specified relative to the harvested principle: the locked 'grouped list cards' rule IS common region (one bounding shape per group of same-kind rows, rows inside, never one card per row), and the system has gone further with a full sourced decision tree for exactly when a bounding region is earned versus when proximity/whitespace alone should carry the grouping -- including a named anti-pattern (the 'big bento box' stopping-point failure the owner has flagged before) for over-applying common region. Adding this principle would be reintroducing something the system already has a more nuanced, dated answer for.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:463-474 ('Grouped list cards, owner-approved 2026-06-11: any LIST of same-kind rows renders as ONE grouped card per group... NEVER separate bordered/shadowed cards per row'); RATIONALE.md:53,55 (Gestalt common region grounds the grouped-list-cards rule); research/TASTE_GROUPING.md (full decision tree: peers-vs-facets, when a container is earned vs whitespace-only, the 'stopping point' anti-pattern).
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonServices.tsx, app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonBundles.tsx, app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonProducts.tsx, app/[locale]/dashboard/bundles/page.tsx (all four use the locked shadow-whisper grouped-card treatment for same-kind row lists).

### dp-uniform-connectedness , Uniform Connectedness
- Reasoning: The named Gestalt term isn't in RATIONALE.md's short list (proximity/similarity/common-region/common-fate/continuity/figure-ground), but the actual visual pattern is live and scoped correctly: a literal connecting line joins the step nodes of the walk-in queue tracker, with the line's own fill color (done vs pending) carrying the sequence state. This is the right place for it because a queue/booking flow is a genuinely ordered sequence ('these five things happen strictly in this order'), which is exactly what a physical joining line communicates more strongly than proximity or color alone -- unlike salon cards or service rows, which are unordered peers where a connecting line would be a lie about their relationship. The component registry has already locked this exact recipe (node states, connector color logic) as the canonical ProgressStepper pattern for short sequential flows.
- Law evidence: _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md row 45 ('ProgressStepper... UNIFIED BLUE... s-border connector (done portion renders bg-s-accent), distinct Lucide glyph per node... Use: a short booking/checkout/queue flow header'); RATIONALE.md:53 lists the adjacent Gestalt cues (proximity/similarity/common-region/common-fate/continuity) but does not yet name uniform connectedness specifically, a documentation gap rather than a code gap.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/queue/[token]/page.tsx:423-449 (the 'blue step tracker': 42px node circles at line 431-440, and the literal connecting-line segment at line 444 -- `h-[2px] ... rounded-full ${lineDone ? "bg-s-accent" : "bg-s-border"}` -- joining each pair of nodes, colored by completion state); components-legacy/onboarding/SetupWizard.tsx:108-115 (a second live instance, the dashboard setup wizard's animated connecting-line progress bar).

### dp-closure , Closure
- Reasoning: For a Swiss booking marketplace under an explicit no-decorative-artifacts law (CLAUDE.md taste rule 2: 'every element carries information or it gets deleted'), closure only earns its place where the implied boundary does real work: marking an empty, not-yet-filled region without the visual weight of a solid box. That is exactly what the live photo-upload dropzones already do. A dashed-border rectangle is textbook Gestalt closure (the eye reads the gapped line as one continuous boundary, not N segments), and it is deliberately lighter-weight than a solid border, which fits the restraint doctrine (RESTRAINT_TEST.md's ~3% depth budget, calm-not-loud). Extending closure further, e.g. decorative corner-bracket 'viewfinder' frames on non-functional surfaces, would drift toward ornament the taste rules explicitly ban, so there is no genuine gap left to fill.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:53 (Gestalt catalog names proximity/similarity/common-region/common-fate/continuity/figure-ground as the grounded principles; closure is not separately named, so nothing conflicts, but also nothing new needs codifying); CLAUDE.md taste rule 2 (no decorative artifacts, restraint doctrine)
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/ImageUploader.tsx:165 (`border-2 border-dashed border-s-border` empty dropzone, live in onboarding/salon/page.tsx + SalonProfileStep.tsx + GalleryManager.tsx); components-legacy/ui/ImageUpload.tsx:330 (same pattern, second live call-site)

### dp-figure-ground , Figure-Ground
- Reasoning: Figure-ground is one of the six Gestalt principles this codebase explicitly grounds its elevation system on, and CONTROL_ELEVATION.md is an entire dedicated doctrine document built on exactly this mechanic: a control only wins white+shadow 'figure' status when it sits over unpredictable photo pixels and needs guaranteed local contrast (frosted glass), otherwise it stays flat 'ground' (ink text or bordered-white) so the one primary CTA remains the unambiguous figure. This is precisely right for a marketplace that is 80% white/ink chrome with photography confined to a few framed slots (hero, cards, gallery): applying figure-ground everywhere would manufacture grey haze and destroy the single-primary hierarchy CONTROL_ELEVATION was written to protect.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:53,716 (figure/ground named as one of the grounded Gestalt principles; FROST_GLASS mechanic explicitly labeled 'figure/ground preservation'); _design-system/CONTROL_ELEVATION.md:1-52 (full decision tree: over-photo=A frosted glass, calm surface=B flat, primary=C ink); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:492 (control elevation lock, two sanctioned white+shadow places)
- Code evidence: lib/frost-glass.ts:16-22 (FROST_GLASS recipe: 80% white + blur(4px) + inner border + shadow, used to make a control read as figure against unpredictable photo background); app/[locale]/_components/homepage/SalonCard.tsx:122,140 and app/[locale]/_components/layout/Header.tsx:713 (live call-sites of the same figure-ground treatment)

### dp-chunk-dont-count , Chunk, don't count
- Reasoning: This is not a gap, it is one of the most explicitly codified psychology rules in the whole system. The product already distinguishes recognition (scanning a visible list, not span-limited) from recall, and has a standing ban on citing Miller's 7±2 for exactly the menu/option-count use case this principle targets. For a booking marketplace, capping comparable items (service rows on a card, visible toasts, onboarding chips) at 3-4 with a 'load more' escape hatch is precisely what prevents a service list or filter set from becoming an uncomparable wall, which matters directly for the core booking-decision flow.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:71 ('Memory span: cite Cowan (~4 chunks, 2001), never Miller's 7+/-2... No Solen rule should ever justify 7 items max from Miller'); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:236 (folklore table: Miller's 7±2 for menu/option counts marked Superseded by Cowan 2001 ~4 chunks)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/dev/results-full/page.tsx:43 and app/[locale]/_components/search/SalonResultCard.tsx:468 (`.slice(0, 3)` with inline comment 'RULE: max 3 service rows per card'); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Toast.tsx:88 (`MAX_VISIBLE = 3`); app/[locale]/onboarding/OnboardingFlow.tsx:120 (`.slice(0, 4)`)

### dp-duration-matched-loading , Duration-matched loading feedback
- Reasoning: The system already runs a tiered response-time model matched to Nielsen/Doherty-Thadani thresholds, not a single blanket loading treatment: sub-100ms tap feedback, skeletons shaped to the final layout for full-module loads, and staged progress copy (not a bare spinner) once a wait can cross roughly 10 seconds, explicitly citing the labor-illusion research this principle's own claim rests on. This is the right model for a booking marketplace where the two genuinely long waits are payment confirmation (TWINT/card) and image upload, both of which already get real percentage-driven or step-labeled feedback rather than an indefinite spinner, which matters because a payment screen that looks frozen is the single worst place for the app to feel broken.
- Law evidence: _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:37 ('Feedback within ~100ms on every tap; ~1s is the flow ceiling; any wait that can exceed ~10s (TWINT/card confirm) gets staged progress copy, never a bare spinner. Skeletons ONLY for full-module loads matching final layout; small inline fetches keep small spinners'); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:739 (labor illusion / operational transparency, Buell and Norton 2011, candidate for availability waits over ~1s); _design-system/components/LoadingStates.md:15-70 (skeleton pattern, anti-pattern: spinner-only loading)
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/ImageUploader.tsx:153 and components-legacy/ui/ImageUpload.tsx:270 (`width: ${progress}%` determinate upload bars); app/[locale]/dashboard/loading.tsx and primitives/Skeleton.tsx + SkeletonCard.tsx (shape-matched skeletons per COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:30-31)

### dp-edges-before-shadow , Edges Define Elevation First, the Shadow Only Confirms It
- Reasoning: The system already treats geometry-level edge shading as the primary elevation cue and the cast shadow as secondary, but scopes it deliberately: it is applied to the two places CONTROL_ELEVATION.md sanctions any elevation at all (the one ink primary CTA and glass-over-photo controls), not universally. A flat, restrained B&W chrome cannot afford to bevel every control, that is exactly the 'grey haze' CONTROL_ELEVATION.md was written to prevent, so the fact that most controls stay genuinely flat with zero shadow and zero edge-highlight is the correct, deliberate application of the same underlying light-from-above logic (the system also explicitly locks shadows offset-DOWNWARD and pressed states going INSET, the same physical light model). Adding edge-highlights to more controls would conflict with the elevation-is-earned-not-decorated law.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:531 (state matrix: Primary button rest = `bg-s-ink + elevation-2 + inset top-highlight`); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:710,712 ('Light-from-above prior... shadows offset DOWNWARD... pressed states go INSET'; 'Two-light model... key light + ambient occlusion' as the mechanic for the elevation-1/2/3 stack); _design-system/CONTROL_ELEVATION.md:63 ('Shadow lives in exactly two places: the one ink primary, and glass-over-photo. Zero box-shadow on any control resting on white')
- Code evidence: lib/frost-glass.ts:16-22 (`boxShadow: '0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.10), inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.4)'`, an inset top-light edge highlight plus an outer confirming drop shadow, live on SaveHeart/hero/card-overlay controls); note: live ink-CTA call-sites (e.g. app/[locale]/checkout/page.tsx:374, app/[locale]/walk-in-pay/page.tsx:581) currently ship only `shadow-elevation-2` without the LOCKFILE-specified inset top-highlight, a code-vs-doc drift worth a follow-up fix, not evidence the principle itself is missing from law

### dp-optical-weight-circle-square , Optical weight compensation: circle must exceed square
- Reasoning: This is close to verbatim the exact axis Solen settled one day before this evaluation, in the owner's own words, grounded in the identical bounding-box-lies argument (a circle at equal box height encloses less area and reads smaller than a square). For a marketplace with round avatars sitting next to square action icons on nearly every surface (SalonCard avatars, staff pickers, review avatars, category tiles), this genuinely matters, which is presumably why the owner locked it rather than left it as a probe. The correction is adopted as LAW but not yet swept into components (the same 'implementation queued' state as the sibling contrast-on-sunken retune decided in the same session), so the actionable gap is an implementation pass against an already-decided rule, not a new proposal to add.
- Law evidence: _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md, '2026-07-15 Taste Lab round 1' table, Optical alignment row: 'Adopt corrections as a rule: play/asymmetric glyphs nudge toward visual center, circles next to squares get a small size overshoot, icon-text baselines eye-checked at 2x'; RATIONALE.md:144 and :754 state the same mechanic ('a circle must slightly exceed a square's box to read equal ... verified by eye at zoom, not by flexbox faith')
- Code evidence: grep for 'overshoot' across app/components/lib finds only unrelated spring-motion easing comments (SuccessMark.tsx:9, primitives/motion.ts:35, CelebrationRing.tsx:120) ,  no live callsite yet applies a numeric circle-vs-square size correction, so the decision is locked in law but implementation is still queued

### dp-left-anchored-row-icon , Left-Anchored Row Icon
- Reasoning: A booking marketplace's own list surfaces (profile menu today; staff pickers, favorites, and notification-style rows elsewhere) are exactly the 'scannable list where the eye needs a fixed landmark' case this principle describes, and Solen's own shared Row primitive already places the icon flush-left as the first flex child, at a constant size and constant gap, identically on every row instance. The pattern is both specified in the locked icon-size table (list-row leading icon, 18-20px inside a fixed box, ahead of a 15px title) and shipped in real code today, not merely aspirational. The remaining work, if any, is auditing whether every OTHER list surface reuses this same Row shape rather than a bespoke layout, which is a consistency sweep, not a new principle to adopt.
- Law evidence: LOCKFILE.md §13.7 icon-size pairing table: 'List-row leading icon | 15px title | 18-20px inside a 38-44px bg-s-bg-sunken box (radius per nesting formula) | 12-13px [gap]'; §13.7 'Ghost control recipe: rows/sidebar items are ghost buttons'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/profile/page.tsx:251-263, the shared Row component used for every profile menu item (bookings, favorites, loyalty, hair profile, looks, intake forms, referral, settings, help): `<Link className="flex items-center gap-3.5..."><Icon size={22}.../><span className="flex-1">{label}</span>...<ChevronRight/></Link>` ,  icon is the fixed first child at a constant size on every row

### dp-repetition-beats-creativity , Repetition beats creativity: cached recognition vs. reprocessing cost
- Reasoning: This is one of the more thoroughly internalized principles in the system: the owner's own feedback history is a direct real-world instance of the cache-hit-vs-reprocessing argument, discovered empirically rather than cited from cognitive science ,  a one-off, per-surface opacity-only entrance read as 'raggedy' and got replaced by a single locked ENTER RECIPE reused everywhere and machine-gated against deviation. For a booking marketplace, users repeat the exact same flows weekly (rebooking a stylist), so consistent, boring, repeated visual and motion language is a retention asset, not a creativity deficit, which is why the codebase already blocks per-surface novelty in motion and forces twin controls to be the identical component instance in statics. There is no gap to add; the system is already stricter than the harvested principle since it enforces this with a PreToolUse hook, not just a guideline.
- Law evidence: RATIONALE.md:359-365 'Twin-control rule' ('Two controls sharing one purpose... must be the SAME component instance... OPTIMIZES FOR: predictability, a user who learns one control's behavior recognizes its twin instantly... MECHANIC: Gestalt similarity (T1), identical form is the grouping cue'); MOTION.md 'THE ENTER RECIPE' ('Source of truth in code: the shared enter-motion module. Never hand-roll initial/animate per surface, import the variants, same as <SuccessMark> is never rebuilt per screen')
- Code evidence: MOTION.md documents `.claude/hooks/motion-recipe-gate.py` as a PreToolUse hook that blocks a net-new framer-motion entrance deviating from the locked recipe, i.e. repetition is enforced by gate, not just convention; TASTE_LOG.md:288 records the content-repetition mirror of this same law ('a person/event appears in EXACTLY ONE place; no right-rail to-dos... restating the page')

### dp-center-bias , Center Bias: One Focal Element at the Layout Center
- Reasoning: For a calm-not-loud booking marketplace, the actionable content of this claim (one heading + one CTA at the focal point, secondary chrome pushed to the edges) is already the locked pattern on exactly the two surfaces the principle names: EmptyState and Modal. EmptyState centers a single icon, one heading, one message, one CTA and nothing else; Modal is explicitly documented as the centered, single-task-focus primitive with a fixed Header/Body/Footer slot structure that keeps secondary controls (cancel, eyebrow) subordinate to the one primary action. This already IS the practical 'one focal element at center' rule the house enforces via the one-ink-anchor card rule and the per-screen ceiling of max 2 elements at the top prominence tier. One honest caveat if this were ever written up as new law: Solen's own scanning research (TASTE_HIERARCHY.md) explicitly grounds hierarchy in TASK-optimized patterns (F-pattern as failure mode, layer-cake as target, spotted-pattern for search) and explicitly demotes the unverified 'Z-pattern' claim to folklore precisely because no primary eyetracking source validates a fixed geometric-attraction claim like this one. A literal 'eye lands at center by default' assertion would need the same CONV/T3 tiering discipline, not blanket acceptance, if it were ever promoted from practice to written law.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 2 (squint/blur test as the named focal-hierarchy method; per-screen ceiling 'max 2 elements at the top prominence tier', NN/g, CONV) and section 13 item 4/section 2 (one-ink-anchor rule, T1 Ware/Bertin preattentive pop-out, LOCKFILE:375 A13); _design-system/research/TASTE_HIERARCHY.md findings 6-8 (squint test, 'if you emphasize everything nothing gets focus', max 2 large elements) and folklore-table rejection of Z-pattern as unverified (section 12 of RATIONALE.md)
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/EmptyState.tsx:56-57 (flex flex-col items-center justify-center text-center wrapping exactly one icon, one heading, one optional message, one action slot); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Modal.tsx:88-107 (doc comment: 'Centered overlay for confirmations, login flows, focused single-task interactions'; fixed Header/Body/Footer composition keeps one primary CTA in ModalFooter)

### dp-banner-blindness , Banner Blindness
- Reasoning: This is not a hypothetical for Solen: the exact artifact the claim warns against (a persistent top-strip promo banner, ad-slot shaped) was built and then killed. The house's own research corpus already carries the identical NN/g finding this principle restates. What survives in production is only banners that ARE integrated into content flow and match surrounding container styling, which is precisely the fix side of the claim: SetupBanner (dashboard) uses the exact same border/radius/background chrome as an ordinary dashboard card, carries genuinely task-critical personalized progress data, and is dismissible, not decorative; OfflineBanner only mounts during an actual connectivity event, never as a persistent slot. Nothing in the current surface set looks like the 'BANNER' boxes the claim's example eye-tracking demo tests against. Promoting this to a formal named law would be re-deriving a decision the product already made and then reversed once.
- Law evidence: _design-system/research/PSYCH_PSYCHOLOGY.md:79 ('Banner blindness: ad-zone content gets ~0.8% of fixations; simple clean thumbnails outperform busy ones', NN/g citation, application already stated: 'reject busy/low-contrast salon photos, one clear focal subject')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/layout.tsx:69-97 (Hims-style top promo banner built V3-D92/V3-D97 with measured dimensions, then 'V3-D125 -- fully removed per user', full build-then-kill history left in comments); components-legacy/dashboard/SetupBanner.tsx:52 (surviving dashboard 'banner' rendered as rounded-[12px] border border-s-ink/[0.06] bg-white, identical chrome to a regular dashboard card, not a separate ad-styled container) mounted at app/[locale]/dashboard/page.tsx:160

### dp-visual-weight-stackable-factors , Visual weight is a sum of separable, stackable factors
- Reasoning: This is the mechanics theory the house already cites to ground a dozen separate locked rules, from the one-ink-anchor card rule to the per-screen type budget to the badge taxonomy. It is not a gap, it is the file's own foundational domain-1 mechanic, and it is applied concretely in the card-grammar recipe where name/duration/price each get a distinct combination of size, weight, and lightness so exactly one element reads as the anchor. One thing to flag rather than silently accept: the harvested claim's 'multiply when pointing the same direction' language is a stronger, more formal claim than what Solen's own research found defensible. The house's folklore table explicitly rejects citing any validated ranking or multiplicative model of channel strength ('a fixed strength ranking of hierarchy channels... no validated ordering exists'), landing instead on the softer, correct claim: 'highest contrast to surroundings wins,' which is relative, not additive. If this principle were ever written into law verbatim with the multiply/cancel framing, it would need to be downgraded from the implied T1 to CONV for the stacking mechanism specifically, even though the individual factors (size, hue, isolation, lightness) are each independently T1-grounded.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 2 lines 58-61 (Preattentive attributes/isolation, T1, Ware/Bertin retinal variables: position/size read as ordered, hue/shape as categorical, isolation as pop-out) and section 28 gap register item D1 ('Visual-weight/balance centroid metric: per-element bbox area x darkness x saturation, centroid offset', already named as a recognized, not-yet-mechanized computation of this exact model); _design-system/research/TASTE_HIERARCHY.md finding 9 (four contrast categories: color, size, alignment/position, character/complexity; 'highest contrast to surroundings wins' explicitly stated as relative, not a fixed channel ranking) and the folklore-table rejection of a fixed strength ranking (RATIONALE.md section 12)
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:772-778 service-row format (name 15-16px/600 weight/ink, duration 13-14px/400/muted ink-2, price 14-15px/700/ink -- three elements each carrying a distinct stacked combination of size+weight+lightness to establish one clear anchor); LOCKFILE:1301-1311 card-grammar 5-step recipe, step 5 'one differentiated element' per card

### dp-inattentional-blindness , Inattentional blindness: attention-set filtering
- Reasoning: The mechanism is not named 'inattentional blindness' anywhere in the house docs, but the two practical defenses it implies are both already locked. First, the exploitative direction (something checked/filled outside the user's active attention gets missed and silently accepted) is exactly why pre-checked paid add-ons and marketing consent are a hard-line ban, not a style preference: a checkbox the user is not actively looking at is precisely an item outside the current attention set, and pre-checking it exploits that blind spot.
