Second, the claim's literal login-form example (an unfocused field silently produces an error nobody notices) is the stated mechanic behind two form rules: placeholder-as-label is banned specifically because it 'vanishes on input... reads as a filled value (skip errors)' -- a field that looks filled when it is not is invisible to the user's attention exactly as the principle describes -- and validation is required to render inline at the field the moment focus leaves it (onBlur), which is the concrete antidote already shipped in the booking-lookup form. Nothing here argues for a new rule; it argues for keeping the existing ones, and possibly naming the mechanism explicitly in RATIONALE.md's forms domain the next time that section is touched (a documentation nicety, not a design decision).
- Law evidence: _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md hard line 2 ('No pre-checked paid add-ons or subscriptions') and law 2 ('never pre-check anything that costs money or subscribes; that is the dark-pattern twin of the same mechanism'); _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 21 domain 17 ('Placeholder-as-label is banned by MECHANIC, not taste: it vanishes on input (recall load), reads as a filled value (skip errors)...'; 'Validation timing (T2, forms research): validate on blur or submit, never per keystroke... Error layer: WCAG 3.3.1/3.3.2/3.3.3 + Nielsen heuristic 9... inline at the field, summary linking down on long forms')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/booking/lookup/page.tsx:337-395 (each field renders its own aria-invalid + role="alert" error inline at that exact field position, triggered onBlur at lines 350 and 390, so an error surfaces visually and to assistive tech the moment attention leaves the field rather than depending on the user having noticed it while it was in view)

### dp-von-restorff , Von Restorff Effect (Isolation Effect)
- Reasoning: This is the most directly, explicitly already-covered principle of the five: it is cited by name in the law with the correct tier, and its collapse condition (a second isolated item cancels the recall boost for both) was independently rediscovered and documented as the 'vibrating blue' failure before this evaluation ever named Von Restorff. The blue-sparse rule exists because a rare accent is a preattentive isolation signal that stops discriminating once it gets common enough (8-12 percent of pixels), which is the same mechanism the claim describes for a second orange circle. The badge taxonomy enforces single-isolation directly: a card gets exactly one colored isolated mark (the discount pill, or a stepper's current-state ring) rather than stacking Top-bewertet + Neu + Beliebt + discount all competing for the same pop-out slot, which would be the multi-orange-circle failure mode the claim warns collapses everyone's recall.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:701 (table row: 'Von Restorff isolation | T1 | the one-accent pop-out math (domain 1)', explicit citation with correct tier and grounding pointer); _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 2 line 60 ('blue-sparse... an accent that is rare is a preattentive signal; at 8-12 percent of pixels it stops discriminating, the recorded vibrating blue failure') -- this is the collapse-condition half of Von Restorff already independently documented
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md section 13.3 badge taxonomy (closed table: Selected/Done/Success-focal/Status/Rating/Saved/Notification-count, one color+one meaning per mark, no stacking); project_card_badges.md memory confirms 'SalonCard discount = pale-green -X% pill right slot; NO Top-bewertet/Neu/Beliebt badges' -- a direct, dated ban on the exact multi-isolated-item pattern that collapses Von Restorff's advantage

### dp-optical-vs-geometric-center , Optical center vs. geometric center
- Reasoning: Solen already names this exact mechanic, tiers it, and has an owner-dated adoption: bounding-box centering misrepresents visual center, so the house rule is to correct by eye, not by trusting flexbox math. The harvested principle's fixed 'shift up 2-6%' formula is actually a narrower, less flexible version of what is already locked: the adopted rule deliberately stays case-by-case (asymmetric glyph nudges, circle-vs-square overshoot, baseline eye-checks at 2x) because a single universal vertical constant does not hold across a play-triangle-in-a-circle, a headline in a hero, and a checkmark in a badge. Codifying one fixed percentage would be a regression from the more general rule already in force.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:144 ("Optical vs mathematical alignment (T2; unsettled axis at Solen, probe 2)" - bounding boxes lie, circles/triangles read off-center, corrected by eye not flexbox); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:187 (Taste Lab round 1, 2026-07-15, ADOPTED: "play/asymmetric glyphs nudge toward visual center... icon-text baselines eye-checked at 2x")
- Code evidence: none checked in: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/SuccessMark.tsx, stepper/disc primitives ,  the adopted rule is explicitly a per-instance visual check, not a coded offset formula, so there is no single grep target for it by design

### dp-bars-over-pies , Perceptual accuracy ranking: length and position beat angle and area
- Reasoning: This is already in the system almost verbatim, down to the identical citation. Cleveland & McGill 1984 is named, the operational rule (bars/dots over pies, never encode magnitude in shading alone, always start bar charts at zero) is locked, and there is a dedicated chart-type decision rule that bans pie/donut/gauge/3D outright for dashboard metrics. The dashboard's actual shipped chart inventory (sales line chart, upcoming bar chart) already follows it. Re-adding this would duplicate an existing, well-sourced, already-enforced rule rather than fill a gap.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:760 ("Cleveland and McGill 1984 (T1)... bars or dots over pies/donuts for any comparison") and :762 (truncated y-axis / zero-baseline rule, Correll/Bertini/Franconeri 2020); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1184 (chart color spec) and :1176 (dashboard home = "sales line chart · upcoming bar chart"); _design-system/research/TASTE_DASHBOARDS.md:7 and :55 (explicit chart-type decision rule: "Never pie, donut, gauge, treemap, or 3D for dashboard metrics")
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1176 dashboard IA spec (line/bar charts named as the only chart types); no pie/donut chart component found in app/[locale]/dashboard grep sweep

### dp-stroop-effect , The Stroop Effect: word/color-signal interference
- Reasoning: This is exactly the mechanism behind an already-locked, already-enforced Solen rule and it is architecturally impossible to violate through the normal API. The Toast primitive derives icon, color, and text all from a single 'tone' value, so a caller cannot easily produce a green check next to 'Error' text the way a naive hand-rolled component could. CLAUDE.md's taste rule 4 codifies the exact same congruence requirement in words ('never monochrome a semantic element', success stays green-disc-plus-white-check, error stays red, never swap or mute these) independent of and predating this principle's name. For a booking marketplace, getting this right matters concretely at booking-confirmation and payment-status moments, where a reflexive tap on a miscoded signal could mean acting on the wrong state during a real transaction.
- Law evidence: _design-system/research/IG_DESIGNPARSER_PRINCIPLES.md:67-79 (full Stroop write-up citing MacLeod 1991's 400-study meta-analysis, with explicit Solen relevance to status discs/toasts/badges); project CLAUDE.md taste rule 4 ('never monochrome a semantic element... success = normal green disc + white check... error = red')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Toast.tsx:92-97 (toneIcon map: Check/X/AlertTriangle/Info bound one-to-one per tone) and :208-213 (toneBadge: tint bg + saturated fg bound to the same tone) - tone drives icon, color and text together as one unit, so the component design itself prevents a Stroop-style mismatch

### dp-muller-lyer , Muller-Lyer Illusion: End-Cap Direction Distorts Perceived Length
- Reasoning: The design system already has a full first-principles write-up of this exact illusion pointed directly at Solen's own chevron-bearing surfaces (date/time pickers, breadcrumb chevrons, 'Mehr lesen' expand carets, pagination, see-all arrows) with the correct practical fix already stated: never trust the spec padding number when a directional glyph sits at one edge, measure the rendered result and nudge optically. Writing a fresh 'ADD' entry here would just be restating what already exists almost verbatim in the repo's own research corpus, which the project's exists-check protocol explicitly calls the failure mode to avoid. The genuinely open question is whether to promote this from research-tier documentation into a harder enforcement mechanism (a squint-test checklist item), which is an improve-what-exists move, not a new addition.
- Law evidence: _design-system/research/IG_DESIGNPARSER_PRINCIPLES.md:81-93 (full write-up naming Solen's exact chevron surfaces and the practical measure-don't-trust-the-number fix)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonBreadcrumb.tsx, app/[locale]/_components/primitives/DateTimePicker.tsx, components-legacy/ui/Breadcrumb.tsx (ChevronRight usage confirms the exact surfaces the doc names are real); no hard-coded asymmetric-padding token exists today, so the current enforcement is the doc's own 'measure the rendered element, don't trust equal Tailwind spacing' instruction rather than a locked pixel value

### dp-ebbinghaus , Ebbinghaus Illusion: perceived size is read relative to surrounding elements
- Reasoning: The underlying lesson (never judge size or spacing on an isolated element, always check it against its real neighbors) is already both documented with direct Solen relevance AND operationalized as a standing workflow rule across the project's own memory. RATIONALE.md's hierarchy law independently arrives at the same relative-not-absolute conclusion for a different reason ('highest contrast to surroundings wins', explicitly rejecting any fixed absolute-strength ranking). This is stronger evidence of existing adoption than most of the harvested set: the practical instruction this illusion implies (measure live, in context, never in isolation) is already the default verification discipline for this codebase, not merely an idea sitting in a research file.
- Law evidence: _design-system/research/IG_DESIGNPARSER_PRINCIPLES.md:39-51 (full write-up naming Solen's card/grid surfaces directly, e.g. a salon card's price/rating/avatar reading bigger or smaller depending on neighboring photo/card size); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:243 ('Highest contrast to surroundings wins' recorded as the hierarchy law in place of any fixed absolute ranking, the same context-dependent-perception mechanic)
- Code evidence: no dedicated component enforces this (it is a measurement discipline, not a token); the operative practice is already locked in project memory as standing workflow law: feedback_verify_ui.md ('measure before editing... real UI... gets diffed every state') and feedback_balance_anchor_derive_and_measure.md ('measure via preview_eval') both require checking an element inside its live rendered neighbors rather than in isolation, which is the exact Ebbinghaus takeaway already in force

### dp-picture-superiority , Picture Superiority Effect (Dual Coding Theory)
- Reasoning: Solen already runs photo-led, label-fused cards everywhere a decision gets made: the salon name, star rating, and price sit as text directly over the cover photo (dual coding = image + word together), never a bare photo and never a bare icon standing in for it. For a booking marketplace the picture IS the product (what the haircut/space/nails look like), so leading with it and fusing the decision facts onto it is already the correct mechanic, not a gap. Building a second, separate 'labeled image' pattern on top of this would just be relitigating something already shipped and would also brush against the owner-declined imagery/art-direction domain (G6).
- Law evidence: _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:33-34 law 7 'Photos are the decision input, not decoration' (T1, Airbnb +9% occupancy diff-in-diff; photo is the largest card element, LCP priority); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:822 G6 'Imagery/art direction: OWNER-DECLINED 2026-07-15' means any NEW imagery-pattern proposal needs a fresh owner yes, not a fresh design doc; RATIONALE.md domain 22 (icon+label, line 756) already locks when a glyph needs a fused word.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/search/CategoryHeroCarousel.tsx:135-158 renders salon name (h3), star rating, and 'ab {price} CHF' as text fused directly onto the cover photo with a bottom-up gradient for legibility (this is dual coding in production, not a hypothetical); the same fused-label pattern repeats on SalonCard for the results grid below it on the same route.

### dp-line-height-multiplier , Line-height as a font-size multiplier, split evenly (half-leading)
- Reasoning: Every locked type role in Solen already specifies line-height as a unitless multiplier of its own font-size, and CSS's native line-height behavior already splits the resulting extra space evenly above and below the glyphs (half-leading is not something to opt into, it is what the property does by default absent explicit leading-trim tooling). Headings run tighter multipliers (1.0 to 1.3) and body copy runs looser (1.4 to 1.55), which is exactly the guideline this principle states. There is nothing to add; re-stating it as a new rule would just duplicate a locked, already-shipped decision.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md type scale table (Scale role x size x weight x line-height x tracking) locks LH as unitless multipliers per role: Hero H1 1.1, Salon-PDP H1 1.1, Section H2 1.25, Subsection H3 1.3, Body large 1.4, Body 1.55, Body small 1.4, Star rating large 1.0; _design-system/RATIONALE.md:134 (WCAG 1.4.12 note) independently derives 'At 14px body: 21px line-height' which is a 1.5x multiplier, consistent with the locked 1.55 body value.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonHeader.tsx:83 leading-[1.05]; SalonSidebar.tsx:110 leading-[1.1]; SalonOpeningTimes.tsx:30, SalonReviews.tsx:90, SalonServices.tsx:239, SalonTeam.tsx:116, SalonAbout.tsx:32, SalonAdditionalInfo.tsx:71, SalonLoyalty.tsx:58, SalonContact.tsx:27, SalonProducts.tsx:100/121/179, SalonLocation.tsx:30 all leading-[1.2]; SalonServicesSheet.tsx:216 leading-[1.0] , all unitless multiplier values, all paired with a text-[clamp(...)] font-size on the same element, confirming the multiplier-of-font-size pattern is already universal across the codebase.

### dp-weight-as-hierarchy , Weight as a Hierarchy Signal
- Reasoning: Solen is a scan-first booking marketplace (cards, price rows, service lists) where pre-attentive weight signaling is exactly what the locked Type Role Registry already does: every one of the 11 roles maps to ONE fixed weight (body 400, CTA/chip/nav/label 500, all headings 600), assigned by role not aesthetic feel, with a hard per-screen ceiling of <=2 distinct weights (stricter than this principle's 2-3). The A13 card rule ('exactly ONE ink anchor' per list item, owner quote: 'using too bold... multiple times destroys my eye') is this exact mechanism applied to the highest-traffic surface (search/salon cards). Nothing to add; the system already enforces a tighter version of the claim.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:263-283 (Weight scale: body=400, CTA/label=500, headings=600, never <500 on nav/labels/prices/buttons, never 300/800) + LOCKFILE.md:283-319 (Type Role Registry, 11 roles each ONE locked weight; per-screen HARD cap <=4 sizes/<=2 weights) + LOCKFILE.md:433-448 (A13 card hierarchy: exactly one text-s-ink+bold anchor per list item)
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:226 fontFamily/weight config; LOCKFILE.md:274-282 records executed sweeps (V3-D317: font-extrabold to font-bold, 82 callsites; V3-D325: font-bold to font-semibold in heading contexts, 74 callsites -> 129 semibold headings); residual grep for font-extrabold|font-black|font-light|font-thin across app/ = 19 legacy hits, not the locked pattern

### dp-all-caps-erases-word-shape , All-Caps Erases the Word-Shape Silhouette
- Reasoning: This is a DE/FR/IT/EN product where sentence-length copy in caps would be worse than in English (German compound nouns run long), which is exactly why the owner locked uppercase to two closed roles only (Eyebrow, max 1/surface; Tag/Status). The LOCKFILE cites the identical research family (NN/g glanceable-fonts, T1) the harvested principle draws on. This is not just documented but actively gated: a static drift rule flags any uppercase class outside those two roles, and a second rule hard-bans tracked-uppercase eyebrows on customer surfaces entirely. No gap to fill.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:339-345 (rule A7: only Eyebrow max-1/surface and Tag/Status may use uppercase, everything else = drift violation, sweep target 733 to ~50-80) + _design-system/RATIONALE.md:132 ('ALL-CAPS only for single glanceable labels, never sentences, NN/g glanceable-fonts T1')
- Code evidence: .claude/skills/solen-drift-check/scripts/check.py:252-255,955-964 (A7: uppercase-outside-role rule, logs every violation) + check.py:376-378,932-937 (A21: tracked-uppercase eyebrow hard-banned on customer surfaces, 2026-06-11)

### dp-x-height-governs-scale , X-height, not font-size, governs perceived type scale
- Reasoning: This is the documented, named reason Hanken Grotesk was retired for body text (V3-D410): paired against Inter Tight at the same nominal size, Hanken's smaller x-height made 400-weight 'read thin.' The fix Solen shipped is structurally stronger than what this principle asks for (a manual per-face size correction): by using Inter Tight and Inter, which are two optical cuts of ONE superfamily, the x-height mismatch this principle warns about cannot occur by construction, so no compensating size offset is ever needed anywhere in the system.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:130 verbatim: 'Point size measures the em body, not the letters; apparent size tracks x-height. Inter Tight (display) + Inter (body) is one family in two optical cuts: harmony by construction, the same mechanism as a superfamily pairing, and the actual reason the pairing cannot clash.'
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:226-229 fontFamily (display/heading='Inter Tight', body='Inter') + LOCKFILE.md:210-213 (V3-D410 swap history, 'Two optical cuts of one family, no third face')

### dp-font-pairing-harmony , Font-pairing harmony: shared structural metrics, contrasting style
- Reasoning: Solen already solved this, and more robustly than the harvested principle's serif+sans framing implies. Inter Tight and Inter aren't two separately-matched typefaces, they're two optical cuts of the same superfamily, so shared x-height/stroke weight is guaranteed by construction rather than approximated by eye. RATIONALE.md explicitly names this mechanism and credits it as the reason the V3-D410 switch away from Bricolage Grotesque + Hanken Grotesk worked (Hanken 400 'read thin' against Bricolage). The harvested principle's 'Sans+Sans' bucket is exactly this pairing, already locked and already reasoned-through, not a gap.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:130 ("x-height and optical cuts... Inter Tight (display) + Inter (body) is one family in two optical cuts: harmony by construction, the same mechanism as a superfamily pairing... relabeled from imitation to mechanism")
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:226-244 (display/heading -> Inter Tight, body -> Inter, with V3-D410 comment on why); app/layout.tsx:3,13-14 (next/font loads both families, self-hosted, CSS-variable wired)

### dp-left-align-never-justify , Left-align body text, never justify (WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.8)
- Reasoning: Justify only enters a Tailwind + Inter system if someone deliberately reaches for the text-justify utility or an inline text-align:justify, and nobody has. For a compound-heavy German/French/Italian marketplace this absence is load-bearing, not incidental: justified text would make the uneven inter-word gaps worse exactly where long compounds already threaten the line (the same failure mode RATIONALE.md flags for the measure/hyphenation problem). The correct default is already the only default in use.
- Law evidence: No line cites WCAG 1.4.8 by number, but _design-system/RATIONALE.md:134 shows the system already reasons from adjacent WCAG SC numbers for typography (1.4.12 text-spacing), so this isn't an unconsidered axis, just one that never needed a rule because the failure mode never occurred.
- Code evidence: none checked in: repo-wide grep across app/, components/, and globals.css for text-align and text-justify (Tailwind's justify-* flex utilities excluded as unrelated to text alignment) returned zero hits. No component sets justified text anywhere.

### dp-font-family-vs-font , font-family Names the Typeface, Not the Font
- Reasoning: This is CSS literacy, not a design treatment a booking marketplace ships or withholds; it only becomes user-visible as a bug when an unloaded weight forces a synthesized fake bold/italic. The codebase already prevents that class of bug: every weight actually used across the type scale (400 through 900 for Inter Tight, 400 through 700 for Inter) is pre-declared in the next/font loader, self-hosted, and the loader's own comment already states the mechanic in these exact terms. There is no surface to add this principle to; it would just be re-stating something the team already got right.
- Law evidence: none dedicated (vocabulary/mechanics fact, not a taste decision), but app/layout.tsx's inline comment already states it correctly: "Inter Tight loads up to 900 so bold headings render at full weight" (V3-D190)
- Code evidence: app/layout.tsx:13-14, next/font Inter_Tight({weight:["400","500","600","700","800","900"]}) and Inter({weight:["400","500","600","700"]}); repo-wide grep for inline fontWeight overrides and for font-body combined with font-extrabold/font-black (which would request an unloaded 800/900 weight on the Inter body face) returned zero hits

### dp-extreme-size-contrast , Extreme Size Contrast for Hierarchy
- Reasoning: This is one of the most thoroughly pre-researched principles in the system: it has its own dedicated research file citing the identical 30-50 percent threshold and the identical 'reads as a rendering glitch' failure framing as the harvested claim, already folded into RATIONALE.md as a hierarchy floor, and already codified structurally in LOCKFILE (one anchor element, the name, sized to lead every card; H2 at clamp(18,20) clears the jump over 14px body). The one honest nuance is that Scale B's fine steps (11/12/14/15) are sub-30 percent by design, but those are adjacent demotions (meta vs body), not dominance pairs, and the research doc already treats that as a separate concern from the 'make ONE thing dominant' rule. Adding this principle would mean re-deriving law that is already written down with better sourcing than the prompt supplied.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:132 ("a dominance jump is 30-50 percent larger, never a 1-2px nudge (NN/g)"), sourced from _design-system/research/TASTE_TYPOGRAPHY.md findings #2-#3 and diagnostic #2 (same 30 percent number, same glitch framing)
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:56-57 (frozen scale: name 14 / meta 12 / section-H2 clamp(18px,2vw,20) / body 14 / CTA 15 / eyebrow 11; "hierarchy: name leads by SIZE; price bold-ink but smaller than name"); RATIONALE.md:125 names the hand-tuned ladder "Scale B" (11/12/14/15/18-20/24/40)

### dp-tracking-scales-inversely , Tracking Must Scale Inversely with Type Size
- Reasoning: This is not a gap, it is the literal locked system. LOCKFILE's canonical tracking set runs -0.02em at Hero H1 (40-64px) down to 0 at Body (14-16px) up to +0.08em at Eyebrow/Tag (11-12px uppercase), i.e. negative at display sizes, zero at reading sizes, positive at small caps sizes, exactly the inverse-scaling claim. RATIONALE.md even states the identical mechanism in its own words. For a Swiss booking marketplace running dense small-caps labels (Eyebrow, Tag/Status, service meta) next to a large Fresha-style Hero H1, getting this wrong would show up immediately as crowded small labels or loose oversized display type, so the fact that seven exact values are enforced by a drift gate (rule A8) rather than left to per-component judgment is the right level of rigor here, not overkill.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:321-333 (canonical tracking value set, rule A8 enforced), :221-241 (scale table), :335-337 (display-type recipe); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:333-339 (DECISION + FORCES: "tighter tracking at large display sizes optically compensates for bigger glyphs looking loose, versus looser tracking on small uppercase text compensating for cramped small caps")
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/warum-solen/page.tsx:201 tracking-[-0.02em] on a 46px display heading; app/[locale]/warum-solen/page.tsx:74, app/[locale]/dashboard/commission-admin/page.tsx:112, app/[locale]/profile/haarprofil/HaarprofilForm.tsx:19 all use tracking-[0.08em] on 12px uppercase labels, both ends of the inverse curve are live in shipped code, not just documented

### dp-skip-a-step-weight-contrast , Skip-a-Step Weight Contrast (jump ~200 units)
- Reasoning: The locked weight scale already lands on almost exactly this discipline where weight is used as a hierarchy signal at all: body is pinned at 400, every heading role at 600, an untouchable ~200-unit gap, and 800/300 are explicitly banned as 'clumsy' / 'reads thin'. Where the gap is only 100 units (CTA/nav/chip at 500 sitting between body 400 and headings 600), the system deliberately does not lean on weight for hierarchy there, LOCKFILE says outright hierarchy uses size + position + tracking, 'NOT compound family contrast', and CTAs get their prominence from an ink fill/border and position, not from out-weighing adjacent body text. So the one place hierarchy actually depends on a weight step, it already clears the JND-style threshold this principle describes; the one place it doesn't, weight was never doing that job to begin with.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:265-272 (weight scale: body 400, ALL headings 600, CTA/nav 500, 'NEVER below 500... NEVER 800/extrabold... NEVER 300/light', 'Hierarchy uses size + position + tracking, NOT compound family contrast')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/SalonCard.tsx:465,469,480 CardName bakes font-medium(500)/14px vs CardMeta font-normal(400)/13px for the small-scale pairing (weight gap intentionally small, size carries the anchor per taste rule 5); the 400-vs-600 body-to-heading pairing enforced by the Type Role Registry (LOCKFILE:287-302) is the actual load-bearing 200-unit jump used app-wide for section/page headings over body copy

### dp-standard-ligatures-fi-fl , Keep standard ligatures on (fi/fl collision)
- Reasoning: This is a request to NOT break something, not a feature to add. CSS's initial font-variant-ligatures value is 'normal', which turns on standard ('liga') and contextual ('clig') ligatures automatically in every browser, and nothing in Solen's stylesheet or Tailwind config overrides that. The exact failure mode the principle warns about (a subsetting/export step silently dropping the OpenType liga table) is also checked: Solen self-hosts Inter and Inter Tight through next/font/google with subsets:["latin"], which is glyph-coverage subsetting, not a feature-stripping export path, so the GSUB ligature table survives. For a Swiss booking product running mostly German/French copy, where 'Effizienz', 'Öffnungszeiten', 'Profil' and similar fi/fl-heavy words appear constantly in salon names, service titles and body copy, a silent ligature loss would be a real (if subtle) craft regression, but there is no evidence it has happened or that anything in the pipeline threatens it.
- Law evidence: none , _design-system/LOCKFILE.md and RATIONALE.md pin the font choice (Inter Tight / Inter / no Geist) but neither locks nor discusses ligature settings; RATIONALE.md:778 discusses variable-font axes (opsz, GRAD) without mentioning liga, consistent with it being an unremarkable default rather than an open question.
- Code evidence: grep for font-variant-ligatures / font-feature-settings ligature overrides across app/, lib/, styles/ returned zero hits (checked); app/layout.tsx:13-14 shows the font loading path (Inter_Tight({subsets:["latin"],...}), Inter({subsets:["latin"],...})) uses standard next/font/google subsetting, which preserves OpenType feature tables.

### dp-size-weight-tell-eye-what-to-read , Hierarchy: Differences in Size and Weight Tell the Eye What to Read First
- Reasoning: This is the foundational hierarchy law of the whole design system, not a gap. The frozen design contract states the read order explicitly (name leads by SIZE, price bold-ink but smaller, filler greys out) and RATIONALE.md grounds it in the same preattentive-pop-out mechanism (Ware/Bertin, T1-tier) the harvested principle is citing, plus a hard per-screen ceiling (2-3 heading levels, a dominance jump must be 30-50% larger, never a 1-2px nudge) so the technique is applied with discipline rather than vaguely. For a booking marketplace where a user scans a results grid of salon cards in under a second, this is exactly the mechanism that lets 'name' win the first glance over 'price' and 'distance' without the user reading a word , it is core to why the cards work today.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md design contract row 'hierarchy | name leads by SIZE; price bold-ink but smaller than name; rating = yellow star; filler (category·city·distance) greys out'; _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:265 'Hierarchy uses size + position + tracking , NOT compound family contrast'; _design-system/RATIONALE.md:132 'a dominance jump is 30-50 percent larger, never a 1-2px nudge (NN/g)'; RATIONALE.md:502 'section 2 grounds size-led hierarchy and the one-ink-anchor pattern (preattentive size ordering, T1)'.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/SalonCard.tsx:465 renders the name at text-[14px] via the CardName primitive (medium weight baked in), while :480 and :489/:494 render category/city/distance meta at text-[12px] font-normal text-s-ink-3 (greyed) , the exact size+weight demotion the principle describes, already shipped on the primary discovery surface.

### dp-emphasis-is-a-ratio , Emphasis Is a Ratio, Not a Style
- Reasoning: This is close to word-for-word the system's own stated law, arrived at from an owner complaint about the exact failure mode the principle predicts. LOCKFILE.md:375 records the owner rejecting a card with name+time+rating+distance all bold-ink ('using too bold... multiple times that destroys my eye') because four competing anchors leave the eye nowhere to rest, i.e. the signal collapsed toward zero exactly as the harvested principle's recall-lift curve predicts. The system's fix generalizes it into an enforced budget (max 4 sizes / 2 weights per screen, max 2 stacked emphasis treatments per text run) rather than leaving it as taste, which is a stronger, more auditable version of the same idea. For salon cards and the booking wizard specifically, restraint here is load-bearing: it is the mechanism that keeps the one ink CTA and the one bold name each readable as the single anchor they're meant to be.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:375 'The failure mode is over-emphasis: a card with the name bold-ink AND the time bold-ink AND the rating bold-ink AND the distance bold-ink has four competing anchors... Bolding everything bolds nothing'; _design-system/RATIONALE.md:342-346 'type must draw from four or fewer distinct sizes and two or fewer distinct weights... each added size or weight is another distractor class that dilutes the hierarchy signal (T1, preattentive attribute pop-out... Ware and Bertin)'; RATIONALE.md:73 caps '2 stacked emphasis treatments per text run'; CLAUDE.md taste rule 5 'emphasis (weight or colour) maps to a WHOLE meaningful unit, never an orphan sub-token'.
- Code evidence: SalonCard.tsx:465/480 (cited above) shows exactly one bold-ink anchor (name) against grey secondary text, matching the 'one anchor, not four' fix LOCKFILE.md:375 describes for this same component family.

### dp-one-primary-action-per-screen , One Primary Action Per Screen (No Competing Primaries)
- Reasoning: This is a locked, hook-enforced rule, not a gap. The design contract names it explicitly ('the ONE primary/commit CTA stays ink') and a PreToolUse gate physically blocks new code from ink-filling a second 'selected' element specifically so it can't compete with the one commit button. The blue-restraint law is the same idea from the other direction: blue is confined to small hyperlink-scope elements precisely so it never accumulates into a second competing emphasis signal alongside the one ink CTA (the LOCKFILE's own worked example is a pricing card with badge+pill+button all blue, fixed by demoting all but the one Buy button). For a booking flow where the single action that matters is 'confirm this slot' or 'pay', a second high-emphasis element would directly hurt the thing this product needs most: users completing a booking without hesitating over which button is the real one.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:16 'Primary CTAs stay bg-s-ink... Accent blue NEVER FILLS a primary action button (never a blue-filled primary, never two ink primaries)'; LOCKFILE.md:138 'The one primary commit action? → ink fill'; LOCKFILE.md:122 cites the exact 'wrong' 3-blue-element pricing card fixed by demoting all but one button to neutral, which is this principle's own worked example already adopted as law; CLAUDE.md taste rule 3 and design-contract 'selected/active' row both restate 'the ONE commit button stays ink'.
- Code evidence: .claude/hooks/no-black-selected-gate.py:1-30 is a live PreToolUse gate (built 2026-07-08 after a 62-violation audit) that blocks any new ink-filled 'selected' branch in app|components|components-legacy *.tsx, with the ONE named exception being the primary commit button , i.e. the codebase mechanically enforces that only one element per screen may carry the highest-emphasis ink treatment.

### dp-fill-vs-outline-commitment , Fill vs. Outline Encodes Commitment
- Reasoning: This is exactly the CONTROL_ELEVATION.md decision tree, already more rigorous than the harvested principle: it doesn't just say 'fill=commit, outline=optional', it enumerates four tiers (ink-fill for the one primary, neutral outline for a secondary action, flat text/bordered-icon for calm/quiet controls, frosted glass over photos) and hard-bans the failure mode the principle warns about ('never two ink primaries', LOCKFILE line 16). For a booking marketplace, this actually matters more than for a generic app: the one thing users must never confuse is 'this books/pays' vs 'this cancels/defers', so the ink-fill is reserved with a one-per-region cap rather than left to vibes. Real code already does exactly what the principle describes on three independent surfaces (customer cookie consent, onboarding wizard nav, dashboard Button primitive), so codifying it again would duplicate an existing, stricter law rather than close a gap.
- Law evidence: _design-system/CONTROL_ELEVATION.md:28-52 (Q1 ink-fill primary / Q1.5 neutral-outline secondary per LOCKFILE §1.5 v3 / Q3 flat calm); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:16 ('Primary CTAs stay bg-s-ink... never two ink primaries')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/CookieConsent.tsx:318-345 ('Nur notwendige' = bg-white border-s-border outline vs 'Alle akzeptieren' = bg-s-ink filled); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Modal.tsx:102-105 (documented convention: variant="secondary" outline then variant="primary" filled); app/[locale]/_components/dashboard/DashboardUI.tsx:211-235 (Button variant primary=filled / secondary=bordered-white / ghost=text-only); app/[locale]/onboarding/salon/page.tsx:707-720 (Zurück = outline, Weiter/Fertig = bg-s-ink filled)

### dp-placement-is-information , Placement Is Information: Put the Commit Action at the Flow's End
- Reasoning: The system already treats DOM/reading-order position as part of the commit signal, not just color: ModalFooter defaults to justify-end (right-aligned, i.e. the terminus of an LTR flow) and its own 'between' layout explicitly documents 'destructive tertiary on far left, primary group on right'. Real booking-adjacent flows follow the same rule without anyone having to invoke a named principle: the onboarding wizard puts Zurück first (flex-none, left) and Weiter/Fertig last (flex-1, dominant, rightmost). For a Swiss booking marketplace where a mis-tap on a commit vs. cancel action has real money/time consequences (a paid walk-in slot, a booking confirmation), this end-of-flow convention is load-bearing and already enforced structurally through the shared ModalFooter primitive rather than per-screen judgment calls.
- Law evidence: _design-system/CONTROL_ELEVATION.md:28 (Q1: ink-fill is 'the destination', reinforcing that placement + fill jointly encode the commit path); no explicit RATIONALE.md/TASTE_LOG.md entry naming 'placement' independently, the convention is embedded in the shared primitive instead
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Modal.tsx:265-284 (ModalFooter justify="between" comment: 'destructive tertiary on far left, primary group on right'; default justify-end); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/CookieConsent.tsx:305-345 (Anpassen, then Nur notwendige, then Alle akzeptieren last/rightmost); app/[locale]/onboarding/salon/page.tsx:703-727 (nav row: back button first, flex-1 Weiter/Fertig last)

### dp-engineered-not-intuitive-navigation , Engineered Navigation, Not Intuitive Navigation
- Reasoning: The only surface in the Solen product that HAS a bottom nav is the native iOS app (solen-mobile), and it satisfies all four constraints structurally rather than by a manual checklist: tab count is capped at 5 (owner-decided, D1, down from an original 4), every tab carries a mandatory <Label>, and it is built on Apple's own NativeTabs/UITabBarController, which makes 'two active tabs at once' or 'an action masquerading as a tab' physically unrepresentable, not just discouraged. On the web side, a bottom nav was built, then explicitly killed twice (BottomTabBar removed 2026-05-03, dashboard bottom-dock rejected 2026-07-15) because a second nav-shaped bar read as clutter next to the existing header/sidebar nav. That owner history is itself evidence this exact question was already litigated with a firmer, platform-native answer than a checklist would produce; writing a new checklist doc would sit downstream of a decision that's already settled and already stricter.
- Law evidence: _design-system/REMOVED.md:48 (bottom nav tab bar graveyarded on the map mockup, 2026-07-02); _design-system/REMOVED.md:78 (dashboard bottom action dock rejected, 2026-07-15, 'now we have two navigation... just clutter'); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:285 (Round D1: 'Second nav | NEVER a bottom action dock or any second nav-shaped bar')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/layout.tsx:122-128 (BottomTabBar removed from web rendering per Q58, component preserved but not mounted); /Users/sulo/Documents/solen-mobile/src/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:1-37 (NativeTabs, 5 destinations, mandatory Label per trigger, native UITabBarController); /Users/sulo/Documents/solen-mobile/_design-system/PLAN.md:77-80 (D1 owner decision: tab count locked, 'Termine tab REMOVED... implemented as native UITabBarController')

### dp-3-5-tab-ceiling , 3-5 Tab Ceiling (Primary Bottom Navigation)
- Reasoning: Mobile's tab bar sits exactly at 5, the top of the range the principle names, and that number was an explicit owner tradeoff, not a default: it started at 4 (Home/Suche/Entdecken/Profil, D1, 2026-06-12) and Karte was added later, so the ceiling was actively weighed rather than accreted feature-by-feature. Web deliberately carries zero bottom-nav tabs (see REMOVED.md), so there's no runaway tab count to cap there either. Re-adding this as a new rule would restate a number the team already hit and already justified with a named owner decision, for a marketplace where the tab bar's job (home, search, discovery, map, profile) is narrow enough that 5 was a natural ceiling, not a warning sign.
- Law evidence: /Users/sulo/Documents/solen-mobile/_design-system/PLAN.md:77-80 (D1, owner, 2026-06-12: '4 tabs - Home / Suche / Entdecken / Profil')
- Code evidence: /Users/sulo/Documents/solen-mobile/src/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:11-34 (exactly 5 NativeTabs.Trigger entries: index, suche, entdecken, karte, profil)

### dp-single-active-state , Single Active State: Exactly One Nav Tab Active
- Reasoning: This constraint is satisfied at the platform level, not the app level: NativeTabs renders through expo-router's native binding to UITabBarController, and iOS's own tab-bar controller enforces single-selection (radio) semantics as an OS primitive, there is no independent per-tab boolean state in the app code that could ever desync into two lit tabs. That's a stronger guarantee than a component-level rule could give (a hand-rolled web tab bar CAN drift into two active states if two flags are set independently; a native UITabBarController cannot). Nothing to add: the current implementation already can't violate this, by construction, and web has no bottom-nav tabs to violate it with either.
- Law evidence: none (constraint is enforced by the OS/platform component, not a Solen design doc)
- Code evidence: /Users/sulo/Documents/solen-mobile/src/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:10 (single <NativeTabs> with tintColor, no per-tab active-state prop anywhere; selection is derived from route by expo-router's native binding, never from independent flags)

### dp-consistent-card-anatomy , Consistent Card Anatomy for Scannable Lists
- Reasoning: A booking marketplace lives or dies on fast comparison across many salon/service cards, so a fixed scan path is core, not decorative, and it is already built and enforced rather than missing. LOCKFILE's own 'card grammar' recipe already codifies the exact discipline the principle describes (group, rank by fixed scan order, de-label, one icon row, one differentiated anchor), and the real card component enforces it with the same truncate/slice(0,3) pattern repeated across every size variant.
- Law evidence: LOCKFILE.md:1302-1314 (Section 14.1 'Card grammar, the 5-step recipe': group related facts, rank by scan order, de-label, icon the details row, one differentiated element); CLAUDE.md design contract 'wrap' row: 'name truncate, meta truncate, title wrap, body line-clamp, price/rating nowrap'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/search/SalonResultCard.tsx:284,331,398 (CardName truncate at the same slot across feed/list/compact card variants); :227 featuredSlots.slice(0,3); :468 services.filter(...).slice(0,3) (secondary rows capped identically everywhere)

### dp-reading-gravity-vs-thirds , Reading Gravity Beats Rule-of-Thirds for Hierarchy
- Reasoning: Solen already refuses rule-of-thirds as a justification, grounds hierarchy in real eyetracking research instead of a decorative grid, and ranks card content top-left-first by law, which is exactly the reading-gravity claim. The principle's own illustrated surface, a 2D full-bleed hero banner with the headline in one corner and the CTA/price in the opposite corner, is the one composition type the owner explicitly killed twice, so even the demo case has no live surface to attach to.
- Law evidence: RATIONALE.md:250 (folklore table: rule of thirds is a 'composition convention, weak perceptual evidence... use as CONV heuristic, never as justification'); RATIONALE.md:73 and research/TASTE_HIERARCHY.md:10-12 (T1 NN/g eyetracking: F-pattern is the failure mode, layer-cake is the target, already the adopted grounding); LOCKFILE.md:1309 (Section 14.1 step 2: 'Rank the groups, the user's scan order, most important top-left'); TASTE_LOG.md:265 (2026-07-16: full-bleed hero/banner treatments DENIED and disbanded, 'i want it gone')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/search/SalonResultCard.tsx (CardName always leads top-left of every card; price/rating trail bottom-right nowrap, matching the LOCKFILE hierarchy row 'name leads by SIZE... filler greys out')

### dp-single-focal-point-anchor , Single Focal Point / Anchor Element
- Reasoning: This is close to a verbatim restatement of the house's own dated root-cause finding for its worst clutter complaint: the dashboard redesign diagnosed 'equal visual weight, every block wearing the same card costume with no dominant focal object' as the actual problem, and the owner-approved fix (one hero, everything else bare text) is the single-focal-point principle already shipped as locked law, not a gap.
- Law evidence: TASTE_LOG.md:294,298-302 (Dashboard Round D1 root-cause note, 2026-07-14/15: 'Cluttered was never element count: it was EQUAL VISUAL WEIGHT... one hero in a card, everything else quiet bare text, and the hero is the thing the owner must act on'); TASTE_LOG.md:62 (card price weight rule: 'Bold ink number, name kept LARGER as the anchor', amends rule A13); RATIONALE.md:58-59 (preattentive attributes T1: 'one-ink-anchor card rule... bolding everything bolds nothing is preattentive math, not just taste')
- Code evidence: CLAUDE.md taste rule 5 (V3-D442: 'A card may carry two ink elements only if the NAME is larger, so size, not colour, marks the anchor'); design contract 'hierarchy' row: 'name leads by SIZE; price bold-ink but smaller than name'

### dp-manuscript-grid , Manuscript Grid: One Undivided Column for Uninterrupted Reading
- Reasoning: Solen already runs every start-to-finish reading surface as a single undivided column at max-w-2xl (672px), which sits inside the house measure CONV (45-75ch, Bringhurst) and the owner's 68ch cap adopted 2026-07-15. This is the default page shell for exactly the content class the principle targets (about, careers, contact, blog stub, legal notice), not a gap to fill. The one long-form legal document (terms/AGB) deliberately pairs the reading column with a persistent TOC sidebar; for a 15-section bilingual T&Cs doc, customers jump to a specific clause (cancellation, liability) rather than reading start to finish, so a scan-and-jump aid is the correct call, not a violation of the manuscript-grid premise, whose 'no side blocks' claim assumes linear reading intent that doesn't hold for that surface. There is nothing net-new to add: applying this principle literally would mean stripping the terms sidebar, which would make the one long document Solen has strictly worse to use.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:128 (measure CONV, 45-75ch/66 ideal, Bringhurst), :658 (68ch cap owner-adopted 2026-07-15), :534 (measure grounds the FAQ/search-bar text-column caps), :788 (Muller-Brockmann Swiss grid lineage named as adopted VALUES); LOCKFILE.md:822 (FAQ section max-w-[820px])
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/ueber-uns/page.tsx:15, app/[locale]/blog/page.tsx:15, app/[locale]/kontakt/page.tsx:15, app/[locale]/karriere/page.tsx:13, app/[locale]/impressum/page.tsx:10 ,  all `max-w-2xl mx-auto` single column, no side blocks. Counter-case: app/[locale]/terms/page.tsx:53-63 pairs TermsContent with TermsSidebar.tsx (sticky TOC nav) deliberately, for the one long structured legal doc where scan-to-clause beats linear read.

### dp-column-grid , Column Grid
- Reasoning: This is the standard browsing/search grid Solen already runs everywhere multiple independent, same-schema items need to be scanned side by side: search results, the salon-card carousel, the team carousel. The design system already carries a full DECISION/FORCES/OPTIMIZES/SACRIFICES/MECHANIC entry for this exact pattern, grounding the gutter values in Gestalt proximity (gap-as-grouping T1) and the gap sizes in the 4pt grid CONV. Nothing here is a genuine gap for a Swiss booking marketplace that already treats result grids as first-class Fresha-structure surfaces; re-proposing it would duplicate a documented, tuned system.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:840-848 (grid pattern recipes: carousel gap-3/gap-5, search result grid-cols-2/3/4, bento grid-cols-1/2/2); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:545-550 (full DECISION block for exactly this pattern, MECHANIC = Gestalt gap-as-grouping T1 + 4pt grid CONV)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/[city]/[category]/page.tsx (search result grid, grid-cols-2/3/4 per LOCKFILE:843); app/[locale]/warum-solen/page.tsx:224,258,330 (grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-12); app/[locale]/partner/page.tsx:140 and app/[locale]/fuer-salons/page.tsx:328 (grid-cols-2 md:grid-cols-3 gap-4)

### dp-modular-grid , Modular Grid: Flowlines Turn Columns Into a Fill/Skip/Span Matrix
- Reasoning: The exact fill/skip/span mechanic is live on the single highest-traffic surface in the product: the salon PDP hero photo gallery, a 3-column x 2-row matrix where the lead photo spans 2x2 and two supporting photos fill single cells. A second, independent instance runs on /business (BentoBusiness), where a 3-column matrix has one card spanning 2 columns in row 1 and a different card spanning 2 columns in row 2. Both are shipped, both are documented with explicit row/column comments in the source. Adding a formal 'modular grid' concept would just relabel a pattern already proven at scale in two places; the marginal value is near zero and the duplication risk (a parallel grid abstraction competing with these two working recipes) is real.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1121 (Magazine grid pattern registry entry: '3-col 16:9 thumbnails + one feature card 2x others', names the same fill/skip/span mechanic as law, targeted at /entdecken mode-2, not yet built there)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonHero.tsx:225 (`grid grid-cols-3 grid-rows-2 gap-2`), :229 (`col-span-2 row-span-2` on the lead photo) ,  live on every salon PDP; app/[locale]/_components/homepage/BentoBusiness.tsx:686 (`grid grid-cols-1 gap-4 md:grid-cols-3`), :691 and :707 (`md:col-span-2` on Booking/Chart cards) ,  live on /business

### dp-hierarchical-grid , Hierarchical Grid
- Reasoning: This is the same evidence as the modular grid, viewed from the outcome side: the SalonHero gallery gives the lead photo 4x the area of each supporting photo (2x2 vs 1x1 in a 3x2 matrix) specifically so the eye ranks it first, on every salon page in the marketplace, and the design system's own imagery pattern registry names the identical move ('one feature card 2x others') as law for the Inspo magazine-grid mode. A booking marketplace where the PDP photo is the single highest-leverage trust signal (first-100ms trust window, per PSYCHOLOGY.md) already spends its hierarchy budget correctly there; there is no PDP-level or homepage-level gap this principle would close that isn't already closed.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1121 (feature card 2x others, magazine grid pattern registry)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonHero.tsx:21 ('Desktop: 3-photo gallery in a 2-col grid (1 large left 2/3 + 2 small right 1/3 stacked)'), :223-229 (implementation, col-span-2 row-span-2 on the lead photo) ,  shipped on every salon PDP hero

### dp-alignment-one-shared-axis , Alignment: One Shared Axis Per Group
- Reasoning: Solen's component system enforces this structurally rather than as a named rule: every card (SalonCard, booking rows, dashboard stat tiles) stacks text in one flex-col block behind one consistent padding value, so name/rating, category, and address share a left edge by construction, not by a bespoke alignment pass. The system already names the harder case explicitly (tabular numeric data, where alignment is not free): RATIONALE.md's table rule requires right-aligned tabular figures 'so place value stacks' and left-aligned text. Card stacks get the same result for free from the 4pt spacing/padding conventions. Formalizing 'one shared axis' as a new standalone rule would not change any real surface's output; a violation would already read as broken against p-4/flex-col defaults and would surface in drift review.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:53 (Gestalt grouping: continuity/alignment listed as a grouping cue) + RATIONALE.md:766 (tables: numbers right-aligned tabular so place value stacks, text left-aligned) + _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:446-450 (spacing rhythm / consistent card padding tiers)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/SalonCard.tsx (name / category label / address stacked in one flex-col card body sharing a single left padding edge) and app/[locale]/_components/homepage/MobileCategoriesRow.tsx:90-119 (icon + label centered on one shared vertical axis inside each tile)

### dp-one-separator-not-both , One structural separator, not both
- Reasoning: This is one of the most explicitly litigated rules in the whole system. It is already CLAUDE.md's own taste rule 2 ('existing contrast IS the separator, do not add a middot too') and has a dedicated divider decision ladder: gap-only by default, a hairline only inside dense receipt-style clusters, 'a divider next to generous padding = delete the divider,' and an elevated card must drop its hairline entirely because '1px-border + flat = the dated tell' (one depth cue, never two). TASTE_GROUPING.md (researched 2026-07-15) extends this into a full decision tree for peers vs facets. Re-adding this principle would restate law that is already written down and already enforced on every card/list surface.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1316-1322 (Sec 14.2 divider decision rule, DS-8) + LOCKFILE.md:509,523 (a resting card gets a quiet lift OR a hairline, never both; drop border-s-border on any card carrying elevation) + _design-system/RATIONALE.md:415-419 and 597-602 (divider decision rule, one depth cue per card) + RATIONALE.md:151 pointing to research/TASTE_GROUPING.md's decision tree
- Code evidence: none checked in: this is a documented decision rule, not a single component; enforcement point is _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:518-522 ('the dated tell') applied wherever ProductCard-style components choose shadow vs hairline

### dp-chunk-list-into-icon-card-grid , Chunk identical list rows into an icon-labeled card grid
- Reasoning: The exact move (identical rows to icon-labeled bounded cards for parallel scanning) is already shipped on the homepage and prototyped again for a category chooser. For a booking marketplace, category/service pickers are the canonical case this principle targets (Coiffeur / Barber / Nails / Spa / Walk-in / Karte), and Solen already made the serial-list-to-parallel-grid call there, including a selected-state affordance (ink-ring + check badge) that a plain radio list would need to invent from scratch.
- Law evidence: none blocking; not named as a standalone law, but consistent with RATIONALE.md's pop-out / preattentive-attribute reasoning (section 2) that already grounds why one bold anchor beats many, applied here to icon+card chunking instead of text weight
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/MobileCategoriesRow.tsx:77-123 (3-col grid of icon-labeled, rounded cards with a selected ink-ring + check badge, replacing what would otherwise be a plain category list) and app/[locale]/dev/category-flow/page.tsx:94-103 (Model C: 2-col icon-labeled card grid prototyped for a search-intent chooser)

### dp-nested-corner-radius-formula , Nested corner-radius formula
- Reasoning: This is the exact rule already locked and enforced. A booking marketplace has cards-inside-cards everywhere (service rows inside a grouped card, review panels inside a profile card, a CTA button inside a pricing/plan card), so the concentric-curve requirement is a real, recurring need here, not a nice-to-have, and it was already researched and adopted.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:428-433 (DS-4, 'Nested radius formula', owner-approved 2026-06-11): 'inner radius = outer radius - gap... min 4px... pills exempt.' _design-system/RATIONALE.md:147: 'Nested radius: LOCKED (inner = outer minus gap, min 4px, DS-4)... Nothing to relitigate.'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonServices.tsx:139,147 and app/[locale]/dashboard/bundles/page.tsx:337 show the outer `rounded-[24px]` grouped list card with square/near-square inner rows separated by `border-t`, the degenerate case of the formula when the inner gap is small.

### dp-8pt-grid , The 8-Point Grid (8pt Spacing System)
- Reasoning: Solen already runs a base-unit spacing grid; it just picked 4 as the base instead of 8, explicitly to buy denser mobile rhythm on a mobile-first Swiss booking flow, and then locked three named rhythm tiers (32/12/16, all multiples of 4) on top of it so the base unit alone doesn't leave every spacing value as a free choice. The principle's own example text already allows '4px for finer control,' which is precisely what's shipped.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:140 ('4pt grid (CONV). Solen is 4pt-based (SOURCE.md:369)... Our choice of 4 over 8 buys denser mobile rhythm'). LOCKFILE.md:442-453 (spacing rhythm DS-5: Section 32px / Card 12px / Group 16px, 'Drift signal: any mt-5/mt-6/mt-7... between sections or cards'). Project CLAUDE.md design contract row: 'spacing | 4-pt scale only.' _design-system/research/TASTE_DASHBOARDS.md:3 lists '8pt grid' among mechanics 'already covered in RATIONALE.md.'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonServices.tsx:139,214 uses `mt-3` for the card-tier gap; tailwind.config.js leaves Tailwind's stock 4px-increment spacing scale untouched, which is what every `gap-*`/`p-*`/`mt-*` utility in the codebase draws from.

### dp-space-as-rank-signal , Space as a rank signal (active vs. passive white space)
- Reasoning: The exact mechanic this principle describes (space only signals rank when the ratio is deliberate and unequal; uniform space is inert) is the stated grounding for Solen's locked spacing-rhythm system: the 32/12/16 tiers exist specifically because 'space within a group must read as smaller than space between groups' via Gestalt proximity, and that same proximity math is separately cited as the reason no extra divider glyph is needed where spacing/contrast already separates things. It's a load-bearing, cited mechanic here, not a gap.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:53-54 (Gestalt grouping, T1, Wertheimer/Koffka): 'Proximity is the strongest grouping signal: whitespace ENCODES relationship... space within a group < space between groups is why the three tiers exist.' RATIONALE.md:142: 'Macro whitespace signals structure and calm... Proximity math from domain 1 is the operative rule.' RATIONALE.md:380,550,602 re-apply the same proximity mechanic to grid gaps and the middot-separator ban. _design-system/research/TASTE_HIERARCHY.md:22 separately names 'isolation via negative space increases prominence' as one of the four contrast categories used to diagnose hierarchy.
- Code evidence: LOCKFILE.md:446-461 spacing-rhythm tiers (32/12/16) are implemented via `mt-8`/`space-y-8` (section), `gap-3`/`mt-3` (card), `mt-4` (group) across components, e.g. app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonServices.tsx:139 (`mt-3` between sibling service groups).

### dp-4pt-vs-8pt-two-roles , One spacing scale, two roles: 4pt for precision, 8pt for rhythm
- Reasoning: Solen already runs exactly this two-role split, just tuned by hand instead of forced onto a rigid 8pt grid. RATIONALE.md is explicit that the base grid is 4pt (not 8pt) precisely because a booking marketplace needs denser mobile rhythm than the 8pt tradition allows, and that the resulting 'weaker constraint' (more permissible values) is deliberately clawed back at the macro-layout level by a separate three-tier rhythm scale (32 Section / 12 Card / 16 Group), which is called out as playing the exact role an 8pt macro grid would play. LOCKFILE even states outright that '8pt pedantry above ~100px buys nothing' once you're past small internal gaps, i.e. the team already evaluated and rejected literal 8pt multiples as the macro role and replaced it with a hand-tuned, owner-approved (2026-06-11, DS-5) scale. So the underlying idea (fine-grain scale for internal spacing, a distinct coarser scale for section rhythm, one token system doing two jobs) is live, documented, and enforced (drift signal: any mt-5/mt-6/mt-7 between sections). Re-adding it as a literal 4-vs-8 rule would actually be a regression: it would reopen a value the owner explicitly tuned away from strict 8pt increments (e.g. the Card tier is 12, not 8 or 16).
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:138-140 (4pt grid CONV, explains why 4 over 8 and how 32/12/16 restores the macro constraint); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:410-453 (§3 Spacing rhythm, DS-5, three locked tiers Section 32px / Card 12px / Group 16px, '8pt pedantry above ~100px buys nothing')
- Code evidence: Tier values are enforced sitewide via mt-8/space-y-8 (Section), gap-3/mt-3 (Card), mt-4 (Group) per LOCKFILE:442-450; drift-checker flags any mt-5/mt-6/mt-7, confirming the two-role split is live, not aspirational

### dp-progress-bar-track-thickness , Progress bar track thickness: 4 to 8px
- Reasoning: Solen's actual linear progress bars already sit in or near this range as a de-facto (unwritten) convention, not a coincidence: three of four live instances are 5-6px thin flat pill tracks. This is consistent with LOCKFILE's general elevation/depth restraint (thin, calm, never competing with content) and the one-progress-indicator-per-screen rule that already treats progress UI as a quiet, secondary signal rather than a focal element. Nothing in the harvested principle's stated mechanism ('thin enough to avoid competing with text/icons, thick enough to read as a bar') is new information for this codebase; it's just naming what the shipped, owner-approved surfaces already do. The one outlier (RewardsView loyalty bar at 10px) is a minor consolidation opportunity, not evidence of a missing principle, and formalizing an exact px lock for a component class this small (4 instances, no single shared primitive) is not worth a new LOCKFILE rule versus just nudging the outlier back toward the 5-6px family in a normal edit.
- Law evidence: No explicit LOCKFILE/RATIONALE rule pins linear progress-track thickness to a number; the closest law is LOCKFILE.md:1246 ('One progress indicator per screen') and the general depth-restraint ethos in §1 (whitespace + restraint carry every screen)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/onboarding/OnboardingFlow.tsx:151 (h-[5px] track); app/[locale]/booking/resend-link/page.tsx:547 (h-[5px] track); app/[locale]/queue/[token]/page.tsx:413 (h-1.5 = 6px track); app/[locale]/onboarding/salon/page.tsx:645 (h-1.5 = 6px segments); app/[locale]/rewards/RewardsView.tsx:144 (h-2.5 = 10px, the one outlier above the range)

### dp-whitespace-directional-channel , Whitespace as a Directional Channel
- Reasoning: This is close to the single most load-bearing idea already codified in Solen's design law, just under different names. RATIONALE.md grounds it directly in Gestalt proximity (whitespace ENCODES relationship, T1) and separately states that macro whitespace signals structure and calm while micro whitespace drives legibility; the squint-test diagnostic vocabulary explicitly treats negative space as SHAPE and scores a page on dominance/focal point and the eye's path. Beyond the theory, the practice is enforced as multiple named 'exactly one' restraints that all exist to keep whitespace pointed at a single target instead of getting filled with competing elements: one flagship hero moment, one ink-anchor per card, one progress indicator per screen, one primary/commit CTA per screen. Taste rule 2 in the project's own CLAUDE.md (no decorative artifacts, every element carries information or it gets deleted) is the enforcement mechanism for exactly the failure mode the principle describes (filling whitespace with secondary elements scatters the reading path). Nothing here is a gap; it is the connective tissue between several already-shipped rules that just hadn't been named 'directional channel' before.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:53 (Gestalt proximity T1, whitespace encodes relationship); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:142 (macro whitespace signals structure/calm, Chaparro 2004 cited correctly, the fabricated 'Lin 2004 +20%' explicitly banned); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:784 (squint-test vocabulary: dominance/focal point, movement/eye path, negative space as shape); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:642 (named parallel: one-ink-anchor card rule, one-progress-indicator rule, 'exactly one flagship moment'); CLAUDE.md taste rule 2 (no decorative artifacts, every element must carry information)
- Code evidence: Enforced structurally wherever the 'one X per screen' rules apply, e.g. LOCKFILE.md:1246 (one progress indicator per screen) and the SuccessMark 'one delight peak per screen' rule (LOCKFILE §13.3); no code changes needed to demonstrate this, it is a diagnostic/compositional law already run by the solen-taste-diagnosis skill against real screens

### dp-optical-padding-2x-horizontal , Optical padding: horizontal runs ~2x vertical on small components
- Reasoning: This is not a gap, it is a dated, numbered lock. Solen's own DS-2 rule already states the exact 2:1 ratio (12px/24px) for the same reason the harvested principle gives: a text-hugging pill reads as enclosure faster vertically than horizontally, so equal padding looks cramped top/bottom or bloated left/right. For a booking marketplace where buttons/chips/pills carry most of the tap targets (service chips, filter pills, CTA buttons), this ratio is already the shipped default, not a proposal.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:439 'Button padding ratio (DS-2 detail): standalone buttons aim for horizontal padding ≈ 2× vertical (e.g. 12px/24px). Full-width CTAs are exempt (height-driven, 52-54px).'
- Code evidence: px-6 py-3 (24px/12px, exact 2:1) used across dozens of real buttons: app/not-found.tsx:15,21; app/[locale]/business/page.tsx:204,211; app/[locale]/checkout/page.tsx:374 (px-6 py-3.5); app/[locale]/profile/referral/page.tsx:58; app/[locale]/_components/homepage/WhySolen.tsx:129; app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonServicesSheet.tsx:350

### dp-near-black-over-pure-black , Near-Black Over Pure Black for Large Screen Surfaces
- Reasoning: Already locked, already evidenced with citations, already the shipped default. Solen's primary ink token is #0A0A0A, not #000000, for exactly the eye-strain/sustained-viewing reason the principle gives, and the codebase even names pure black as forbidden. The only places #000000-adjacent (bg-black) appears are brief hover states on small buttons, which is precisely the 'reserve true black for brief high-impact moments' carve-out the principle itself allows, not a violation.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:47 'black | #000000 | NEVER use (eye strain). Use s-ink instead.' _design-system/RATIONALE.md:714 cites Buchner 2007 / Piepenbrock 2013-2014 (positive-polarity reading advantage) and Hashemi 2018 (halation worst for astigmatism, ~40% adult prevalence) to justify charcoal-not-pure-black on sustained surfaces.
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:131 's-ink: { DEFAULT: "#0A0A0A", ... }' with an inline comment at the token's history block: 'never use pure black (#000000) ,  causes eye strain.' bg-black only appears as a :hover state on small pill/button CTAs, e.g. app/[locale]/dashboard/page.tsx:170 'bg-s-ink ... hover:bg-black', never as a resting large-surface fill

### dp-greyscale-test , The Greyscale Test: build hierarchy on tone, not hue
- Reasoning: The underlying claim, that hierarchy should survive on tone/weight/size rather than leaning on hue, is not a technique Solen could adopt, it is the literal premise of the whole B&W pivot. Selected/active states, tab pills, CTA hierarchy, and grouping are all defined by grey fill + weight + size, with blue deliberately excluded from structural roles. A screen desaturated to greyscale today would still show every affordance because color was never load-bearing for structure in the first place; that is a locked, owner-dated decision (2026-06-29), not an open question. The specific verification ritual (run a screenshot through a desaturation filter) is a QA technique, not a visual treatment, so there is no real surface to mock a before/after on, it would just confirm what the selected-state and CVD-redundancy law already guarantees.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md pinned taste rule 3: 'Clickability on structure is signalled by AFFORDANCE (chevron / underline-on-hover / weight / icon), not colour.' Rule 5: 'emphasis... maps to a WHOLE meaningful unit... so size, not colour, marks the anchor.' _design-system/LOCKFILE.md selected/active row: 'calm GRAY fill... NEVER black/ink fill on a selected state... NEVER blue' and _design-system/REMOVED.md:41 (owner voice 2026-06-29: 'never black when selected; wants calm GRAY... reuse the existing TabPill gray treatment everywhere'). _design-system/RATIONALE.md:83-85 CVD math is the floor for 'color never the sole carrier' (WCAG 1.4.1), already practiced as icon+text+color redundancy.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/TabPill.tsx:67 selected className 'border-s-border bg-s-bg-sunken text-s-ink' (gray fill, no hue shift) and line 43 'font-semibold' shared base, i.e. selection is legible purely on tone/weight; same pattern reused across dashboard/calendar, SalonServices, SalonServicesSheet per grep hits on TabPill usage

### dp-semantic-color-tokens-indirection , Semantic Color Tokens as an Indirection Layer Over Primitives
- Reasoning: This is not a gap, it is the load-bearing mechanism the entire palette runs on. Every color in the product is consumed as a semantic Tailwind token (bg-s-ink, text-s-accent, bg-s-success) and SOURCE.md orders 'always reference tokens via Tailwind classes, never inline hex.' The codebase has repeatedly exercised the exact retheme-in-one-place benefit the principle describes: s-coral was repointed from green to ink to blue across its history, and V3-D332 explicitly restored broken rendering 'site-wide WITHOUT touching 1043 callsites' by repointing seven retired-token aliases. For a marketplace mid-pivot between palette eras (V2 earthen wellness to V3 B&W to a vibrant dashboard skin), this indirection is what made three successive rebrands survivable without a component-by-component hunt.
- Law evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md section 2 ('Always reference tokens via Tailwind classes ... never inline hex')
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:1-210 (s-ink/s-accent/s-success/s-error/etc. token definitions with alias-repoint comments, e.g. s-coral history green#3B7A57 -> ink#0A0A0A -> retired); 105 files under app/components consume text-s-accent/bg-s-accent by class name (grep count); app/[locale]/privacy/page.tsx:19 and 4 sibling files carry live 'retired s-coral -> s-accent' repoint comments

### dp-contrast-is-value-not-hue , Contrast: value difference, not hue, makes an element visible
- Reasoning: The system's actual contrast law is WCAG's luminance ratio, which is a value/lightness measure operating on linearized RGB, hue is not a term in the formula. RATIONALE.md goes further than the harvested principle by naming its practical consequence for this product: the riskiest pair on the site is success-green vs error-red, two hues that can sit at similar perceptual value, which is exactly the failure mode the principle describes (matching value, different hue, no clean edge for color-blind users). Solen's answer is not 'pick better hues' but 'never let hue alone carry the signal', every status chip pairs color with an icon and text. That is a more rigorous, WCAG-grounded version of the harvested claim, already codified and already shipped.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md section 4 ('WCAG 2.x ratio, T1, normative standard: (L1+0.05)/(L2+0.05) on linearized sRGB luminance') and section 3 ('highest-risk pair is exactly red-green ... WCAG 1.4.1: color never the sole carrier')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/staff-invite/page.tsx:92, app/[locale]/auth/reset-password/page.tsx:78, app/[locale]/booking/lookup/page.tsx:604 (Check icon + text-s-success color together, never color alone, on every success state)

### dp-three-tier-saturation , Three-tier saturation hierarchy: split the palette by role
- Reasoning: Solen already assigns saturation by functional role rather than taste: the neutral scale (s-ink #0A0A0A, s-bg.sunken #F4F4F5, s-border #E4E4E7) sits at effectively 0 percent saturation, semantic/UI signal colors are locked into a named band (SOURCE.md's saturation contract: DEFAULT L 36-60%, S 65-92%), and pastel surfaces get their own low-saturation band (L 93-96%). The one place true 'vibrant, near-full-saturation, used everywhere' color exists is the operator dashboard, which LOCKFILE deliberately quarantines from the customer-facing 80/17 B&W system precisely because full saturation is a distinct register, not a default. The exact percentage bands in the harvested principle do not match 1:1, but the underlying claim, saturation is a role-keyed system property, not an aesthetic knob, is already the enforced law with a formal contract and worked before/after examples.
- Law evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md 'The saturation contract (V3-D199, 2026-05-26)' with explicit L/S ranges per role, and LOCKFILE.md section 12.2 'Vibrant palette (full saturation, consistent with accent blue)' scoped exclusively to /dashboard
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js: s-ink/s-bg tokens near 0% saturation vs s-success #16A34A (HSL 142,76%,36%) and s-accent #276EF1 (HSL 215,88%,55%) inside the locked 65-92% signal band; LOCKFILE.md section 12.4 restricts the vibrant full-saturation palette to app/[locale]/dashboard/** via drift-checker exemption

### dp-hsl-lightness-not-brightness , HSL Lightness Is Not a Brightness Measurement
- Reasoning: This is not a gap, it is already the codified palette-generation policy. RATIONALE.md domain 2 states the identical mechanic almost verbatim: HSL-L is an RGB coordinate artifact, hues at equal HSL-L differ wildly in perceived brightness, and OKLCH is designated as the space any future multi-hue extension (category colors, data viz, dark mode) must derive in before converting to hex. For a near-monochrome B&W product with one accent blue, this matters less today than it will the moment a second hue class ships (chart series, service-category tags), and the law already anticipates that moment rather than leaving it to be rediscovered.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:79-81 (T1 domain-2-color entry, explicit HSL-L-is-an-artifact claim + OKLCH mandate for future multi-hue derivation) and :99-100 in LOCKFILE.md (chart-series hue stepping already fixes L+C and steps only H, the OKLCH pattern in practice)
- Code evidence: no oklch/OKLab hits anywhere in the codebase (grep across .ts/.tsx/.js, excluding node_modules and _pixel-refs came back empty); tailwind.config.js:70-210 defines every token as a literal hex, consistent with RATIONALE.md's own admission that HSL/hex stays 'legacy documentation, not the generator' until a second hue class is needed

### dp-limited-high-contrast-palette , Limited, High-Contrast Palette
- Reasoning: This is the single most load-bearing law in the entire design system, not a harvestable addition. The B&W pivot (80% neutral surfaces, 17% ink, blue reserved to roughly 3% of small clickable pixels) is exactly the 'fewer colors, large value gap' claim, and it is enforced by a PreToolUse gate, not just documented. Solen's own rationale even names the folk 60-30-10 rule as the same claim-shape and deliberately relabels it as an internal CONV budget rather than borrowed science, which is a more rigorous version of the harvested principle, not a missing one.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md taste rule 3 ('80/17 surfaces+ink; blue is SPARSE'), _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:126-132 ('Cool neutrals + whitespace... the clean B&W base; whitespace + restraint carry every screen with zero accent'), _design-design-system/RATIONALE.md:89 ('The 80/17 budget... same claim-shape as the 60-30-10 folk rule... OUR dated budget decision')
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:70-210 (near-monochrome token set: s-ink/s-bg/s-border/s-bg-sunken dominate, s-accent #276EF1 is the lone saturated interactive hue); enforced live by `.claude/hooks/pre-edit-drift-gate.sh` per CLAUDE.md taste-rules header

### dp-simultaneous-contrast , Simultaneous Contrast
- Reasoning: Already named, T1-tiered, and operationalized with a dedicated verification surface rather than left as a token-table assumption. The system already treats a color's appearance as context-dependent (white vs sunken vs photo) and routes verification through the live styleguide page for exactly that reason. This is good practice already in force, not a gap a harvested principle would fill.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:120 ('Simultaneous contrast (T1): the same token reads differently on white vs sunken vs photo; evaluate pairs in their real context, this is why the styleguide page, not the token table, is the verification surface')
- Code evidence: public/solen-styleguide.html (168 lines, live 'visual rulebook' per CLAUDE.md, exists and is current as of 2026-07-16) is the concrete artifact that operationalizes the law: tokens are shown in situ (on white, on sunken, over photo) rather than in an isolated swatch table

### dp-rgb-not-perceptually-uniform , RGB is not perceptually uniform: build color systems in CIELAB/OKLab
- Reasoning: Solen is nearly monochrome by design (80/17 neutral+ink, one sparse blue accent per LOCKFILE taste rule 3), so the RGB-uniformity problem only actually bites where the system needs to GENERATE a multi-hue ramp, which today is exactly one place: dashboard charts needing 3+ distinct series. RATIONALE.md already names the correct mechanic (OKLab/OKLCH near-uniform L vs HSL's brightness artifacts, Ottosson 2020, tier T1) and LOCKFILE already operationalizes it: derive series hues in OKLCH at fixed lightness+chroma, step hue +25-30 deg per series. Everywhere else in the product, colors are hand-picked semantic tokens (star yellow, success green, error red) checked individually against WCAG/APCA, a different and already-covered problem (RATIONALE.md domain 3). Building a whole CIELAB/OKLab pipeline for a near-monochrome marketplace with no generated gradients would be solving a problem the product doesn't have outside the one place it is already solved.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:79-81 (Domain 2 Color: "OKLCH is the palette-generation space... HSL lightness is a math artifact of RGB... OKLab/OKLCH gives near-uniform L"); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:98-102 ("derive hues in OKLCH from s-accent, fixed lightness+chroma, hue stepped +25-30 per series (perceptually even)")
- Code evidence: none checked in: app/lib/components for a live chart-series color generator (grepped hue-step/generateSeriesColor/chartColors, oklch()/oklab() across tailwind.config.js, globals.css, lib, components ,  zero hits). The multi-series dashboard chart trigger condition (>2 distinct series) has not yet shipped, so the code path is dormant, but the law is dated, T1-cited, and specified precisely enough to implement on demand.

### dp-icon-label-primary-nav , Icon + Label Pairing: primary nav never ships icon-only
