- Reasoning: This is close to verbatim existing, dated law, not a gap. Solen's iOS primary nav ships every single tab with both an SF Symbol icon and a visible text label (Home, Suche, Inspo, Karte, Profil), confirmed live in the tab layout. On the general iconography axis, RATIONALE.md pins a closed, owner-demanded exception list for where icon-only is even allowed at all (close X, back arrow, search magnifier, hamburger, save heart, copy-beside-code, share-beside-object) and states "EVERYTHING else carries a visible word," dated the same day as this evaluation, which is a stricter, more precise rule than the harvested principle's blanket nav-only framing. A dashboard sidebar rail may collapse to icon-only at 64px, but that is a deliberate, desktop-only, tooltip-backed collapse state (Notion/Linear/Vercel pattern), not the always-visible primary nav the principle is warning about.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:756 (Domain 18: "DEFINED boundary (owner demanded the 'almost' be pinned, 2026-07-16): icon-only is permitted for EXACTLY this closed set... EVERYTHING else carries a visible word"); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1293-1294 ("famous icons... ride label-free; anything less iconic gets a visible label on mobile")
- Code evidence: solen-mobile/src/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:11-34 ,  every NativeTabs.Trigger (index/suche/entdecken/karte/profil) pairs an <Icon> with a visible <Label> (Home, Suche, Inspo, Karte, Profil); no icon-only tab exists in the primary nav.

### dp-icon-bounding-box-trim , Icon Bounding Box and Trim (Live) Area
- Reasoning: The mechanic this principle describes (fixed canvas, smaller live/trim area so different silhouettes read as equal visual size) is already named as law and already satisfied structurally, because Solen mandates Lucide as the ONLY icon source for UI chrome, and Lucide is itself built on exactly this system (24px canvas, ~20px live area, keyline-matched silhouettes). RATIONALE.md states this explicitly and concludes Lucide's grid already conforms. For the rare custom (non-Lucide) icon commission, the spec doc already hands the artist the same rule operationally: fixed 24x24 artboard, consistent stroke, transparent background. Re-adding this as a new principle would be re-deriving a rule Solen already gets for free by having picked one disciplined icon vendor rather than hand-drawing icons.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:752 (Domain 18 iconography: "Optical grid (CONV, Material's system): 24px canvas, 20px live area, keyline shapes... Lucide's 24-grid/2px default conforms"); _design-system/_icons-to-make.md:13 ("Source/export: 24x24 art board, ~1.75-2px stroke, line style... B&W")
- Code evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:756 and LOCKFILE.md:15 confirm lucide-react is the exclusive icon source project-wide (grep across app/components shows only lucide-react imports for UI icons, e.g. FAQItem.tsx:3, Header.tsx:7, SearchTemplate.tsx:47), so the bounding-box/trim discipline is inherited from the library rather than needing a house-authored spec.

### dp-optical-cap-height-alignment , Optical (cap-height) icon-to-text alignment
- Reasoning: Solen already named this exact failure mode (flexbox align-items:center trusts the box, not the ink) and settled it as a dated owner decision one day before this review: 'icon-text baselines eye-checked at 2x' instead of trusting flexbox. The harvested principle's specific fix (a fixed 0.1-0.2em translateY offset) is one plausible implementation, but Solen deliberately chose eye-checking over a blind formula, because Lucide glyphs vary in optical weight per icon, so a single numeric offset would drift on some icons. Re-adding this as a new rule would relitigate a decision made 2026-07-15; the correct next step is the already-queued retrofit sweep, not a new principle.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:144 ('icon-in-disc centering is checked by eye at 2x zoom, not by flexbox faith'); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:187 (2026-07-15, owner verbatim '1a 2 your pick 3b 4 b 5 no dark mode' -> 'Optical alignment | Adopt corrections as a rule: ... icon-text baselines eye-checked at 2x'); RATIONALE.md:658 confirms 'optical corrections adopted as a rule'.
- Code evidence: none checked in: no per-component translateY/baseline offset found on icon+text rows (retrofit is an open item per RATIONALE.md section 15, not yet swept into code).

### dp-optical-centering-icon-design , Optical centering over geometric centering in icon design
- Reasoning: This is the same 2026-07-15 owner-dated decision, and it names the identical mechanic the harvested principle describes (asymmetric mass distribution making box-centering read as off-balance), with the same asymmetric-shape example class (a triangle/chevron's visual mass sitting toward one edge). Solen already ships numeric guidance for it (circles overshoot squares by ~2-4%, asymmetric glyphs nudge ~4-8% toward visual center) rather than leaving it as an unquantified idea, so adding the harvested version would be a downgrade, not a gap-fill.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:144 ('a triangle centered by box reads off-center ... circles sized next to squares get a ~2-4 percent overshoot'); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:187 ('play/asymmetric glyphs nudge toward visual center, circles next to squares get a small size overshoot').
- Code evidence: none checked in: no icon component currently applies a measured overshoot/nudge (open retrofit item, same sweep as above).

### dp-one-second-feedback-threshold , The one-second feedback threshold
- Reasoning: Solen already reasoned through this exact mechanic and wrote it into the loading-state grammar with its own tuned numbers: gate the trigger-button spinner behind a >300ms threshold, and suppress any refresh indicator under 500ms so a fast response completes silently. That is the identical gate-behind-a-delay-timer idea the harvested principle describes, just calibrated tighter (300-500ms vs a generic ~1s) to 'our scale' i.e. Solen's typically fast API responses, which is a defensible product-specific number, not a gap. The one place a spinner fires the instant a mutation starts (PayConfirmStep's isSubmitting spinner) is not actually a violation: Stripe payment round-trips always exceed 300ms, so immediate-and-persistent IS the correct behavior for that specific >300ms case, it is simply implemented via 'submission always takes a while' rather than a formal timer.
- Law evidence: _design-system/components/LoadingStates.md Pattern 4 ('Loading inside mutation: If the mutation takes >300ms (rare for our scale), show a loading state on the trigger button') and Pattern 5 ('Stale/refreshing: OR no visual indicator if refetch is <500ms, let it complete silently').
- Code evidence: components-legacy/booking/PayConfirmStep.tsx:606 (`{isSubmitting && <Spinner size="sm" invert />}`, fires immediately, correct because payment submission inherently exceeds 300ms); grepped app/components/lib for a shared delay/debounce loading hook, zero hits, confirming the threshold lives as documented prose/discipline rather than a reusable primitive, but the design rule itself is already settled.

### dp-ease-out-over-linear , Ease-out over linear motion
- Reasoning: Solen's whole motion system is built on this exact physics logic and goes further than the generic principle: it names FOUR curves by semantic role (glide=decelerate for entrances, thud=accelerate for press-down/exits, snap=standard in-out, spring=earned overshoot) rather than a flat ease-out-vs-linear binary, and RATIONALE.md documents the Material-motion research grounding for why decelerate reads as arrival and linear reads as mechanical. Linear easing does not appear anywhere in the production codebase (grep across app/globals.css and the primitives motion module turned up zero linear usages), and the ENTER RECIPE (opacity+scale+blur, 420ms glide) is gate-enforced by a PreToolUse hook, so this isn't just documented, it's physically hard to violate. Re-adding this as a fresh principle would just restate law that is already stricter than the claim.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:163 ("Easing semantics... decelerate (ease-out) for entrances... linear only for continuous processes"), RATIONALE.md:457-460, _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:578-585 (four named curves), _design-system/MOTION.md:28,52,62 (ENTER RECIPE + gate-enforced)
- Code evidence: app/globals.css:161-163,467,907,1132,1138 and app/[locale]/_components/primitives/motion.ts:24,35 all use cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1) (glide); no `ease: linear` or `linear` easing found in app/ or lib/ via grep

### dp-touch-target-independent-of-icon-size , Touch Target Size Is Independent of Icon Size
- Reasoning: This is not just present as a rule, it's the textbook shipped implementation. HeartButton renders a 44x44 transparent button as the tap target, wrapping a visually smaller 28x28 (or 38 on hero) frosted glass circle, wrapping a 16-22px heart glyph, with an explicit code comment naming the WCAG/ergonomic rationale and the exact V3-D73 decision date. RATIONALE.md independently derives this as a worked Fitts's Law example (hit area is the W term, not the paint) and treats the split as the canonical pattern other components should follow. This is a mature, load-bearing pattern in a Swiss mobile-first booking product where fat-finger mis-taps directly cost bookings.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:64 ("HeartButton 44 hit / 32 visible, the hit-area/visible split is exactly Fitts"), RATIONALE.md:474-478 (worked DECISION/FORCES/MECHANIC writeup), LOCKFILE.md:714-715 ("44px hit area, 32px visible circle")
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/HeartButton.tsx:164-193 (outer <button> is `h-11 w-11` 44x44, comment: "Button hit area is 44x44 (WCAG + ergonomic minimum); the VISIBLE glass circle is 28x28... Outer button is transparent + larger; inner div carries all the glass styling"); pattern repeats at 10 other call sites via `h-11 w-11` grep in app/[locale]/_components/

### dp-44px-minimum-touch-target , 44px Minimum Touch Target
- Reasoning: The 44px floor is a frozen literal in the design contract, applied via a documented h-11/w-11 utility class convention, and RATIONALE.md ties it explicitly to Fitts's Law plus the WCAG/HIG/Material standards ladder, deliberately choosing the AAA/HIG 44px line over the weaker AA 24px minimum. There's also a named, deliberate exception (FeaturedStylists SaveHeart at 28x28, acceptable because the parent card is the primary tap target) which shows the floor is enforced with judgment, not blindly, exactly the kind of maturity that makes this ALREADY_EXISTS rather than a gap.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md design contract table row "touch target | interactive controls >= 44px (h-11), the a11y floor"; _design-system/SOURCE.md:1030-1033 ("Minimum 44x44 px hit area per WCAG 2.1... HeartButton's pattern... is the canonical solution"); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:63-65,153 (Fitts's Law T1 grounding, WCAG 2.5.5 AAA/HIG 44pt citation)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/HeartButton.tsx:177 (`h-11 w-11` outer button); 10 total `h-11 w-11` occurrences across app/[locale]/_components/ confirming the pattern is applied broadly, not a one-off

### dp-whole-container-tap-targets , Whole-Container Tap Targets (Fitts's Law)
- Reasoning: This is the exact live pattern for salon cards on a booking marketplace where users scan many cards fast: the whole card is the primary target (navigate to the salon) and only the heart-save is a secondary, smaller, intentionally-distinct action. Making just the card title or a tiny button the target would make the dominant use case (tap to view salon) slower and more error-prone per Fitts, exactly what RATIONALE.md's Fitts section already argues for the 44px floor. Nothing to add; this is standing practice, not a gap.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:63-65 (Fitts's Law, T1, cites the HeartButton hit-area/paint split as 'exactly Fitts'); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md design-contract table row 'touch target | interactive controls >= 44px (h-11), the a11y floor'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/SalonCard.tsx:363-501 wraps the entire card in one <Link href={.../salon/${slug}}>; HeartButton is nested inside at line 448 and calls e.stopPropagation() at app/[locale]/_components/homepage/HeartButton.tsx:144 so the save-tap doesn't also fire card navigation; same convention documented inline at app/[locale]/_components/homepage/FeaturedStylists.tsx:151,169 ('stopPropagation so taps don't bubble up to the parent Link')

### dp-wcag-contrast-luminance-not-hue , WCAG Contrast Ratio Measures Luminance, Not Hue Distinguishability
- Reasoning: For a booking marketplace with status-critical color coding (open/closed, available/booked, success/error on payment), passing-ratio-but-indistinguishable-hue is a real risk the project has already reasoned through and answered with a stricter policy than the raw claim: WCAG 1.4.1 (never color-alone) plus icon+text+color redundancy on every status chip. Re-adding this as a new principle would just restate law that's already deeper and already dated/owner-approved.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:93-120 (domain 3, documents the exact WCAG-formula limitation the principle describes: 'overstates contrast for dark pairs... polarity-blind, ignores weight/size', names APCA as the supplement) and line 84 ('WCAG 1.4.1: color never the sole carrier... icon + text + color on status chips, check inside the success disc, is the mechanic that makes it non-negotiable rather than stylistic'); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:188 records the dated owner-approved policy ('WCAG 2.2 AA floor + APCA supplement' with an on-sunken retune)
- Code evidence: none checked in: this is a documented policy layer (RATIONALE.md line 660 flags the on-sunken retune as an implementation sweep not yet swept sitewide), not a component to point at

### dp-hicks-law , Hick's Law (Hick-Hyman Law)
- Reasoning: Hick's Law is already named, cited (T1, formal standard tier) and used to ground two live product decisions: single-select search filters (an explicit owner lock, implemented in ServiceCategoryFilter.tsx and the search UI) and grouping services into 3-5 labeled categories rather than a flat long list. RATIONALE.md also does the more sophisticated thing the harvested principle skips: it names the exact myth trap ('fewer options convert better' from the debunked jam study) and separates decision-latency (Hick, real, T1) from choice-overload-hurts-conversion (weak, meta d=0.02, PSYCHOLOGY.md myth table), which is a stricter and more honest application of the law than the generic pitch. There is nothing left to add; the gap this principle would fill is already closed with better rigor than the source material itself.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:68-69 (Hick's Law, T1); _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:46 (3-5 labeled categories, choice-overload myth caveat)
- Code evidence: components-legacy/salon/ServiceCategoryFilter.tsx (category grouping); search single-select behavior per project_search_facts memory (owner lock)

### dp-fitts-law , Fitts's Law
- Reasoning: Fitts's Law is extensively documented (T1, formal standard) and already drives multiple shipped decisions: the 44px touch-target floor with an explicit hit-area/visible-paint split (HeartButton 44px hit / 32px visible circle is cited as the textbook W-term example), the ~90px sheet drag-dismiss release threshold, and a dedicated 'touch ergonomics' domain (RATIONALE.md domain 10) that extends Fitts from size to placement: bottom-center thumb-zone as the cheap D-term zone, top corners as the expensive zone, and slip-adjacency spacing between high-consequence targets (Bezahlen vs Abbrechen). The specific retrofit the harvested principle's example argues for, bottom-anchored, edge-docked primary CTAs, is not a gap: Solen's sticky bottom commit bars (booking, pay, walk-in) are a locked, shipped pattern (TASTE_LOG.md round 2, SalonMobileBookBar) explicitly reasoned about as 'the highest-value tap sits in the cheapest zone.' Nothing in the harvested principle exceeds what is already law and code here.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:63-66, 153, 210-217 (Fitts's Law, T1, touch ergonomics domain); _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:95-113 (sticky bottom CTA, round 2, 2026-06-07)
- Code evidence: LOCKFILE.md:715 (HeartButton 44px hit / 32px visible, cited in RATIONALE.md:64); components-legacy for SalonMobileBookBar sticky-bottom pattern per TASTE_LOG.md applied-in-code note

### dp-jakobs-law , Jakob's Law
- Reasoning: Solen's entire STRUCTURE axis is Jakob's Law operationalized as a build process, not just an idea. The dual-axis rule (structure=Fresha, aesthetic=Uber) exists precisely because a Swiss booking marketplace's users arrive with mental models trained on Fresha/Booksy/every other booking app: logo top-left, category nav center, account top-right, sticky search bar, PDP hero as photo grid. Deviating from those positions to chase novelty would cost bookings, not win them, since booking flows are low-frequency, high-friction tasks where users need zero relearning. There is nothing left to add: the product already treats category convention as free usability and pays a structure-capture cost (fresha-section-capture skill) specifically to avoid guessing at conventions from memory.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:690 names Jakob's law explicitly ('users spend most time on other products, so category conventions are free usability; the STRUCTURE = Fresha axis is applied Jakob'); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:900 (§10.0 dual-axis table) codifies STRUCTURE=Fresha as the source of truth for IA/layout/nav/hero-pattern/sticky-nav with worked examples ('PDP hero = 3-photo grid not full-bleed', 'sticky tab nav after hero')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/layout/Header.tsx:20-33 (documented anatomy: 'Solon· Salons Stilist:innen Entdecken Über uns [Anmelden]' with logo left / nav center / account right, the conventional pattern) and lines 41-72 (SERVICES_MENU / BUSINESS_MENU dropdowns, standard header-nav convention); the entire fresha-section-capture skill + public/_pixel-refs/fresha/<section>/SPEC.md pipeline exists to capture conventional structure before building any section

### dp-repetition-establishes-system , Repetition (establishing one system)
- Reasoning: This is exactly the mechanic Solen already cites and enforces mechanically through two named, locked rules: the twin-control rule (same-purpose controls must be the identical component instance) and grouped list cards (same-kind rows render inside ONE enclosed card, never as separate same-shaped-but-ungrouped cards). The claim's second half, that repetition alone only establishes 'same kind' and needs proximity/enclosure to establish 'same group', is the precise justification Solen's grouped-list-card rule gives for banning per-row bordered cards: identical row styling without a shared container reads as a list of similar-but-unrelated items, so the rule mandates the enclosing card specifically to add the missing grouping cue.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:53-54 and :364 (Gestalt similarity: 'identical form is the grouping cue... styling them apart breaks that signal'); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:435-437 (Twin-control rule, DS-4: same-purpose controls must be the SAME component instance, styling twins differently is called drift); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:463-477 (Grouped list cards rule: same-kind rows render as ONE card per group with rows inside, 'NEVER separate bordered/shadowed cards per row', explicitly because separate identical cards lack the enclosure cue)
- Code evidence: Grouped-list-card rule applied 2026-06-11 per LOCKFILE:477 to SalonServices, SalonServicesSheet, booking ServicesStaffStep, StaffProfilePage Leistungen (rounded-[24px] border shadow-whisper container with px-5 py-[18px] rows separated only by border-t, not by repeated card shadows)

### dp-kill-ambiguity-in-form-fields , Kill Ambiguity in Form Fields, Not Just Friction
- Reasoning: Every element the principle asks for is a shipped, locked primitive: persistent above-field labels (floating labels explicitly banned), a required-marker convention, per-field real-time tone states, and exact-cause error copy. For a Swiss/DACH booking form (payment details, guest checkout, salon signup) ambiguous fields are especially costly since users abandon rather than guess at unstated formats in a language-sensitive UI, which is why the system additionally locked validate-on-blur/submit timing and top-aligned labels as the i18n-safe default (German labels don't fit left columns). There is no gap here to fill.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:734 (validation timing: 'validate on blur or submit, never per keystroke; confirm success early, deliver errors late'); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:744 (label placement: top-aligned default, placeholder-as-label banned by mechanic not taste); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1345-1347 (error copy names the exact cause, e.g. 'Password needs at least 8 characters', never generic 'Invalid input'; field errors sit under the field with red border + icon, banner errors reserved for whole-form failures)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/FieldLabel.tsx (required -> trailing red dot, optional -> trailing tag, labels rendered above field only, floating labels banned per _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:52); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/FieldHelper.tsx (default/error/warning/success tones with icon + role="alert" on error, per COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:62); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/TextInput.tsx:34-51 (tone variants default/error/warning/success/active with distinct border+ring treatment per field)

### dp-actions-vs-navigation-buttons-links , Actions vs. Navigation: Buttons Mutate, Links Relocate
- Reasoning: The underlying mechanic, more visual commitment for state-mutating actions and lighter affordance for pure navigation, is already law, just implemented with a finer three-way split than the harvested principle's binary. Solen's per-element decision rule puts the ONE primary mutating commit action (Buchen, Bezahlen) as the sole ink-FILLED control on any screen; everything else, including pure-navigation buttons like See-all, is deliberately NOT filled, it gets ink text plus a chevron/weight affordance instead. This is a stricter and more considered version of the harvested claim, correct in spirit, wrong in one literal detail: the principle's own example ('filled BLUE CREATE ACCOUNT') would violate the explicit lock that blue never fills a primary action button. So the mutate-vs-navigate distinction is already enforced, but through ink-fill vs ink-affordance, not through blue-vs-underline, and re-adding this principle verbatim risks reintroducing the blue-filled-button pattern the owner rejected.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:16 ('Primary CTAs stay bg-s-ink... Accent blue NEVER FILLS a primary action button, never a blue-filled primary'); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:134-141 (per-element decision rule: rule 3 'The one primary commit action? -> ink fill'; rule 5 'Everything else tappable -- secondary/ghost buttons, See-all, Skip/Spater, tertiary under-CTA links, icon tints, tappable rows -> INK with affordance. Only the ONE primary commit button is ink-FILLED')
- Code evidence: not separately checked in code beyond the LOCKFILE-cited component rules (per-element decision rule is enforced project-wide via taste rule 3 in CLAUDE.md and the drift-checker gate, not a single file)

### dp-information-scent-link-labels , Information scent: the link label itself must predict the destination
- Reasoning: Information scent is named and cited by source (Pirolli and Card 1999) as the exact mechanic behind two live rules: the icon-verbosity ladder (icon-only permitted only where the adjacent object already supplies the scent, otherwise a visible word is mandatory) and CTA copy guidance that explicitly prefers a verb-plus-object over a generic phrase when the destination isn't obvious. For a marketplace where users tab through review counts, salon links, and booking CTAs, a scentless label ('here', 'click', 'mehr erfahren') costs a re-read or a wrong tap, exactly the failure this principle describes, and it is already the named justification for banning vague link/button copy. Worth flagging as a live drift rather than a gap: the law's own worked counter-example, 'Mehr erfahren', still ships as literal CTA copy in BusinessTeaser.tsx and WhySolen.tsx, which is a violation of an existing rule, not evidence a new rule is needed.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:692 ('Information scent (T2, Pirolli and Card 1999...) vague labels have weak scent and raise abandonment of the trail... CTA copy: verb + object beats "Mehr erfahren" when the destination is not obvious'); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:756 (icon+label boundary: icon-only permitted only for a closed set where the adjacent object supplies scent, everything else carries a visible word)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/BusinessTeaser.tsx:79 and app/[locale]/_components/homepage/WhySolen.tsx:131 both render literal 'Mehr erfahren' CTA copy, the exact weak-scent example RATIONALE.md:692 warns against, showing the rule exists but has live drift (not something this evaluation should silently fix, flagged for a separate follow-up)

### dp-atomic-design-compose-by-reference , Atomic Design: Compose by Reference, Not by Page
- Reasoning: This is exactly Solen's existing component discipline, not a gap. COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md is a live, enforced registry organized by Layer (1 chrome / 2 accent / 3 semantic) that every component must be checked against before building, and CLAUDE.md hard-requires a registry entry the same turn a shared component is built. Real composition-by-reference is documented and verifiable in the registry itself: CookieConsent's settings modal composes `Modal` + 3x `Switch` by reference; SalonBundles composes a shared `BundleCard`; DateTimePicker is explicitly 'THE one date/time primitive... do NOT build another, extend this one' with two real call sites cited (SearchBar.tsx:542, DateTimeStep.tsx:194); TabPill.tsx is documented as 'currently inlined in 3 places... this is the single source... will be used by search filters too' ,  the exact atom-propagation mechanic the principle describes. This is a mature, working system already, not something to add.
- Law evidence: _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md (Layer 1/2/3 system, universal-components rule V3-D205 banning if-category branches); project CLAUDE.md 'Component registry' section (mandatory registry entry same turn as new component)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/TabPill.tsx:1-17 (single-source doc comment, 3 call sites); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/DateTimePicker.tsx (2 real call sites per registry row); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/CookieConsent.tsx (composes Modal + Switch by reference)

### dp-numeric-percentage-builds-trust , Numeric percentage increases perceived trustworthiness of a progress bar
- Reasoning: The underlying insight (a bar alone forces estimation; a live number removes it) is already applied in Solen, just via the more literal format that fits this product's no-fabrication ethos: a raw X/Y count rather than a derived percentage. `ImportProgressBar.tsx` computes `percent` for the bar's fill width but renders the user-facing number as `{current}/{total}` in tabular numerals next to the bar, i.e. the actual measured quantity, not an abstracted percentage that implies false precision. For the two most prominent 'could get a progress bar' surfaces in the product, percentage bars are specifically rejected: booking flow has a graveyard entry banning 'ANY progress UI... icon stepper, segment bar, Schritt-N meta' in favor of a bare back arrow (mockup-20, exact Fresha), confirmed live in `BookingWizard.tsx`'s own comment ('NO progress UI anywhere'); the dashboard setup wizard uses a discrete numbered-step tracker with a connector-line fill, never a numeric percent badge. Adding a standalone '21%' pill would duplicate an already-solved problem and, on the two flagship surfaces, would be a graveyard re-proposal.
- Law evidence: _design-system/REMOVED.md:15 ('booking stepper progress-bar step-indicator... ANY progress UI in the booking flow... mockup-20 lock'); _design-system/components/ProgressStepper.md ('Owner flagged a screen showing a step tracker AND a separate time bar - why two. Never both')
- Code evidence: components-legacy/discovery/ImportProgressBar.tsx:9-24 (percent computed for bar width, current/total rendered as the trust-building number); components-legacy/booking/BookingWizard.tsx:30-43 (comment documents the old 'Q56 progress indicator' with Schritt N/3 was removed, 'NO progress UI anywhere'); components-legacy/onboarding/SetupWizard.tsx:82-108 (discrete numbered-step tracker + connector fill, no percent label)

### dp-goal-gradient-effect , Goal-gradient effect: motivation spikes as the finish line nears
- Reasoning: This is a direct, near-verbatim hit against Solen's own behavioral law layer, not a new idea. PSYCHOLOGY.md Law 3, 'Never start a user at zero (T2)', is this exact mechanism: 'Endowed progress + goal gradient: a stated head start raises completion... Any progress UI credits what is already done... Loyalty shows a numeric distance to the next tier and seeds head starts only WITH a stated reason... After a reward, immediately show the next goal (post-reward slump is real).' It is evidence-tiered (T2, honest about being one field study plus a replication, not overclaimed) and it already embeds the exact caveat the harvested principle raises: never fabricate the value, because Solen's Law 9 ('Real numbers only, forever') sits directly beside it and Solen's blanket no-fabricated-data rule governs any progress UI. Real code implements the numeric-distance half of this law today.
- Law evidence: _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:21-22 (Law 3, T2, goal-gradient + endowed progress, with the no-fabrication caveat via Law 9 and the project's blanket no-fabricated-data rule)
- Code evidence: lib/loyalty/status.ts:34,69 (`toNext: number | null` literally computes visits remaining to the next tier, i.e. numeric distance to goal, not a fabricated value)

### dp-pre-selection-is-not-consent , Pre-Selection Is Not Consent
- Reasoning: Solen already implements this as both law and verified code, correctly. PSYCHOLOGY.md Law 2 states the hard line in almost the same words as the harvested principle: 'Hard line: never pre-check anything that costs money or subscribes; that is the dark-pattern twin of the same mechanism.' In the live cookie-consent implementation, the only pre-checked toggle is the 'Notwendig' (necessary) switch, which is disabled and always-on for a legitimate reason (auth session, i18n, the banner's own state) ,  exactly the one exception GDPR/Recital 32 permits. The Analytics and Marketing switches default to `consent?.analytics ?? false` / `consent?.marketing ?? false`, i.e. unchecked until the user acts, and the shared `Switch` primitive itself defaults `defaultChecked = false`. A repo-wide grep for pre-checked patterns outside the necessary-cookies case returns nothing, and the sole newsletter opt-in (Footer) requires typing an email and submitting, which is itself the affirmative act. There is nothing to add; this is a compliance floor the codebase already clears.
- Law evidence: _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:18-19 (Law 2, hard line: never pre-check anything that costs money or subscribes)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/CookieConsent.tsx:363-364 (analytics/marketing default to false), :393 (`<Switch checked disabled>` only for Notwendig, the legitimate-interest exception), :400,:409 (Analytics/Marketing switches unchecked until toggled); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Switch.tsx:47 (`defaultChecked = false`)

### dp-peak-end-rule , The Peak-End Rule
- Reasoning: This is law 1 of the 15-law canon almost verbatim, and it has already been built out end to end, not just documented: the booking-confirmed moment is explicitly named 'the peak' and was rebuilt to a 5/5 score with the SuccessMark celebration component, ink actions, and the single blue manage-link, exactly the mid-flow-friction-vs-ending distinction the principle describes. Reusing SuccessMark on every success peak (booking-confirmed, walk-in-joined, gift-card-sent, package-bought) rather than a static checkmark is a direct implementation of 'the peak and ending carry disproportionate memory weight, don't waste them.'
- Law evidence: _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:15-16 (law 1, near-identical claim, T1, meta-analysis r=.58 cited); _design-system/MOTION.md:54 ('Celebratory moments on emotional peaks... the peak is booking-confirmed'), :70 ('Confirmation rebuilt to 5/5... date is focal... one blue manage-link'), :64 ('Reuse on every success peak, do not re-build the animation per surface'), :77 (DONE log), :106
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/SuccessMark.tsx (solid green disc + white check, spring-in with ring pulse, MOTION.md:60); components-legacy/booking/PayConfirmStep.tsx and BookingWizard.tsx final confirm step wire SuccessMark on the booking-confirmed peak per MOTION.md:77 commit references c9e405378/f89587203

### dp-golden-ratio-is-a-myth , The golden ratio is a myth, not a design law
- Reasoning: This isn't a UI change to add, it's an epistemic/citation rule, and Solen's own design-law file already states the identical conclusion with the identical debunking source: phi is real math but 'design in the golden ratio' is folklore with no historical evidentiary record, so never cite it as a justification. The system goes further than the harvested principle by planning a mechanical PreToolUse gate that blocks any doc edit citing golden-ratio-as-law, with a self-test pair already specified. Nothing here would change any pixel of the product; it would just be re-stating a settled, already-enforced editorial rule.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:235 (folklore/myth table: 'Golden ratio governs beauty | MYTH (Markowsky 1992: canonical examples unsupported) | 1.618 is one available ratio if you like it, never because phi'); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:225 (planned folklore-citation gate explicitly named to block 'golden ratio as law' claims in docs, with should-block/should-pass self-test already written)
- Code evidence: none checked in: no golden-ratio-derived sizing exists in tailwind.config.js or _design-system/LOCKFILE.md's radius/spacing scales (radius steps are 'a hand-picked set, CONV, not derived from a ratio or formula' per RATIONALE.md:356), so there is no live ratio-based system to correct either

### dm-corner-radius-threat , Corner Radius as a Threat Signal
- Reasoning: The claim (rounded > sharp for anything touchable) is both cited and already the universal default. Every interactive surface in the locked design contract is rounded: cards 16px, buttons/chips/pills fully round, inputs 16px, sheets 28px. The only 0-radius surface is flush photography, which is decorative, not interactive, so it doesn't contradict the mechanism (nothing that invites a touch/commitment is sharp-cornered anywhere in the system). Re-adding this as a new principle would just restate an already-enforced, already-cited rule.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:147-149 ('Rounded reads friendly (T2, Bar and Neta 2006/2007: preference for curved contours; sharp contours raise amygdala response)... the mechanic under our pill buttons and 16px cards'); CLAUDE.md design contract table 'radius' row (card/block 16 rounded-card, button/chip pill, input 16, sheet 28, image flush 0); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:351-356 (radius scale DECISION block, MECHANIC citing the same Bar-Neta study)
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:252 card:'16px', :256 pill:'9999px', :258 input:'16px'; app/globals.css:40-43 --radius-card:16px, --radius-pill:9999px, --radius-input:16px (live CSS vars backing every card/button/input in the app)

### dm-chunking-forms , Chunking long forms into small steps
- Reasoning: Every substantial data-entry surface in the product is already chunked rather than a single continuous wall: the booking flow is a multi-screen wizard (services, staff, time, pay), salon onboarding is a config-driven one-question-per-screen flow, salon setup is a wizard, and even the one screen with genuinely many fields (dashboard settings, 27 inputs) is split into 11 separate tabs via ExpandableTabs rather than rendered as one form. The 'reduce what's visible at once, not total work' mechanism the principle describes is the exact grammar Solen already ships everywhere data entry happens.
- Law evidence: _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:45 (ProgressStepper locked component for 'a short booking/checkout/queue flow header... or onboarding checklist'); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1227 stepper recipe; _design-system/REMOVED.md:15 notes only the visible progress-bar CHROME was removed from booking specifically (mockup-20: back arrow is the nav), not the underlying multi-screen chunking, which stays
- Code evidence: components-legacy/booking/BookingWizard.tsx (multi-step services->staff->time->pay); app/[locale]/onboarding/OnboardingFlow.tsx:26-61 (STEPS config array, one question per screen, TOTAL = STEPS.length); components-legacy/onboarding/SetupWizard.tsx (wizard); app/[locale]/dashboard/settings/page.tsx:1489-1499 (27 inputs split into 11 ExpandableTabs tabs: ProfileTab, PaymentsTab, VacationTab, etc., not one continuous form)

### dm-group-digits-four , Group digits four by four
- Reasoning: Solen already groups long digit strings into readable chunks everywhere it renders them, just not always in groups of exactly 4 because the chunk size follows the data's own natural grouping (Swiss phone numbers group as 2-3-2-2, not 4-4-4-4). The one place a true 16-digit card number would appear is payment entry, and Solen correctly never renders that itself: card capture runs through Stripe's hosted Elements (`@stripe/react-stripe-js`, imported in app/[locale]/checkout/page.tsx), which owns its own digit-grouping and PCI scope. Building a custom formatted card-number field would be a security regression, not an improvement, for a marketplace that has deliberately kept card data out of its own DOM. Booking reference codes are 5-char alphanumeric (SOL-7K2QX) and don't need chunking at that length, and Stripe Connect owns payout/IBAN capture the same way. The underlying UX instinct (long runs of digits should be perceptually pre-chunked) is already codified as policy and enforced by a real utility, so there is nothing left to add.
- Law evidence: LOCKFILE.md section 13.4 / lines 208, 1264: codes render in Inter Tight tabular-nums specifically so digits stay aligned and scannable (the closest existing law to 'digit legibility'), no rule anywhere calls for building a custom raw card-number field.
- Code evidence: lib/format-phone.ts:1-24 (owner-commissioned 2026-06-12 'as you type, automatic spacing' live formatter, e.g. '0791234567' -> '079 123 45 67'); app/[locale]/checkout/page.tsx:7-13 uses @stripe/react-stripe-js so Solen never renders a raw card-number string; app/[locale]/walk-in-pay/page.tsx:84 shows the masked convention 'Visa ···· 4242' (last 4 only, never the full run); lib/bookings/reference.ts:18-33 (5-char SOL- codes, too short to need chunking).

### dm-fill-the-track , Fill the Track: filled length is the value
- Reasoning: This is exactly the pattern Solen's own search price slider already implements, down to the same reasoning in the code comment: a flat/unfilled track is a slower relative-position read, so the segment from the track start to the thumb is colored to give a second, faster boundary-length read of the value, plus a printed live number above it. For a booking marketplace where the price slider gates a real filter decision (which salons even show up), that second read matters more than in a cosmetic slider, so the product already invested in it deliberately.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md taste rule 3 ('price slider' listed explicitly among neutral filter surfaces) and LOCKFILE section 1.5 v3 (filters are neutral, not blue) govern the fill's COLOR, but the filled-track pattern itself predates and survives that law: FilterSheet.tsx line 60-62 cites 'V3-D391: price filter is a SLIDER (Fresha "Maximum price" model)'.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/search/FilterSheet.tsx:63-139 (PriceSlider). Line 111-112 comment: 'custom track so the FILL (left -> thumb) shows colour ... not a flat grey bar'; line 125-131 renders a full-width neutral track plus an absolutely-positioned filled div sized to `${pct}%` under the thumb; line 108-110 prints the live value ('upToLabel(val)') above the track, satisfying both the boundary-length read and the numeric label the harvested principle calls for.

### dm-type-scale-discrete-steps , Type sizes from a fixed scale, never eyeballed
- Reasoning: A discretely-stepped, named type scale is one of the most heavily enforced parts of this design system, not a gap. For a booking marketplace running four locales and dozens of screen types, an ungoverned type scale is exactly the failure mode that produced the documented 'confirmation page shipped with ~11 sizes' incident; the fix already in place (11 named roles, a canonical tracking table, a hard per-screen budget, and a drift checker that logs any off-scale size) is a stricter version of the harvested principle, not a looser one.
- Law evidence: LOCKFILE.md section 2.5 (lines 281-333): 11 enumerated type roles each with ONE locked recipe, drift rules A7-A9 flag any callsite that doesn't map to a role; line 319 hard rule: any single screen draws from <=4 distinct sizes and <=2 weights, measured by DOM measurement as SENIOR_SCORECARD dimension 4; RATIONALE.md domain-1 (Preattentive attributes, T1 Ware/Bertin) explicitly grounds this: 'per-screen type budget, 4 sizes / 2 weights ... each added size is a distractor class that dilutes the hierarchy signal.'
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:221-253 (the literal role table: Hero H1 40/64px 700, Section H2 18/20px 600, Body 14/15px 400, etc., all clamp()-based, no eyeballed values); solen-drift-check skill statically flags arbitrary `text-[Npx]` classes against this table.

### dm-hierarchy-size-and-weight , Hierarchy needs size AND weight contrast together
- Reasoning: Every role in Solen's type scale already pairs a size jump with a weight jump (Section H2 is both larger AND heavier than Body, never just one), so single-axis hierarchy the way the harvested principle warns against structurally cannot happen through the role system. The product goes further than the principle asks: the one-ink-anchor card rule caps compound (size+weight+color) emphasis to exactly one element per card, because the owner's own complaint history ('using too bold multiple times destroys my eye') showed that over-applying the compound signal is the more common failure on this product, not under-applying it.
- Law evidence: RATIONALE.md line 132 (Hierarchy floors, researched 2026-07-15): 'a dominance jump is 30-50 percent larger, never a 1-2px nudge (NN/g)'; LOCKFILE.md section 2 role table pairs size+weight for every heading role against Body (e.g. Section H2 18-20px/600 vs Body 14-15px/400, roughly 30-33% larger AND two weight steps heavier); LOCKFILE.md lines 371-389 (rule A13, 'one ink anchor'): 'Bolding everything bolds nothing' -- explicitly the compound-signal mechanic the harvested principle describes, cited to the same Ware/Bertin preattentive-pop-out source in RATIONALE.md line 59.
- Code evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:221-241 (role table, every heading role locks size+weight together); LOCKFILE.md:371-389 (Card/list-item text hierarchy rule, drift rule A13, enforced against real card components: salon card, stylist card, service row).

### dm-one-accent-neutralized-siblings , One Accent, Neutralized Siblings (Pop-Out Effect)
- Reasoning: This is close to the single most load-bearing, most-repeated rule in the whole design system (CLAUDE.md taste rule 3, the 80/17 B&W pivot, the LOCKFILE accent table), and it is grounded in exactly the Von-Restorff/preattentive mechanic the harvested principle names. For a calm, restrained booking marketplace the owner has explicitly steered away from 'screaming' UI (rejecting a blue-everywhere v2 for this exact reason, the recorded 'vibrating blue' failure at 8-12 percent pixel coverage), this isn't a gap, it's the product's central design law, independently re-derived and cited with the same mechanic (Von Restorff isolation, T1) in RATIONALE.md.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md taste rule 3: 'blue #276EF1 goes ONLY on small clickable accents ... NOT on big CTAs ... The one primary/commit CTA stays ink'; LOCKFILE.md section 1.5 accent table (line ~190 area) enumerates the exact swaps (banned dual-blue combos, decorative blue dots -> ink-3 or success-green); RATIONALE.md line 60 and line 701: 'an accent that is rare is a preattentive signal; at 8-12 percent of pixels it stops discriminating (the recorded vibrating blue failure)' and 'Von Restorff isolation | T1 | the one-accent pop-out math (domain 1)'.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/search/FilterSheet.tsx:126-127 comment: 'Owner (2026-07-01): filters are neutral, not blue -- the fill + handle are ink' (the price slider deliberately does NOT take the accent color so it doesn't compete with the one true CTA); LOCKFILE.md:371-389 (A13 one-ink-anchor rule) is the same pop-out-preservation logic applied to card text instead of color.

### dm-primary-cta-mandatory-empty-states , Primary CTA Mandatory in empty states
- Reasoning: Solen already has both the law and the component this principle describes. RATIONALE.md's 'no dead ends' rule names EmptyState's action slot as its own enforcement mechanism, and the component ships an action prop that renders a CTA below the message. The principle's banned pattern (a passive 'refresh' on an empty query) doesn't occur anywhere: retry-style actions in this codebase live on ErrorState (a genuinely failed fetch), never on EmptyState (a query that correctly returned nothing). The real gap isn't a missing principle, it's inconsistent adoption: about ten dashboard admin screens (all-salons, all-users, badge-manager, marketing, review-moderation, salon-of-month-admin, segments, bundles, reviews, barber-leaderboard) render EmptyState with only icon+title+message and no action, which is an audit/enforcement debt against an already-locked law, not a new principle to add.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:798 ("every empty, error, and zero-results state offers a next action; EmptyState's action slot is the enforcement"); _design-system/SOURCE.md §10.2 (lines ~708-719, empty-state pattern requires a CTA button)
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/EmptyState.tsx:31 (action?: React.ReactNode) and :86 (renders action when passed); action IS wired at components-legacy/discovery/DiscoveryEmptyState.tsx:15 and app/[locale]/help/[slug]/page.tsx:61; action is MISSING at app/[locale]/dashboard/all-salons/page.tsx:208, dashboard/segments/page.tsx:82, dashboard/reviews/page.tsx:123 (and 7 more dashboard call sites)

### dm-match-error-severity-to-surface , Match error severity to its surface, never one universal modal
- Reasoning: Four distinct surfaces already exist and are already scoped to different severities: inline red border+caption for field/payment validation, Toast tones for transient/background failures, an ErrorState retry panel for a section whose fetch failed, and a route-level error.tsx/ErrorFallback boundary for an unrecoverable crash. The z-index stack even codifies these as ordered interruption tiers (toast above modal above sheet). Where Solen's real practice diverges from the principle is instructive: the principle's own example maps a declined card to a blocking Modal, but BookingPaymentForm renders 'Your card was declined' as inline red text under the Pay button, deliberately non-blocking, because checkout is a no-personality, no-interruption zone by law. So the general claim (severity determines surface) is already this product's practice; the specific severity-to-modal mapping in the principle's example would need to be dropped for payment failures specifically, since a blocking modal there conflicts with the calm-checkout law.
- Law evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md:1195 (anti-pattern: generic network-error toast for everything, distinguish RLS/4xx/5xx/network); _design-system/SOURCE.md §2.5 catalog (Toast tones, FormFieldError, AlertBanner rows, ~lines 276-284); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:606 (personality/interruption banned in booking flow/checkout/pay); _design-system/RATIONALE.md:400-401 + LOCKFILE.md:497 (toast 600 / modal 510 / sheet 410 stacking = deliberate interruption tiers)
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/AddressAutocomplete.tsx:49 (inline validation border); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Toast.tsx (success/error/info/warning tones, transient); components-legacy/ui/ErrorState.tsx (retry panel for a failed fetch); app/[locale]/salon/[slug]/error.tsx + components-legacy/ui/ErrorFallback.tsx (route-level boundary); components-legacy/booking/BookingPaymentForm.tsx:50,173-176 (card-declined rendered inline, never a modal)

### dm-rank-autocomplete-by-usage , Rank autocomplete suggestions by usage, capped and category-tagged
- Reasoning: A full usage-based ranking system already exists in this codebase: a search_popularity materialized view fed nightly by real click events, fused into the main salon-search ranker via a w_popularity weight. It just isn't joined into the 2-9 character typeahead RPC specifically, and that's the right call, not an oversight, for a marketplace this size: per-prefix click volume for a query fragment is close to zero for most terms across a handful of Swiss cities, so raw popularity would either be noise or entrench whichever service got the first lucky click, worse than the current text-relevance plus typo-tolerance plus personalization approach for a cold-start dataset. The list is already capped (5 services / 3 salons / 5 stylists). The category-badge-per-row half of the principle would actively regress Solen: the dropdown already declares category via section headers (Salons/Services/Stylists), so a repeated inline badge on every row would be exactly the redundant-tag pattern the copy-economy law bans.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md copy-economy rule 4: "A tag must add a decision-relevant fact not already on the row... remove the tag, if the user loses nothing, it was noise" (category is already conveyed by the SectionLabel groups)
- Code evidence: supabase/migrations/20260606204021_search_phase6_learning_loop.sql:34-58 (search_popularity materialized view from real click events) and 20260606204921_search_phase5_hybrid_vector_fusion.sql:107,113 (w_popularity fused into the results ranker) vs supabase/migrations/20260701150000_search_suggest_prefix_synonym.sql:60-63 (the actual /api/search/suggest RPC ranks by ts_rank + word_similarity + user_style_affinity, no popularity join, capped 5/3/5); app/[locale]/_components/search/SearchOverlay.tsx:643-659 (services grouped under one SectionLabel, no per-row badge)

### dm-match-notification-intrusiveness , Match notification surface intrusiveness to severity
- Reasoning: Solen already has all four tiers this principle asks for, each scoped to the trigger class it should be: Toast for a just-completed action (save confirmation, background mutation rollback), OfflineBanner for a degraded-but-not-blocking condition, Modal/WaitlistModal for a decision that must actually block progress, and the NotificationBell's red count badge for a passive there's-something-to-look-at signal nobody has to act on immediately. The locked z-index stack (toast above modal above sheet) formalizes these as genuinely different interruption weights, not stylistic variants of one alert. This principle reads as a description of the system that's already shipped rather than a gap.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:400-401 and LOCKFILE.md:497 (toast 600 / modal 510 / sheet 410, a deliberate interruption-order hierarchy); LOCKFILE.md:1260 (red count-badge convention on the notification bell)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Toast.tsx (transient, tones, 4s auto-dismiss); app/[locale]/_components/layout/OfflineBanner.tsx (dismissible-by-resolution bottom bar for connectivity); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Modal.tsx + components-legacy/booking/WaitlistModal.tsx (blocking, decision-required); app/[locale]/_components/layout/NotificationBell.tsx:47-51 (passive unread-count badge, unread === null renders nothing so it's never a fabricated dead control)

### dm-content-aware-skeletons , Content-aware skeleton shapes
- Reasoning: This principle is close to a direct restatement of a pattern table already written into SOURCE.md and already shipped in code: skeletons are composed from a shape-matched silhouette (photo aspect ratio, differently-sized text bars, pill chips, avatar circles) rather than one generic bars-and-rectangle block. SkeletonCard.tsx is essentially a worked example of exactly what this principle asks for. There is nothing to add here; if anything, this harvested principle is confirmation the codebase already got this one right.
- Law evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md §10.1 pattern table (~lines 694-701): salon card = photo+name-bar+meta-bar, stylist row = 72px circle + 3 stacked bars, review card = star row + 3 bars + avatar+name row, hero dropdown = icon-square + text-line
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/SkeletonCard.tsx:36-46 (aspect-square photo, h-4 w-75% title, h-3 w-50% subtitle, two rounded-full chip placeholders, mirroring the real SalonCard); components-legacy/ui/Skeleton.tsx:16-17 (variant="avatar" renders a w-10 h-10 rounded-full circle) vs variant="card"

### dm-column-grid-ratios-and-breakpoints , Layout proportions are column-grid ratios that change by breakpoint
- Reasoning: Solen already builds region sizes as column-span ratios that reflow when the breakpoint changes the total column count, exactly the mechanic described: dashboard KPI cards use lg:col-span-2 vs lg:col-span-1 (2:1 of a 3-wide row) that stacks to 1-col below lg, the salon photo gallery uses col-span-2 row-span-2 for the hero shot against 1-span thumbnails, and the search result grid steps grid-cols-2 to md:grid-cols-3 to lg:grid-cols-4. For a booking marketplace with a handful of recurring grid contexts (search results, bento feature cards, dashboard KPIs, photo galleries), this ratio-of-a-fixed-grid approach is the standard building block already in use, not a gap to fill.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:832-847 (Breakpoints table sm/md/lg/xl/2xl + Grid patterns: 'Search result grid: grid-cols-2 md:grid-cols-3 lg:grid-cols-4', 'Bento: grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 md:grid-cols-2')
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/dashboard/page.tsx:220,240 (lg:col-span-2 revenue card vs lg:col-span-1 siblings); app/[locale]/_components/salon/SalonHero.tsx:229 (col-span-2 row-span-2 hero photo); app/[locale]/_components/homepage/BentoBusiness.tsx:691,707 (md:col-span-2 bento cells)

### dm-split-form-steps-by-context , Split form steps on shared context boundaries, not equal field counts
- Reasoning: Both multi-step wizards Solen ships are already cut at real-world context boundaries, not arbitrary field counts. Booking is locked to Services+Staff / Zeit / Haare / Bezahlen, each step even getting its own icon (scissors / clock / brush / credit-card) specifically because 'the steps have real identity,' which is the same working-memory-chunking logic the principle argues for. Onboarding mirrors it: SalonProfile / Team / Services / Hours / Schedule / Payments / GoLive are separate files, one per real context, never a field-count split. On a mobile booking flow, where a user re-orients after every interruption, this is exactly where the pattern earns its keep, and it is already the law.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:1238 ('Distinct icon per node when the steps have real identity... booking = scissors (Service) -> clock (Zeit) -> brush (Haare) -> credit-card (Bezahlen)'); memory project_booking_flow_canonical.md ('booking flow LOCKED to mockup 20: services -> Staff -> Zeit(->Haare) -> Bezahlen')
- Code evidence: components-legacy/booking/{ServicesStaffStep.tsx,DateTimeStep.tsx,HairStep.tsx,PayConfirmStep.tsx}; components-legacy/onboarding/steps/{SalonProfileStep.tsx,TeamStep.tsx,PaymentsStep.tsx,GoLiveStep.tsx,OpeningHoursStep.tsx,ScheduleStep.tsx,ServicesStep.tsx}

### dm-white-space-three-jobs , White space has three jobs: Micro, Macro, Active
- Reasoning: RATIONALE.md already separates whitespace into the same three functional jobs under sourced, evidence-based names rather than the video's slogans: micro (Chaparro et al. 2004, legibility/padding), macro (Gestalt proximity, operationalized as the locked 32/12/16 section/card/group rhythm), and isolation-for-emphasis (Von Restorff / preattentive pop-out, domain 1) ,  visible in shipped code as BusinessTeaser's deliberately larger py-12/py-20 gap specifically to give the CTA transition breathing room. RATIONALE.md's stated purpose is replacing exactly this kind of unsourced video framing with cited mechanisms, so relabeling it Micro/Macro/Active would be a step backward for a system this developed, not new information.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:142 ('Macro whitespace signals structure and calm (premium read); micro whitespace drives legibility. Proximity math from domain 1 is the operative rule.', citing Chaparro et al. 2004, and explicitly rejecting the fabricated 'Lin 2004 +20 percent' claim); RATIONALE.md:53 (Gestalt proximity grounding for the 32/12/16 tiers); RATIONALE.md:58 and :701 (preattentive pop-out / Von Restorff isolation)
- Code evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md:379 ('BusinessTeaser top padding: py-12 md:py-20 (48/80px)... More breathing room because it's a transition out of the feed' ,  a shipped example of deliberate extra space isolating a CTA section for emphasis)

### dm-simultaneous-contrast , Simultaneous contrast: a color's perceived lightness depends on its surround
- Reasoning: Solen already treats 'judge a color only against its real background' as load-bearing law, not a swatch-book exercise: the RATIONALE.md contrast table measures the SAME token separately on white vs sunken vs photo and gets different verdicts (s-accent 4.58:1/AA-pass on white, 4.17:1/AA-FAIL on the sunken #F4F4F5 card bg), which is exactly the simultaneous-contrast effect the principle names. This matters concretely for a booking marketplace where blue is deliberately a sparse hyperlink signal (review counts, Mehr lesen) sitting on both white and sunken cards, so a link that reads fine in isolation can silently drop below the legal floor once it lands on a sunken card. The system already response to this: the styleguide page (rendered, in-context), not the token table, is named as the verification surface.
- Law evidence: RATIONALE.md ~line 120: 'Simultaneous contrast (T1): the same token reads differently on white vs sunken vs photo; evaluate pairs in their real context (this is why the styleguide page, not the token table, is the verification surface).' Same file's measured table: s-accent on white 4.58:1/Lc 71.1 (AA pass) vs s-accent on sunken 4.17:1/Lc 64.6 (FAILS AA for normal text).
- Code evidence: none checked beyond RATIONALE.md's own measured token table (which cites tailwind.config.js s-accent/s-ink-2 values); the practice is codified as doctrine rather than a single component file.

### dm-frosted-glass-build-order , Frosted glass build order: transparency, blur, border, shadow
- Reasoning: The exact four-property order the principle describes, background transparency then backdrop blur then border then shadow, is already the literal locked code recipe, extracted into one shared constant specifically so components stop re-deriving it ad hoc. It's scoped to the one case the physical reasoning actually applies to (a control sitting over an unpredictable photo: hero icon buttons, gallery overlays, SaveHeart) and explicitly banned on flat white/stone surfaces, which matches the principle's own claim that skipping blur (or applying the recipe where there's no photo underneath) reads as a flat tinted overlay, not glass.
- Law evidence: LOCKFILE.md:492 (Control elevation lock V3-D420: 'a control sitting OVER a photo, the FROST_GLASS recipe... on a flat white/s-bg-sunken surface a control casts NO shadow'); RATIONALE.md:716 ('frost is locked for over-photo controls; its mechanic is figure/ground preservation... blur + tint as a worst-case floor').
- Code evidence: lib/frost-glass.ts:16-22, the FROST_GLASS constant: background rgba(255,255,255,0.80) then backdropFilter blur(4px) then border 1px rgba(255,255,255,0.6) then boxShadow, in that exact order, comment-labeled 'the ONLY place white+shadow is allowed.'

### dm-semantic-color-tokens , Reference colors through purpose-named variables, never raw hex
- Reasoning: This is already the load-bearing law of the whole design system, not a gap. Every color in the app is required to be a named Tailwind token (s-accent, s-error, s-success, s-ink, etc.) mapped to hex once in tailwind.config.js; hardcoded hex in JSX/TSX is a hard-banned §0 rule, mechanically enforced by a PreToolUse hook so an agent cannot even land a raw hex literal. Re-theming Solen's accent, error, or success color is already a one-line tailwind.config.js edit, exactly the payoff this principle claims.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:20 section 0.6 "No hardcoded hex in JSX/TSX. Use the Tailwind tokens below. Only exceptions: the per-token literal definitions in tailwind.config.js itself..."
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js:131-178 (s-ink/s-success/s-warning/s-error token definitions, single source of truth); .claude/hooks/pre-edit-drift-gate.sh (blocks raw hex / arbitrary color values on edit, per its header comment)

### dm-illustration-over-void , Illustration over void in empty states
- Reasoning: Solen's locked EmptyState primitive already does exactly this: a full-clarity 32px Lucide icon (not a near-zero-opacity placeholder) sits inside a 64px sunken disc with a soft background halo, paired with a title and an optional action, and the richer profile-list variant (EmptyStateDiscovery) goes further with a real photo banner and a genuine salon rail rather than a blank void. RATIONALE.md's own G4 decision ("empty-state + illustration language... teach value, one action") and the "no dead ends" rule already mandate a visible icon plus a next action for every empty state. There's no void to fix here.
- Law evidence: _design-system/RATIONALE.md:820 "G4 Empty-state + illustration language (bridge from the personality-zone table; NN/g blank-slate: teach value, one action)"; RATIONALE.md:798 "No dead ends... every empty, error, and zero-results state offers a next action"
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/EmptyState.tsx:70-75 (Icon size={32} at full opacity in a bg-s-bg-sunken 64px disc with soft blur halo, not near-zero opacity); app/[locale]/_components/profile/EmptyStateDiscovery.tsx + _design-system/components/EmptyStateDiscovery.md (richer real-content empty state: banner photo, hint icon, salon rail)

### dm-card-network-first-digit-detection , First digit names the brand: instant card-network detection
- Reasoning: Solen never builds a raw card-number input to run digit-matching against in the first place: both live payment surfaces (checkout and tips) mount Stripe's prebuilt <PaymentElement>, which auto-detects the card network from the leading digits and swaps in the network logo inline natively, with zero custom code. Building a custom digit-range matcher would require reading raw PAN digits, which Stripe Elements deliberately keeps inside a cross-origin iframe specifically so the merchant's JS never touches them (PCI SAQ-A scope). Recreating this principle from scratch would mean regressing PCI posture to rebuild something Stripe already ships for free. For a Swiss booking marketplace processing real card payments, that trade is a strict downgrade, not a feature.
- Law evidence: none (architectural/PCI, not a taste-system rule)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/checkout/page.tsx:7-13 and app/[locale]/_components/tips/TipFlow.tsx:14-15,259-261 both use @stripe/react-stripe-js <Elements>/<PaymentElement>, the only two payment-collection surfaces in the app (grep for @stripe/react-stripe-js found no other importers)

### dm-proportional-star-fill , Proportional (fractional) star fill for average ratings
- Reasoning: The exact failure the principle names (rounding a continuous average through a discrete 5-icon scale, destroying resolution and biasing upward) cannot happen in Solen today because decimal averages never render as a discrete star row at all. Every salon/staff average rating uses RatingStars mode="compact": one star icon plus the literal decimal, e.g. "4.4", which is strictly more precise than a fractional-fill 5th star (reading a 44%-filled star accurately is harder than reading the digit "4"). The only place RatingStars mode="five" (which does Math.round) is ever invoked is on a single review's own rating, which is inherently a whole number the user picked 1-5, so rounding is a no-op there, not a bug. Card redesign C11 (owner-approved, logged in TASTE_LOG.md line 200) locked "rating = star + value only", and PSYCHOLOGY.md law 6 ("Stars never render bare") already mandates showing the exact score, not an approximated glyph. Adding fractional fill would contradict the locked single-star treatment for no gain in accuracy.
- Law evidence: _design-system/TASTE_LOG.md:200 (card redesign C11: "rating = star + value only"); _design-system/PSYCHOLOGY.md:30-31 (law 6, stars always paired with the exact decimal + count, never rendered bare); _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:74 (single s-star token, no fractional-fill spec)
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/RatingStars.tsx:190-198 (compact mode renders one star + value.toFixed(1), no rounding, used for every salon.average_rating call-site per grep of SalonHeader.tsx:96, SalonSidebar.tsx:120, SalonResultCard.tsx:296+); mode="five" (lines 170-188, Math.round(value)) is only called with review.rating (integer 1-5) at SalonReviews.tsx:197 and MarketplaceReviewsList.tsx:58, never with a decimal average

### dm-skeleton-over-spinner-prediction , Skeleton screens over spinners: show the incoming layout
- Reasoning: This is settled, locked law for the product, not a gap. Solen's design contract explicitly states loading states must be a shape-matching <Skeleton>, not a bare spinner, and the component registry locks both a generic Skeleton and a composite SalonCard-shaped SkeletonCard for grid loading. Nothing to add.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md design contract table, row "states": "loading = <Skeleton> (shape matches the final layout, NOT a bare spinner)"; _design-system/COMPONENT_REGISTRY.md:30-31 (Skeleton + SkeletonCard, both status "locked")
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Skeleton.tsx and app/[locale]/_components/primitives/SkeletonCard.tsx exist as canonical components with shimmer animation respecting prefers-reduced-motion; _design-system/components/Skeleton.md and SkeletonCard.md document them as the required pattern

### dm-ease-out-over-linear , Ease-out motion over linear motion
- Reasoning: Solen's entire motion system is already built on a locked, gate-enforced easing token set, and the primary entrance/transition easing (`glide`, cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1)) is a textbook ease-out curve: fast start, decelerating into the landing, used for the mandatory ENTER RECIPE on every new element, sheet opens, and TabPill swaps. None of the four locked easing tokens is linear; `thud` (ease-in, for press-down) is the only accelerating curve and it is used deliberately for the opposite physical feel (a finger pressing down gains speed into the surface), which is itself consistent with the principle's physical-realism reasoning rather than a violation of it.
- Law evidence: _design-system/LOCKFILE.md:582-588 (four core easing tokens: snap/spring/glide/thud, all cubic-bezier, none linear) and :592-594 (Fresha-measured motion patterns all use thud/glide, never linear); _design-system/MOTION.md:16-40 (ENTER RECIPE locks glide at 420ms for every new-element entrance, gate-enforced by motion-recipe-gate.py)
- Code evidence: tailwind.config.js and the shared enter-motion module referenced in MOTION.md:33-34 consume these four tokens; no linear timing function is defined in the design tokens for UI transitions

### dm-modal-exit-scale-with-fade , Modal exit should scale down with the fade
- Reasoning: Solen's Modal primitive already does exactly this: opacity and scale animate together on exit, and the doc explicitly frames modals as functional not playful (no spring/bounce, ease-snap only), which matches the 'reads as premium, not cheap' reasoning in the claim without adding gratuitous bounce. For a booking marketplace where a modal usually gates a real decision (cancel booking, confirm), a calm scale+fade exit (not a spring) is the correct restrained execution, already shipped, not a gap.
- Law evidence: Modal.md:74-76 ('Entry/exit... scale-[0.95]... Reduced motion: scale collapses to 100... Functional (no bounce/spring) per the file comment: modals are functional, not playful'); MOTION.md ENTER RECIPE section establishes the house rule that entrances/exits combine multiple properties, not opacity alone.
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Modal.tsx:38-41 (`transition-[opacity,transform] duration-[250ms] ease-snap`, `data-[entering]:opacity-0 data-[entering]:scale-[0.95]`, `data-[exiting]:opacity-0 data-[exiting]:scale-[0.95] data-[exiting]:duration-150`); Modal.tsx:29-30 comment 'ease-snap for both entry and exit... No spring/bounce'.

### dm-hover-scale-shadow-glow , Hover feedback: scale + shadow + glow together
- Reasoning: The core claim, that a static-looking control needs a coordinated hover cue to prove it's live, is already law and already shipped app-wide as translateY lift + shadow-elevation bump (+ a modest 1-2% scale on the dominant SalonCard), not the 5% scale + glow combo in the example. The 'glow' half of the claim actively conflicts with CONTROL_ELEVATION's restraint doctrine: shadow is deliberately rationed to two places (the one ink primary, glass-over-photo) specifically to avoid a 'grey haze' / 'fourth visual dialect', and a purple-gradient glow button would also break the ink-only CTA rule. So the useful half of this principle is already shipped in a more restrained form; the glow half should not be added on top.
- Law evidence: _design-system/SOURCE.md:513-524 (§6.4 state matrix: Card/CTA/Tile hover = translateY + shadow-elevation bump over ease-glide, distinct from active-press scale); _design-system/CONTROL_ELEVATION.md 'Shadow lives in exactly two places... Zero box-shadow on any control resting on white or s-bg-sunken' and the philosophy line 'Elevation is earned by the background, not the button.'
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/homepage/SalonCard.tsx:403-404 (`group-hover:-translate-y-[3px] group-hover:scale-[1.015]` + `group-hover:shadow-elevation-3`); app/[locale]/_components/homepage/HeartButton.tsx:192 (`group-hover:scale-110`); app/[locale]/_components/layout/Header.tsx:742 (`hover:shadow-[...]` on the ink CTA). No `glow`/halo box-shadow pattern exists anywhere in app/components (grep clean).

### dm-immediate-feedback-closes-loop , Immediate feedback closes the action-response loop
- Reasoning: The underlying behavioral claim (tight temporal contiguity between action and reaction, then a distinct resolution signal) is already the system's default, just implemented as instant active:scale press-down plus optimistic UI and a completion signal (SuccessMark, toast, or the optimistic state itself), not a Material ripple. A booking marketplace has a small number of high-stakes 'silence = anxiety' moments (submit payment, confirm booking, join a walk-in queue) and those are already the ones wired with the celebratory SuccessMark specifically to close the loop; adding a ripple would import Material's own dialect into a system that has deliberately rejected Material-flavored affordances elsewhere (e.g. the middle-dot/pipe separator was rejected for reading as 'Material/web form-field').
- Law evidence: _design-system/components/LoadingStates.md:189-196 ('Pattern: assume success... Immediately: local state flips... Animation plays... On failure: ROLLBACK... Show toast') and :197-204 (confirmation-toast pattern for higher-stakes mutations); _design-system/MOTION.md SuccessMark section ('Celebratory moments on emotional peaks... the peak is booking-confirmed... also walk-in-joined, review-posted, package/gift bought, payment settled').
- Code evidence: SOURCE.md:513-524 press-state table gives every interactive control an immediate `active:scale` reaction; app/[locale]/_components/primitives/SuccessMark.tsx is the reusable completion-confirmation component reused across booking/walk-in/payment peaks. No `ripple` pattern exists in the codebase (grep clean across _design-system + app).

### dm-spinner-sub-3-seconds-only , Spinners: contained sub-3-second actions only
- Reasoning: This is verbatim house law already, not a gap: skeletons are mandated for any surface-level load, and bare spinners are restricted to inside a button during a short mutation, matching the claim's reasoning almost exactly (a stalled vs working spinner look identical, so a whole-page spinner gives no sense of progress or health). Nothing to add.
- Law evidence: _design-system/components/LoadingStates.md:24-31 (Pattern 1, mandates skeletons matching final layout for any first-render data fetch) and :292 ('Don't use spinners as the only loading affordance. Skeleton first; spinner only inside buttons.') and :72-75 (anti-pattern: 'Spinner-only loading (a centered circle on white). Communicates wait but doesn't pre-render layout').
- Code evidence: _design-system/components/LoadingStates.md:208-218 (Pattern 4 code example: `<button disabled={isPending}>{isPending ? <Spinner size={14} /> : <Heart />}</button>`, spinner scoped to the trigger control only) and :220-223 anti-pattern ('Spinning the WHOLE page on a single-item mutation. Spin the trigger only').

### dm-skeleton-known-shape-past-300ms , Skeletons only for known shapes, and only past ~300ms
- Reasoning: Both halves of this claim are already codified law for Solen: skeletons must mirror the PREDICTED final layout (not a generic shimmer block), and near-instant loads should show nothing rather than a flashing skeleton. This is the right call for a booking marketplace where most first-paint data (salon cards, service lists, calendar slots) has a fixed, known card footprint, so content-shaped skeletons cost nothing to build and pay off in perceived speed, while flashing a skeleton for a 50ms Supabase read would read as jank, not polish.
- Law evidence: _design-system/components/LoadingStates.md:24-76 (Pattern 1: skeleton must match the future layout exactly, 'no shift'; anti-pattern lists spinner-only precisely because it doesn't pre-render the known layout) and lines 208-216 + 227-243 (Pattern 4/5: mutations under ~300ms and refetches under ~500ms show NO loading indicator at all, 'let it complete silently', matching the harvested principle's 300ms floor almost exactly); _design-system/MOTION.md:96 ('Anything loading -> skeleton SHIMMER, content-shaped ... spinners only INSIDE buttons')
- Code evidence: app/globals.css .skeleton-shimmer / animate-shimmer keyframe used content-shaped across feeds; no code-level delay guard (e.g. a useDelayedLoading hook) was found gating skeleton mount behind a real 300ms timer, so the 300ms-threshold half of this principle is currently LAW, not yet enforced in code -- a legitimate small gap, but the principle itself is already the house rule, not a net-new idea

### dm-exit-faster-than-entrance , Exit should run faster than entrance
- Reasoning: This is not a gap, it's how every overlay primitive in the app is already built, and the ratios land almost exactly on the claimed 30-40% reduction. Modal.tsx's own code comment states the reasoning nearly verbatim: 'Motion: ease-snap for both entry (200/250ms) and exit (150ms). No spring/bounce, modals are functional, not playful.' Toast.tsx encodes the identical asymmetry with an explicit comment citing SOURCE.md's motion vocabulary. For a booking marketplace where sheets/modals/toasts fire constantly (filters, confirmations, cancellations), a snappy dismissal that doesn't make the user wait to get back to the task is the correct default, and the team already arrived at it independently three separate times.
- Law evidence: SOURCE.md motion vocabulary referenced in Toast.tsx comments (SOURCE.md §6.1/§6.2); LOCKFILE.md:594 documents sheet close timing under §3.5
- Code evidence: app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Modal.tsx:39-41 (entry duration-[250ms], data-[exiting]:duration-150, a 40% reduction, exactly inside the claimed 30-40% band); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Sheet.tsx:43-45 (surface entry 600ms / exit 200ms) and :178-180 (backdrop entry 300ms / exit 200ms); app/[locale]/_components/primitives/Toast.tsx:263-264,325-328 (entry 200-350ms / exit 150ms, comment explicitly names the enter/exit split)

### dm-determinate-progress-only-when-computable , Determinate progress bar only where the percentage is genuinely computable
- Reasoning: This is a direct restatement of Solen's single most-repeated law, the taste-rule-1 fabrication ban, generalized from ratings/counts/availability text to any rendered number including a progress percentage. Nothing about a booking marketplace changes the reasoning: a user watching a percentage climb reads it as a factual claim the same way they'd read a review count, and a fabricated one erodes trust exactly like a fake 'Frei in 15 Min' would. Worth flagging separately as a real, live violation rather than a reason to add anything: components-legacy/ui/ImageUpload.tsx assigns hand-picked percentages (10/30/50/80/90/95/100) to pipeline milestones of a Supabase Storage upload that exposes no real byte-progress callback in this call path, so the number on screen is not actually derived from bytes-transferred, it is a fabricated stand-in dressed as a real percentage. That is a bug against the EXISTING law, not evidence the principle needs adding.
- Law evidence: CLAUDE.md taste rule 1: 'No fabricated data. Never render a number / status not wired to a live source ... A fake value is worse than a dot, it's a lie the user trusts.' (also restated at CLAUDE.md:134 for mockups)
- Code evidence: components-legacy/ui/ImageUpload.tsx:90-128 (setProgress(10)/(30)/(50)/(90)/(80)/(95)/(100) assigned at pipeline milestones, not derived from actual bytes-uploaded/total-bytes; the Supabase .storage.upload() call at line 116-118 is awaited with no progress callback wired, so every intermediate percentage shown to the salon owner during a gallery-photo upload is invented)

### dm-drag-release-snap-vs-free , Drag release snaps on structured layouts, stays free on open canvases
- Reasoning: Every drag surface Solen actually has is discrete/structured, and every one of them already snaps on release.
